Templates for a better day (lrcpwa 08:00, sosagent 08:30, per-family merges, backup, job watcher) are in `deploy/mac/`; nothing is installed. Details: `docs/OPERATIONS.md`.

## Rules for working on the board

- **One writer at a time, and it is enforced.** `write_artifact` refuses unless the process holds `logs/.board.lock`. Run any board-writing script with `scripts/with_board_lock.sh <name> -- uv run python scripts/<x>.py`. Check the holder before starting: `cat logs/.board.lock/pid` (pid, owner, start, heartbeat, max runtime, token). A job that finds it held skips (exit 0) and logs a `skipped_lock` event.
- **Load through `web_artifact.load_board()`** (or `read_board_records`), or the vision, comps and CAMA sidecars are wiped. `load_board` now counts every row it drops and fails above 0.1%.
- **Never load the board in more than one process at a time.** One load is about 2.8 GB; three concurrent loads reached about 20 GB and forced a restart. The Mac is an 8 GB Air with 6 to 7 GB of swap in use under a single job.
- **The manifest.** After a write, `docs/board.manifest.json` names every payload file. If a load raises `BoardIntegrityError`, the set is torn or the manifest is stale: `scripts/board_manifest.py --verify`, then restore (`docs/RESTORE.md`) or, once you have checked the files, `--rebuild`.
- **Stage payloads with `board_payload_add`** (`scripts/board_payload.sh`) so the manifest travels with the board.
- **Do not push inside the lock**, and do not run `git add` on the payload by hand without the manifest.
- **A bad board:** `scripts/restore_board.sh <commit>` (`--list` first). One commit, every payload file. `docs/RESTORE.md`.
- **Compliance line** (owner rule, 2026-09-20): a `robots.txt` Disallow is not a wall; a CAPTCHA, a login, a Cloudflare challenge and click-through terms are. See `CLAUDE.md`.

## What was done on 2026-09-21

Audit: `docs/AUDIT_2026-09-21.md` (findings register, section 11) and `docs/audit_operations_2026-09-21.md`. Fixes: `docs/ops_fixes_2026-09-21.md` (operations), `docs/scoring_fixes_2026-09-21.md` (scorer), `docs/data_quality_fixes_2026-09-21.md` (data). The operations work, in one table:

| Area | What exists now |
|---|---|
| Lock | Enforced in `write_artifact`; start time, heartbeat, max runtime, token, registered children; stale by max runtime; memory gate (default `warn`) |
| Board integrity | Manifest written last and verified on load; changed-since-load check; dropped rows counted and capped |
| Health | `health_as_of`, `health_age_hours`, `health_stale` (nulls `source_status` and `errors` past 48 h); `source_last_success` per source |
| Failures | `main.py` exits 3 on a failed board write and skips the export and the email; exit 6 on a scorer failure; `run_local.sh` aborts on both |
| Job log and watcher | `logs/job_events.jsonl`, `scripts/job_watch.py`, `scripts/check_payload_size.py` |
| Pushes | `scripts/publish_helper.sh`, `src/foreclosure_scraper/publish.py`: commit in the lock, push outside it with a timeout and retries |
| Refresh | Per-family merge jobs `scripts/run_family_{qpaybill,nc_tax,sc_tax,nc_ecourts}.sh` |
| Backup | `scripts/backup_local_state.sh`, `scripts/restore_board.sh`, `docs/RESTORE.md` |
| Scope and window | Flip leads only in the 18 counties (coastal carve-outs no longer shelter a flip); the sold-pool partition waits out the upset window; `auction_date` is kept apart from the docket date |

Four files a running job owns are shipped as patches, not applied: `run_daily_vision.sh`, `daily_api_refresh.py`, `patch_vision_gemini.py`, and optionally `enrichment_vision.py` and `pyproject.toml` (`docs/ops_fixes_2026-09-21.md`, section 16).

**Tests.** I ran targeted files only, never the whole suite. The last whole-suite count in this file (3201 passed, 2 failed) is from 8/20 and was not re-run; the audit noted CI runs only on pull requests. **That claim was never actually verified against `.github/workflows/` and was wrong on both halves — see the 2026-09-30 correction below.**

9. **DONE (2026-09-30). The `geo_imprecise` ARV-confidence fix is now live on the published board**, via a TARGETED recompute instead of the full-board `recompute_valuation.py` (which still cannot run — `load_board()` self-refuses at 1200 MB and was separately measured at an 11+ GB "Physical footprint" even with the streaming fix; see item 5). `scripts/recompute_geo_imprecise_confidence.py` streams the board with the lazy-detail sidecar merged (`web_artifact._iter_board_records`, never a full `list[Listing]`), recomputes `valuation/calc.compute()` + `valuation/grading.grade()` (the real functions, unmodified) for just the 10,575 rows tagged `census_geocode` that still carried the stale `geo_imprecise_comps` flag, and patches only those via `patch_existing_rows()`. Verified safe because calc/grade are pure per-row functions (comps live on `raw.comps`, populated by an earlier pass, never looked up from other rows) — confirmed with a hard safety gate (`arv_expected`/`rehab_expected` must come out byte-identical to the previous run, else the row is excluded rather than guessed at; 5 rows tripped this and were left alone). Result: 10,506 rows patched, 5,135 fully clean (no ARV flag left), 626 confidence upgrades (mostly MEDIUM→HIGH), 186 deal verdicts restored. `scripts/board_manifest.py --verify` passes (219,530 records, 241/241 checks).
   **Second-order bug found and fixed the same session**: `patch_existing_rows()` matches rows by `Listing.dedupe_key()` and applies one patch to every row sharing a key — correct for an identity fact like lat/lng, wrong for valuation, which is NOT identity-invariant. 93 of the 10,511 recomputed rows shared a `dedupe_key()` with a sibling row whose `property_kind` genuinely disagrees (most often one source calling a parcel `single_family`, another calling the same `parcel_id` `land` — a pre-existing board dedupe-key collision, 1,599 such groups exist board-wide, 557 with disagreeing property_kind/opening_bid). The patch briefly published the `single_family` row's valuation onto its `land` sibling too. Fixed by `scripts/repair_dedupe_key_collision.py`, which bypasses `patch_existing_rows()`'s key-matching entirely — it streams the board and the pre-corruption backup in lockstep BY POSITION (row count/order provably unchanged) and recomputes each of the 190 affected rows from its OWN data individually. All 190 passed the same identical-dollar safety gate on the first try (0 needed a backup revert). **Lesson for any future patch script**: `patch_existing_rows()` is only safe for fields that are true of the IDENTITY, not fields that depend on the specific record's own data (opening_bid, property_kind, comps) — check for dedupe_key() collisions with disagreeing source data before trusting a keyed patch at scale.

11. **DONE (2026-09-30). Fixed `Listing.merge()` resurrecting withheld money on a contradicted ARV, and backfilled the 99 already-published rows it hit.** `board_selfcheck.py` found 97 rows publishing `max_bid_70`, 43 `wholesale_mao`, and 100 `raw['equity']` alongside an `arv_flags` entry in `valuation.grading.ARV_FLAGS_CONTRADICTED` — exactly the bug commit `48187218` ("Stop publishing money computed from another property's record") was meant to close, back from the dead. Root cause: `models._deep_merge_dict` (used by `Listing.merge()`, the one seam `dedupe()`'s 3 call sites and `merge_duplicate_rows()` both fold duplicate/multi-source copies of a lead through) unions dict KEYS — it can overwrite or add a key either side names, but a key ABSENT from one side (because `calc.to_dict()`/`withhold_equity()` DROP a gated field's key rather than writing `None`) is invisible to it, so it can never delete one. Merging an older, un-gated copy of a lead with a freshly re-graded copy of the SAME lead therefore kept the stale money. Confirmed by direct recompute (fresh `calc.compute()`+`grade()` on the live row both correctly null the fields — the forward path was never broken) and by reproducing the exact bug in a synthetic merge. Fixed in `models.py`: `Listing.merge()` now re-runs `grading.gate_calc_dict()` + `enrichment_equity.withhold_equity()` on the merged raw dict (local imports break the `models`↔`grading`↔`enrichment_equity` cycle — both those modules import `Listing` for type hints; safe because they only execute at merge()-CALL time). `scripts/backfill_contradicted_arv_gate.py` landed the fix on the board: 99/107 affected rows patched (collision-safe two-pass, same pattern as `backfill_tax_owed_amount_owed.py`), 8 dropped as `dedupe_key()` collisions — a PRE-EXISTING un-merged-duplicate condition (the board's separate, still-open "no duplicate identifiable properties" invariant, 983 violations) — rather than guessed at. `board_selfcheck.py`: `max_bid_70` 97→0, `wholesale_mao` 43→0, `equity` 100→9 (the collision holdouts). Two large ARV swings the same `board_selfcheck` run flagged in MOVEMENT (5555 Groves Road 16,700→2,050,900; a Madison Co. parcel 83,400→1,550,300) were checked and are NOT this bug — both already correctly gated/captioned per their own tier (contradicted-but-money-absent, and WEAK-tier-keeps-dollars-by-design respectively) — a separate, unexplained valuation-input change, noted but not chased further. Full suite: 6141 passed, 59 skipped, 0 failed. Commits: `a40f3689` (code), `8f88a754` (board data).

13. **DONE (2026-09-30). Re-verified the SCDOT token wall live — still genuinely walled, docs were accurate, not stale.** Investigation-only session (no board load, no scraper run, per that day's memory-safety constraint). Read every SCDOT reference in `docs/` and `src/foreclosure_scraper/` (the circuit-breaker in `enrichment_arcgis.py`, its wiring into `enrichment_parcel_from_geo._arc_query`/`enrichment_owner_mailing`/`enrichment_lis_pendens_resolver`/`parcel_inventory`, and `tests/test_scdot_breaker.py`), then sent 6 small, hard-timeout-wrapped direct HTTP probes (`asyncio.wait_for`, no retries) straight at the live endpoint — all answered in under 0.6s, no hang. Confirmed: `smpesri.scdot.org/arcgis/rest/services/GISMapping/SC_Parcels/MapServer` (SC's only statewide parcel owner/situs resolver, and separately the owner-MAILING-address fallback for all 46 SC counties via `enrichment_owner_mailing.SCDOT_SC_BASE`) still returns HTTP 200 + `{"error":{"code":499,"message":"Token Required"}}` on the service root and on every layer queried (Anderson layer 4, Greenville layer 23) — unchanged since 2026-08-12, re-confirmed 2026-09-28 (`80fca14d`). Precise nature of the wall (new this session): it's a deliberate, service-level ArcGIS auth restriction backed by SCDOT's own ArcGIS Enterprise Portal (`isTokenBasedSecurity: true`, `tokenServicesUrl` → `smpesri.scdot.org/portal/...generateToken`, an agency-internal portal with no public self-service sign-up found) — not a rate limit, IP block, or outage. It's selective, not host-wide: the same server's unauthenticated catalog lists other services fine (`SC_Address_Locator`, `EGIS_Imagery`, `Bridges`, `SC_Contours`), and `SC_Parcels` doesn't even appear in the anonymous `GISMapping` folder listing, consistent with a deliberately configured restriction rather than a bug. Per the compliance line, no attempt was made to obtain a token or sign up. **Existing mitigation (built 2026-08-12, still the active path) was NOT touched or re-enabled**: the per-host circuit breaker trips on the first token error and short-circuits the rest of that process's run, and `SC_GIS` county-native replacements already cover 7 of 11 in-scope counties (Spartanburg, Laurens, Pickens, Colleton, Beaufort, Georgetown, Charleston); Cherokee, Union, Oconee, Anderson remain gapped for county-specific reasons unrelated to SCDOT re-opening (see `docs/walls_register.md`, which got a 2026-09-30 re-verification note added, same place). One narrow, safe fix made: `scripts/reprobe_walls.py`'s `scdot_parcels` entry had been probing an unrelated, already-dead AGOL FeatureServer URL that no scraper ever calls (404s with a different "Invalid URL" error) — cosmetic (`docs/wall_status.json` gates nothing in production) but it meant that monitor could never detect a real SCDOT re-open; corrected to the real production URL. **Bottom line: nothing changed, nothing was re-enabled — SCDOT is confirmed, precisely, still walled**, and SC's 8-18% contact-coverage constraint (`project_county_coverage_matrix` memory) is not explained by a stale doc here.

15. **URGENT, PARTIALLY FIXED (2026-09-30). The board outgrew its own load ceiling — two real scheduled jobs were silently failing every run since this morning.** The board is now 2,645-2,651 MB, over double `BOARD_LOAD_MAX_SOURCE_MB=1200`. `launchctl list` showed `com.highway.foreclosure.sosagent` and `com.highway.foreclosure.dailyvision` both at exit code 1; today's logs (`logs/sos_agent_refresh.log` 14:00, `logs/daily-vision-20260930T093004.log` 09:30) show `BoardLoadTooLarge` tracebacks from `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py`'s `load_board()` call and `scripts/patch_vision_gemini.py`'s `read_board_records()` call — both scripts have been no-oping since the board crossed the ceiling. **Fixed (commit `d80da378`):** both migrated onto the safe streaming/patch APIs already proven earlier today for `resolver_backfill_parcel.py`/`resolver_backfill_geocode.py`. `sos_agent_refresh.py` now does two `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` passes (collect resolved profiles, then propagation targets) + one `patch_existing_rows()` call — its cross-lead propagation is a hash-join, not board-relative math, so it streams cleanly. `patch_vision_gemini.py` now uses `_iter_board_records()` (sidecar merged, `vision` is a lazy-detail key) and scopes its calc/grade recompute to only the rows vision actually touched this run (object-identity check on `raw["vision"]`), not the whole board — a real efficiency fix too, since the old code recomputed calc/grade on all ~217K rows every run for a ~4,500-row vision survivor set. `VISION_INCLUDE_NO_PHOTO=1` is refused cleanly (needs the full ~190K population, genuinely can't stream) rather than silently truncated. Tested against synthetic fixtures only — a live run wasn't safe given concurrent session load at the time (`tests/test_sos_agent_refresh_patch_integration.py`, `tests/test_patch_vision_gemini_patch_integration.py`, 8 tests, plus regression on the pre-existing suites). **`scripts/daily_api_refresh.py` is NOT fixed and remains broken** — its daily merge does genuine whole-board `dedupe()` (cross-bucket fuzzy address matching) and `distress_score.score_board()` (board-wide parcel grouping + percentile HOT/WARM/COLD tiering), plus drops stale REO rows after consecutive misses. No existing safe primitive supports row deletion or requires the full `list[Listing]` at once the way these two functions do — this needs genuine new primitives, not a plumbing migration, and forcing one would mean either reintroducing `load_board()`/`write_artifact()` or silently breaking dedupe/rescore/deletion correctness. **This script will keep failing on every 09:30 run until someone builds a real solution** (a streaming/bounded dedupe, or a deliberately-supervised full-board pass, or a redesign of what needs whole-board state) — flagging clearly rather than leaving it quietly broken. Separately, **all Anderson/Gaston/Greenville fixes landed earlier today (sqft, situs/value, false distress signal) still need a board-level backfill** once a safe window opens — that queue is: Gaston NC sqft/value, Anderson SC situs/value (owner_mailing path), ~2,600+ Greenville SC rows carrying the fabricated distress stack-of-2 from item 14 above.

21. **DISCOVERY ONLY (2026-10-01) — systematic sweep for unsafe `load_board()`/`read_board_records()`/`read_board_json()`/`write_artifact()` call sites, requested after four of these (`resolver_backfill_parcel.py`, `resolver_backfill_geocode.py`, `sos_agent_refresh.py`, `patch_vision_gemini.py`) were found and fixed one at a time over two days. Nothing in this item was fixed — grep + `launchctl`/log reading only.** Methodology: grepped all four function names across `scripts/*.py` (171 files matched) and the relevant `src/foreclosure_scraper/` modules (matches there are the library definitions + already-safe internal use — not counted), cross-checked every match against `~/Library/LaunchAgents/*.plist` (`launchctl list` confirms only 5 are loaded: `dailyvision`, `lrcpwa`, `sosagent`, `parcelcache`, `weekly`; `com.highway.foreclosure.dailycourt.plist` is present but renamed `.disabled-2026-09-20`, confirmed not loaded; `crontab -l` is empty — launchd is the only scheduler), traced the 5 wrapper shell scripts + 3 Desktop `.app`s to the actual Python entry points, and — because a plain 4-name grep has a blind spot for indirection — searched for shared helpers that wrap the same calls. One was found: `scripts/_dq_common.py`'s `run_apply()`, used by 10 more scripts invisible to the direct grep. Total distinct scripts carrying a real (non-comment, non-already-fixed) unsafe call site: **~159** (171 raw matches, minus 17 confirmed false-positives/already-safe-by-design minus the 2 already-fixed, plus 7 found only via the `_dq_common` indirection = 159). They sort into four buckets:

    **TIER 0 — one shared root cause, fixes 10 sites at once (NEW, not previously catalogued).** `scripts/_dq_common.py:113` (`rows = load_board(REPO / "docs")`) and `:121` (`write_artifact(rows, {...}, docs_dir=REPO / "docs")`), inside `run_apply()` — the shared `--apply` skeleton every one of these ten `fix_*`/`repair_*`/`resolve_*`/`backfill_*` data-quality scripts imports instead of writing their own board I/O: `backfill_missing_county.py:397`, `map_account_ids_to_parcels.py:164`, `fix_county_mc_mac_casing.py` (`--apply` only; default run is a read-only streaming dry-run), `fill_address_from_parcel.py:480`, `undo_resolver_middle_conflicts.py:242`, `quarantine_flip_leaks.py:195`, `run_parcel_from_geo.py:52` (also has its own direct `load_board()` at line 40), `resolve_parcel_from_address.py:993`, `promote_ptscloud_block.py:264`, `repair_parcel_from_address.py:714`. Two of these ten were touched **yesterday** (`repair_parcel_from_address.py`, `resolve_parcel_from_address.py`, both 2026-09-30) and six more on 2026-09-21 — this is an actively-maintained family, not dead code, and a single fix to `_dq_common.run_apply()` clears all ten `--apply` paths at once rather than needing ten separate migrations.

    **TIER 1 — scheduled via launchd, ranked by urgency, with TODAY's actual log output, not just static analysis:**
    1. **`scripts/lrcpwa_refresh.py` — NEW, not previously known, and confirmed actively broken right now.** Scheduled daily at noon (`com.highway.foreclosure.lrcpwa` → `lrcpwa_refresh.sh` → this script). Unsafe calls: `load_board(DOCS)` line 50, `write_artifact(listings, {...}, docs_dir=DOCS)` line 73. Evidence this is not theoretical: `logs/job_events.jsonl` shows it actually reaching `load_board()` on 2026-09-23 and 2026-09-27 (other days it was skipped by the lock) and both times **timing out at rc=124** after churning enormous swap — 2026-09-23: `rss_peak_mb=1471`, `swap_out_mb=311152.2`; 2026-09-27: `rss_peak_mb=2790`, `swap_out_mb=54926.1`. As of today (2026-10-01) the board has grown past even that: `logs/lrcpwa_refresh.log`'s latest entry (2026-09-30) is a clean `BoardLoadTooLarge: board source is 2646 MB, over the 1200 MB ceiling` — the same self-refusal that stopped `sos_agent_refresh.py`/`patch_vision_gemini.py` before their 2026-09-30 fix. This script was missed by every prior one-at-a-time discovery.
    2. **`scripts/daily_api_refresh.py`** — already known (found, not fixed; needs new whole-board dedupe/score-without-full-materialization primitives, see item 15). Scheduled daily 9:30 (`dailyvision`). Confirmed still failing as of this morning: `logs/daily-vision-20261001T093005.log`, `BoardLoadTooLarge: board source is 2645 MB`. Call site: `write_artifact(...)` line 336.
    3. **`scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py`** and **`scripts/patch_vision_gemini.py`** — already fixed 2026-09-30 (board_stream/patch_existing_rows). Confirmed no remaining direct `load_board()`/`write_artifact()` calls (grep matches left are explanatory comments only). **However: today's run of `patch_vision_gemini.py` (`logs/daily-vision-20261001T093005.log`) failed anyway** — not on the old `load_board()` ceiling, but on a *different, newer* one: `patch_existing_rows refused ... board source is 2645 MB, over the 2300 MB ceiling`. The board has grown past the streaming-patch ceiling too, one day after the fix landed. This isn't a new unsafe call site (the script is doing the right thing and self-refusing loudly, by design), but it means the memory-safety work from 2026-09-30 is already being outrun by board growth — worth the next session's attention even though it's outside this item's grep-for-unsafe-calls scope.
    4. **`scripts/refresh_parcel_cache.py`** (parcelcache, weekly Sunday 4am) — confirmed clean: it never touches the board at all (reads/writes only `data/parcel_cache` SQLite), no match in any of the four greps.

    **TIER 1.5 — triggered routinely by the Tue/Fri popup and the "Ingest Saved Court Pages" Desktop app (already known, not fixed):** `scripts/ingest_publicindex_files.py` (`load_board(DOCS)` line 50, `write_artifact(...)` line 101) and `scripts/ingest_fresh_court_leads.py` (`load_board(DOCS)` line 182, `write_artifact(merged, summary, docs_dir=DOCS)` line 250), both invoked by `scripts/ingest_saved.sh`, which `scripts/prompt_run.sh`'s Step 1 (fires Tue/Fri 9:30, or at next wake) and the Desktop "Ingest Saved Court Pages.app" both call directly.

Nothing above was fixed this session.

22. **DONE (2026-10-01) — migrated `scripts/_dq_common.py`'s `run_apply()` (item 21's Tier 0 shared helper) off `load_board()`/`write_artifact()`.** First correction to item 21's own count: `run_apply(` is actually called by **9** scripts, not 10 — `scripts/run_parcel_from_geo.py` only imports `require_raw_keep` from `_dq_common`; it has its own separate, still-unmigrated `load_board()`/`write_artifact()` pair (lines 40/52), unaffected by this fix (confirmed by `grep -rln "run_apply(" scripts/*.py`).

    **How the migration works.** `run_apply()`'s own contract (`apply_fn(rows, dry_run=False)` mutates a `list[Listing]` in place, FILL-ONLY) is unchanged for every caller — no `apply_rows()` function in any of the 9 scripts was touched. What changed is how `rows` is built and how the result is written: `_light_rows()` builds the working set from `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` + `Listing.model_validate()` per row (real validation/enums, same as `load_board()`, just without merging the lazy-detail sidecar — none of these 9 scripts read/write a `vision`/`comps`/`cama`/`foreclosure_sold_comps`/`rent_comps` key, confirmed by grep) instead of `load_board()`'s full sidecar-merged materialization. A pre-mutation snapshot (`_snapshot_rows()`: `dedupe_key()`, a JSON-mode dump of every non-`raw` field, and a **deep** copy of `raw` — a shallow copy would miss `map_account_ids_to_parcels.py`'s in-place `raw["qpaybill_roll"][...] = ...` mutation) is diffed against each row's post-`apply_fn` state (`_diff_to_patches()`/`_diff_raw()`) to build a sparse `patches` dict of only the rows actually changed, landed in one `web_artifact.patch_existing_rows()` call instead of a whole-board `write_artifact()`. `_raise_if_board_too_large_to_patch()` (the ceiling `patch_existing_rows()` itself re-checks) runs BEFORE the full-list read, not after, so an oversized board fails loud immediately rather than mid-build.

    **7 of 9 callers transparently fixed**, confirmed via code reading (not assumed) that each only SETS/fills fields and raw sub-keys, never deletes one: `backfill_missing_county.py`, `fix_county_mc_mac_casing.py`, `fill_address_from_parcel.py`, `map_account_ids_to_parcels.py`, `promote_ptscloud_block.py`, `resolve_parcel_from_address.py`, and `quarantine_flip_leaks.py` (its self-correcting "stamp cleared" removal of `raw['scope']` is the one raw-key deletion among these 7 — verified safe: `distress_score.flip_outside_footprint()` is the ONLY consumer, reading `r.get("scope") == OUT_OF_FOOTPRINT`, so representing the removal as an explicit `raw['scope']=None` — `patch_existing_rows()` can only MERGE via `dict.update()`, never delete a key — is equivalent; `_RAW_DELETE_SAFE_AS_NONE` in `_dq_common.py` is this verified allowlist).

    **2 of 9 excluded — `undo_resolver_middle_conflicts.py` and `repair_parcel_from_address.py`** — both blank `raw['owner_mailing']` (among other keys) as core "undo a proven-wrong resolution" behavior, and `src/foreclosure_scraper/mailing_shape.py:41` reads it via a literal `"owner_mailing" in raw` presence test, which an explicit `None` would NOT satisfy the same way an absent key does (confirmed; `scripts/backfill_buncombe_dam_situs.py:389` has the same presence test). Rather than silently emulating the deletion wrong, `run_apply()`'s `_diff_raw()` raises a loud, specific `RuntimeError` naming the script and the unsafe key if this is ever hit — both scripts still refuse exactly like before (previously `BoardLoadTooLarge`, now this), just with a clearer reason; neither is a functional regression since neither could write to the current oversized board either way. They need their own dedicated `board_stream`/`patch_existing_rows` migration (same shape as `sos_agent_refresh.py`'s), not this shared helper.

    **Tests.** All 176 pre-existing `apply_rows()`-level unit tests across the 9 scripts (`test_dq_*.py`, `test_dq_apply_rows_contract.py`) pass unchanged, since `apply_rows()` itself was never touched. Added `tests/test_dq_run_apply_migration.py` (8 new tests, synthetic-fixture only): `fix_county_mc_mac_casing` round-trips through a real on-disk board and is idempotent on a second run (exercises `patch_existing_rows()`'s empty-`patches` early return, which crashed `run_apply()`'s own print statement on `stats['existing']=None` until fixed during this work); `quarantine_flip_leaks`'s scope-clear lands as `raw['scope']=None` on disk and the real `distress_score.flip_outside_footprint()` agrees it's no longer out-of-footprint; `map_account_ids_to_parcels`'s in-place `raw` sub-dict mutation is correctly captured (proving the deep-copy requirement is real, not theoretical); both exclusions raise the expected `RuntimeError` naming `owner_mailing`; an untouched row is never sent to `patch_existing_rows()`; the too-large-to-patch ceiling fires before the full-list build. A broader scoped run (`pytest -k "dq_ or web_artifact or board_stream or patch_existing or run_apply"`) passed 257/257; a full unfiltered `pytest -q` run was started but hung with near-zero CPU progress for several minutes under this machine's existing memory pressure (~63 MB free, 66-76% swap) and was killed rather than left to make the pressure worse — this looks like a pre-existing slow/network-touching test elsewhere in the suite, not something introduced here, but it was not isolated/confirmed.

    **Memory, not re-verified live.** `_light_rows()` is still a full materialization (every row held as a `Listing` at once, like `load_board()`), just without the sidecar-merge cost that's `load_board()`'s documented dominant factor — real peak RSS against the current, much-grown (2.4+ GB) board was NOT measured today: memory conditions at the time (~63 MB free, 66-76% swap, matching the same regime `sos_agent_refresh.py`'s/`patch_vision_gemini.py`'s 2026-09-30 migration also declined to live-test) made even a read-only probe against the real board imprudent. A supervised live dry run (RSS/footprint watchdog, `BOARD_PATCH_ALLOW_LARGE=1` if the board is still over `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`'s 2,300 MB ceiling — it was at 2,646 MB as of item 21) is recommended follow-up before trusting this at full scale; no real write was attempted in this session. Commit: see `_dq_common.py` + `tests/test_dq_run_apply_migration.py`.

23. **DONE (2026-10-01) — migrated `scripts/lrcpwa_refresh.py` (item 21's remaining noon job, the one bucket-1 script not yet fixed) off `load_board()`/`write_artifact()`.** Traced every enricher it calls, not assumed: `enrich_lrcpwa_parcel()`/`enrich_lrcpwa_photo()` are bounded, network-bound, per-row passes with their own small `targets` lists (capped by `LRCPWA_MAX`/`LRCPWA_PHOTO_MAX`, historically ~2,772/~3,849 of ~212K rows) — same shape as `sos_agent_refresh.py`'s targets. `enrich_strategy_fit()`/`enrich_buyer_match()` and `valuation.calc.compute()`/`valuation.grading.grade()`, by contrast, ran over the WHOLE board in the original (not just lrcpwa's targets) — but every one is a pure per-row function of that one `Listing`'s own (sidecar-merged) raw dict, confirmed by reading all four enrichment modules plus `valuation/calc.py`/`valuation/grading.py` in full and grepping for any `listings`-wide loop (none exists; `COUNTY_MEDIAN_PPSF` and similar are static tables). That is "evaluated against every row" (a large population), not board-wide cross-referencing like `dedupe()`/`distress_score.score_board()` (`daily_api_refresh.py`'s case, correctly still unmigrated) — so it streams: one pass over `web_artifact._iter_board_records()` (sidecar merged — `calc.compute()` reads `raw['vision']`/`raw['cama']`/`raw['comps']`, all `LAZY_DETAIL_KEYS`, the exact hazard `patch_vision_gemini.py`'s own docstring warns about) that collects the bounded lrcpwa target lists via a cheap pre-check (reusing `enrichment_lrcpwa_parcel._county()`/`enrichment_lrcpwa_photo._worth_photo()`/`_has_image()` directly, not duplicated) and, for every OTHER row, finishes it (calc/grade recompute + strategy_fit/buyer_match retag) right there and discards it — never holding more than one non-target row in memory at a time, a tighter profile than `_dq_common.run_apply()`'s `_light_rows()` (which must hold its whole working set because ITS callers need repeated/random cross-row access; this script's callers do not). The bounded lrcpwa-touched set (a row can be a target for BOTH enrichers at once — same object in both lists, preserving the original's "photo enrichment reuses whatever parcel enrichment just resolved" interaction) is finished the same way after `enrich_lrcpwa_parcel()`/`enrich_lrcpwa_photo()` run, each diffed against a snapshot taken BEFORE any mutation (dedupe_key() captured pre-mutation too, since `_apply()` fills `street_address`/`zip_code` — both identity fields — on exactly the rows targeted; same hazard `resolver_backfill_parcel.py`'s parcel_id case documents). One `web_artifact.patch_existing_rows()` call lands everything. `RAW_KEEP` already covers every key this script writes (`lrcpwa`, `images`, `zillow`, `calc`, `grade`, `strategy_fit`, `buyer_match` — checked directly in `web_artifact.py`, not assumed), so there is no silent-drop-on-next-rewrite risk. Disclosed, deliberate behavior improvement: a row that fails `Listing.model_validate()` is no longer silently deleted from the board (a patch-based write cannot delete what it doesn't target) — it is simply skipped this run and left exactly as it was; counted and printed, not hard-failed on. Tests: `tests/test_lrcpwa_refresh_patch_integration.py` (4 new, synthetic-fixture only) covering a parcel-only target, a photo-only target, a dual target, an ordinary row outside the lrcpwa footprint still getting board-wide strategy_fit/calc/grade, and idempotency (a third run, not a second — the first run's own grade computation can newly qualify a row for a photo on the NEXT run, a faithful reproduction of the original's own cross-run timing since photo-targeting always ran before that day's calc/grade recompute, not a bug introduced here). Pre-existing `test_lrcpwa.py`/`test_lrcpwa_photo.py`/`test_strategy_fit.py`/`test_buyer_match.py`/`test_publish_plumbing.py`/`test_ops_shell.py -k lrcpwa` all pass unchanged. **Not verified live**: memory conditions at migration time (~63 MB free, ~67% swap, two other Claude Code sessions concurrently active) matched the same regime the 2026-09-30 and item-22 migrations also declined to live-test against the real board, including a read-only probe — this is recommended follow-up (RSS/footprint watchdog, `BOARD_PATCH_ALLOW_LARGE=1`, since the board was at 2,646 MB against `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`'s 2,300 MB ceiling as of item 21) once a safe window opens. Commit: see `scripts/lrcpwa_refresh.py` + `tests/test_lrcpwa_refresh_patch_integration.py`.

24. **DONE for 2 of 3, GENUINELY NOT BOUNDABLE for the third (2026-10-01) — item 21's Tier 2 `patch_distress_score.py` trio.** Read all three scripts in full (`patch_owner_mailing.py`, `patch_court_detail.py`, `patch_distress_score.py`): all three shared the identical unsafe shape (`read_board_json()` to parse the whole board, hydrate every row via a hand-rolled `_hydrate()`, mutate, `path.write_text(json.dumps(data))` straight to `docs/listings.json`, then a hand-rolled `reseal_board(resplit=True)` — bypassing `write_artifact()`/`patch_existing_rows()` entirely, same "manual trap" class as the already-fixed `patch_vision_gemini.py`). What they patch, verified by reading the actual enrichment calls rather than trusting the filenames: `patch_owner_mailing.py` runs `enrich_owner_mailing()` (county GIS owner/mailing/absentee + a CAMA distress sub-object + GIS building specs); `patch_court_detail.py` runs `enrich_with_nc_case_status_authenticated()` + `enrich_case_detail_addresses()` (NC Tyler / SC Public Index case status, judgment amount, sale date, sale_status) then conditionally recomputes calc/grade; `patch_distress_score.py` runs `distress_score.score_board()` (the stacked HOT/WARM/COLD tiering).

    **Boundedness check (the actual question this item answers, not assumed from the names).** `enrich_owner_mailing()`'s and both court enrichers' own `targets` filters read only each row's OWN fields (state/county/parcel_id/street_address/case_number/raw's own status flags) — no cross-row comparison, each target resolved independently. Bounded, per-row, same shape as `sos_agent_refresh.py`'s/`lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s already-migrated enrichers. `distress_score.score_board()` is the opposite: it groups EVERY listing on the board by parcel key first (`_score_group()`), then scores each group from the UNION of signals across every listing in it — "the same property in foreclosure AND tax sale stacks; cross-listing" per its own module docstring. A row's `distress_stack` therefore depends on every OTHER row sharing its parcel, discovered only by having scanned the whole board first. This is not a hypothetical distinction: `docs/HANDOFF.md` item 15 already named this EXACT function, by name, as one of the two reasons `daily_api_refresh.py` is "NOT fixed and remains broken" — "`distress_score.score_board()` (board-wide parcel grouping + percentile HOT/WARM/COLD tiering) ... No existing safe primitive supports row deletion or requires the full `list[Listing]` at once the way these two functions do — this needs genuine new primitives, not a plumbing migration." `patch_distress_score.py` calls the identical function, so the identical conclusion applies: **not migrated, same honesty standard as `daily_api_refresh.py`** — forcing it onto `board_stream.iter_board_rows()`/`patch_existing_rows()` would mean either re-materializing the whole board anyway (no actual memory win) or silently changing the scoring algorithm's cross-row grouping, neither acceptable as a "plumbing only" change. No code in `patch_distress_score.py` was touched this session; it remains exactly as unsafe as item 21 found it, a `read_board_json()`/`path.write_text()` pair with no size guard at all — a real risk if anyone runs it by hand against today's board, now flagged a second time with the specific reason it can't just be copy-pasted from its two siblings.

    **The lazy-detail subtlety (item 21's own flagged "check this carefully"), resolved two different ways for the two migrated scripts.** `enrich_owner_mailing()` WRITES one `LAZY_DETAIL_KEY` (`raw['cama']`) but never READS one first (grepped in full) — a straight unconditional replace built fresh from the GIS response — so `patch_owner_mailing.py` safely uses the slim `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` (no sidecar merge, the cheaper read). `patch_court_detail.py`'s post-pass recompute (`vcalc.compute()`/`vgrade.grade()`) DOES read `raw['vision']`/`raw['cama']`/`raw['comps']` (confirmed by grep, the same fact `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s migration already established) — so it uses `web_artifact._iter_board_records()` (sidecar merged), the heavier read, same tradeoff `patch_vision_gemini.py` made. A genuine, disclosed side effect: because the OLD `read_board_json()` never merged the sidecar either, `patch_court_detail.py`'s calc/grade recompute was ALREADY silently running without vision/comps/cama on any board that had gone through a normal publish since those keys were last set — a real, pre-existing impoverishment this migration fixes as a byproduct of using the correct primitive, not something it introduces.

    **Two more disclosed, deliberate improvements (same house style as the lrcpwa/patch_vision_gemini migrations — called out, not left for someone to discover).** The OLD code in both scripts only ever copied a couple of fields from the mutated `Listing` back onto the row it wrote (`patch_owner_mailing.py`: `raw` + `parcel_id` only; `patch_court_detail.py`: `judgment_amount` + `street_address` only) — silently discarding every OTHER scalar field those enrichers also fill: `tax_value`/`market_value`/`assessed_value`/`living_sqft`/`year_built`/`bedrooms`/`bathrooms` on the owner-mailing side, `sale_date` on the court-detail side (all verified by reading the enrichment modules in full, not assumed). The new diff-based patch (every scalar field, before vs. after) persists all of these now. Separately, `patch_existing_rows()` MERGES a `raw` update rather than replacing it, unlike the old `d["raw"] = _to_dict(li)["raw"]` full-replace — an existing row's vision/comps/grade/skip_trace now reliably survive a run of either script.

    **Tests** (synthetic fixtures only, no real board touched): `tests/test_patch_owner_mailing_patch_integration.py` and `tests/test_patch_court_detail_patch_integration.py`, 2 tests each — a target row gets every changed field (incl. the newly-persisted scalars) landed in one patch, an already-filled/no-case-number row is left completely untouched, a pre-existing unrelated `raw` key survives alongside the new one (proving merge-not-replace), and a second run is idempotent (nothing to patch). Scoped regression run: `pytest -k "patch_owner_mailing or patch_court_detail or patch_distress_score or web_artifact or board_stream or dq_ or patch_existing or run_apply or lrcpwa"` — 289 passed. `tests/test_publish_plumbing.py` (which asserts every one of these three scripts still calls `board_lock(`) — 36 passed, unchanged.

    **Not live-tested against the real board**, same reasoning as every 2026-09-30/10-01 migration this week: `ps aux` showed no board-writer or git-push process running, but `vm_stat`/`sysctl vm.swapusage` showed ~79 MB free / ~78% swap / load average 3.65 at migration time, matching the regime every prior migration this week also declined to live-test against, including a read-only probe. `patch_court_detail.py` in particular still fully materializes the board WITH the sidecar merge (the same cost `load_board()` has) because its calc/grade recompute genuinely needs it — a true constant-memory redesign (re-deriving both court enrichers' own internal targeting/capping externally, `lrcpwa_refresh.py`-style) was judged not worth the duplication risk for this pass and is the clearest remaining follow-up if this script is ever put back on a schedule. A supervised live dry run (RSS watchdog, `BOARD_PATCH_ALLOW_LARGE=1` if the board is still over `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`'s ceiling) is recommended before trusting either migrated script at full board size. Commits: see `scripts/patch_owner_mailing.py` + `scripts/patch_court_detail.py` + their two new test files (one commit, migrated together — same shape, same shared `_dq_common.require_raw_keep` preflight, differing only in which board-stream reader the lazy-detail check calls for); `scripts/patch_distress_score.py` deliberately untouched.

25. **DONE (2026-10-01) — a live test of item 24's `patch_owner_mailing.py` migration found it still didn't achieve memory safety; fixed that plus item 24's own flagged follow-up for `patch_court_detail.py` the same session.** The live test's finding: swapping `read_board_json()` for `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` dropped the raw JSON text/dict `load_board()` parses (its documented dominant cost), but `patch_owner_mailing.py` still collected that reader's full output into one `listings: list[Listing]` for the WHOLE board, unconditionally, before `is_target()` ever got to filter anything — item 24's own `patch_owner_mailing.py` docstring already admitted this ("still a FULL materialization… same as `load_board()`"), but hadn't been load-tested against it. There was also no `OM_MAX`/`LIMIT`-style env var anywhere in the script, unlike `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s `LRCPWA_MAX`/`LRCPWA_PHOTO_MAX` — no ceiling on how much work one run could take on.

    **The fix (`patch_owner_mailing.py`).** `enrich_owner_mailing()`'s own `targets` filter (previously three closures nested inside that function) was pulled out to top-level `is_reachable()`/`has_mailing()`/`is_target()` functions in `enrichment_owner_mailing.py`, so `patch_owner_mailing.py` could apply the SAME filter per-row, during the `iter_board_rows()` pass itself, on a cheap `Listing.model_construct()` over just the fields the filter reads — instead of hand-deriving a second copy, which risks silently drifting from the real filter the next time a county is added to `COUNTY_GIS`. Reuse over duplication, the same principle `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s `_is_parcel_target()`/`_is_photo_target()` already established against `enrichment_lrcpwa_parcel`/`enrichment_lrcpwa_photo`. A new `OM_MAX` env var (default 6000, matching `LRCPWA_MAX`/`LRCPWA_PHOTO_MAX`'s default for a comparably-shaped per-row ArcGIS-bound pass) caps how many eligible targets get fully hydrated and added to the working set per run; every further eligible row past the cap is counted ("deferred") and dropped without ever being hydrated, left exactly as-is for a future run — same incremental-coverage-builds-across-runs model the vision/lrcpwa passes already use. The "mailable now N" board-wide summary survives without ever holding the board: a running count during the streaming pass (same cheap `has_mailing()` check that decides whether to build a Listing at all) plus the newly-resolved count from the small capped working set. Unlike `lrcpwa_refresh.py`, non-target rows need no board-wide recompute here (`enrich_owner_mailing()` has no board-wide pass), so a deferred row is left completely untouched, not partially finished.

    **The same investigation for `patch_court_detail.py` (item 24's own flagged follow-up — its docstring named this exact gap: "a true constant-memory redesign … was judged not worth the duplication risk … the clearest remaining follow-up if this script is ever put back on a schedule").** Confirmed it had the identical (arguably worse) problem: it hydrated a FULL Listing, WITH the sidecar merge (`_iter_board_records()`, the heaviest read shape — required because its calc/grade recompute reads `raw['vision']/['cama']/['comps']`), for literally every board row, not just the ~110 rows (`NC_ECOURTS_AUTH_CAP`=50 + `SC_COURT_CAP`=60 by default) either court enricher would ever touch over the network. Fixed the same session, with the same reuse-not-duplicate approach: `enrich_with_nc_case_status_authenticated()`'s and `enrich_case_detail_addresses()`'s own targeting closures were pulled out to top-level `is_target(li, incremental)` functions in `enrichment_nc_case_status_tyler.py`/`enrichment_case_detail.py`. `patch_court_detail.py`'s single `_iter_board_records()` pass now applies each predicate on a cheap `Listing.model_construct()` per row, splitting eligible rows into `nc_candidates`/`sc_candidates` — fully hydrated only for those, plus any row that already carries a court status from a prior run (finished immediately, one at a time, for the unconditional calc/grade recompute the original always ran on those). Deliberately NOT pre-capped at this script's own level: `enrich_with_nc_case_status_authenticated()` sorts its own targets by sale-date priority BEFORE applying its cap, so the FULL NC-eligible candidate set has to reach it for that sort to pick the right cases — pre-capping here would have silently substituted board order for priority order. Each enricher's own internal `is_target()`+sort+cap logic still runs exactly as before, unchanged, just no longer fed listings this script never needed to hydrate. Honestly flagged as NOT a hard O(small-constant) bound the way `patch_owner_mailing.py`'s `OM_MAX` is — `nc_candidates`/`sc_candidates` hold every NC/SC row with a case_number eligible this run, board-wide, which is a large reduction from the whole board (most listing types never carry a case_number) but not capped by a ceiling; if that population ever grew to approach the whole board, an `LRCPWA_MAX`-style ceiling would be needed here too.

    **Tests.** `tests/test_patch_owner_mailing_patch_integration.py`: 4 new tests alongside the 2 existing (unchanged) ones — below-cap behavior matches the uncapped script, above-cap only the first `OM_MAX` targets (board order) get processed and the rest are left byte-for-byte untouched, a later run with a bigger cap clears the deferred backlog, and the "mailable now N" arithmetic stays correct (pre-existing + newly-resolved) without a board-wide hold. `tests/test_patch_court_detail_patch_integration.py`: 1 new test alongside the 2 existing (unchanged) ones, proving the NC/SC candidate lists reach the real enrichers at full size (not pre-capped) using fakes that just record the list length they were called with. Full relevant-area regression (`pytest -k` across `patch_owner_mailing`, `patch_court_detail`, `nc_case_status`, `case_detail`, `owner_mailing`, `lrcpwa`, `web_artifact`, `board_stream`, `dq_`, `patch_existing`, `run_apply`, `patch_distress_score`): 368 passed, 8 skipped. Full suite: `python -m pytest -q` — see commit for the result (ran to completion separately; one pre-existing slow-but-passing test, `test_arc_query_hard_timeout.py`'s trickling-host cases, briefly looks stalled at ~3% because it deliberately exercises a multi-second retry/backoff budget — not a hang, not touched by this change).

    **Code-only.** No board data touched or committed — explicitly out of scope per the task that found this (memory was too tight — ~87 MB free, 83.5% swap used — to even read-stream the real board safely). Commits: see `scripts/patch_owner_mailing.py` + `src/foreclosure_scraper/enrichment_owner_mailing.py` + `scripts/patch_court_detail.py` + `src/foreclosure_scraper/enrichment_nc_case_status_tyler.py` + `src/foreclosure_scraper/enrichment_case_detail.py` + their two test files.

26. **DONE (2026-10-01) — closed the equity-staleness structural gap at `grading.grade()`: CLOSED for the dominant path, one small residual acknowledged.** Two live-board incidents the same day both needed a reactive backfill for the identical shape of bug: `5616f840`/`00beac83` (9 rows) and `533d8e58` (671 rows, caused by `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s first successful per-row calc/grade recompute in over a week) both found rows publishing `raw['equity']` alongside a freshly `ARV_FLAGS_CONTRADICTED` ARV — money the calc-side gate (`grading.apply_arv_trust_gate`) correctly withheld, sitting next to an equity figure nothing re-checked. `a40f3689` (2026-09-30) had already fixed the identical shape for `Listing.merge()` specifically (`models._regate_merged_valuation`, called right after `_deep_merge_dict`); both of today's incidents were the OTHER, much larger half of the same seam: a targeted recompute (any script that calls `valuation.calc.compute()` then `valuation.grading.grade(li, c)` over some subset of an already-equity-bearing board) flips `arv_flags` into the contradicted set, and nothing tells the separate, later `enrichment_equity.enrich_equity()` pass to re-run on just those rows.

    **Investigation, not assumed.** Grepped every `vcalc.compute(li)` call site in `src/` and `scripts/` (~25, listed in the commit) and confirmed each one is immediately followed by `vgrade.grade(li, c)` — matching `grade()`'s own docstring claim that it is "the ONE seam every producer of raw['calc'] shares." `apply_arv_trust_gate()` (called inside `grade()`) already returns the trust level computed from the fresh `Calc` object; it just never touched `raw['equity']`, which `enrichment_equity.py` writes in a wholly separate pass main.py runs well afterward. `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s `_finish_row()` was read line-by-line as the confirmed culprit for the 671-row incident: it writes `li.raw["calc"]` after `grade()` but never looks at `li.raw["equity"]` at all.

    **The fix (`src/foreclosure_scraper/valuation/grading.py`).** New `_regate_equity_after_grade(li, level, c)`, called from `grade()` immediately after `apply_arv_trust_gate()` returns, reusing that call's own trust level (not re-derived from a possibly-stale `li.raw['calc']`) — local-imports `enrichment_equity.withhold_equity` (dodges the grading↔enrichment_equity import cycle, same pattern `models.py` already uses for the merge fix) and strips a stale `raw['equity']` only when the level is CONTRADICTED/WITHHELD. Scoped with the same discipline as the merge-path fix: a no-op when level is ok/weak (never imports or touches equity), a no-op when `li.raw` isn't a dict (mirrors `_regate_merged_valuation`'s own guard — grade() must not be the one to invent a raw dict), and a cheap no-op when equity is absent or already withheld (`withhold_equity` is itself idempotent). Because every real call site already runs `compute()` then `grade()`, this one hook reaches `lrcpwa_refresh.py`, `sqft_backfill.py`, `fill_sqft.py`, and every future script of the same shape for free — the same guarantee `apply_arv_trust_gate` already gives the calc-side money fields, extended to cover the field that incident after incident kept being the one left out.

    **Verdict for tracking: CLOSED for the path both incidents actually came from (any script that calls compute()+grade()).** One acknowledged, narrower residual: `gate_calc_dict()` (the dict-only "belt and braces" mirror of this gate, for already-serialized/legacy calc blocks with no `Listing` in scope) structurally cannot reach `raw['equity']` itself — it has no Listing to read or write, and widening its signature was judged not worth forcing. Its two current callers (`models._regate_merged_valuation` and `scripts/backfill_contradicted_arv_gate.py`) already call `withhold_equity` explicitly themselves, same discipline `enrichment_board_qa.py`'s `SHARED_ANCHOR_FLAG` retraction uses — so this is a "the next caller must remember" gap, not an active one, and it caused neither of today's incidents. `enrichment_data_quality.py`'s own `gate_calc_dict()` call was checked too: every real caller of `enrich_data_quality()` already runs `compute()`+`grade()` first (now fixed), so it never independently encounters a calc-contradicted-but-equity-still-fresh row this change hasn't already handled.

    **Tests.** `tests/test_arv_trust_gate.py`, 7 new cases: a newly-contradicted row withholds equity with no caller ever invoking `enrich_equity()`/`withhold_equity()` directly; the withheld-ARV tier behaves the same; WEAK-evidence and never-flagged rows are completely untouched (equity dict compared byte-for-byte before/after); a second `grade()` pass over an already-withheld row is a verified no-op; a bare `Listing.model_construct(raw=None, ...)` does not crash and does not get a raw dict invented; and a direct comparison proves the `merge()` path (`a40f3689`) and this `grade()` path reach an identical withheld marker for an identical calc block. Full suite: `python -m pytest -q` — 6293 passed, 60 skipped, 0 failed. Commit: `3dc5b012`.

27. **DONE for `dedupe()`, DONE for `score_board()`, 1 of 4 scripts migrated (2026-10-01) — investigated whether a genuinely safe, reusable primitive could exist for the class of problem item 24 declared un-migratable ("board-wide parcel grouping ... no existing safe primitive supports ... requires the full `list[Listing]` at once"). Found a third option neither item 15 nor item 24 considered, and built it: `src/foreclosure_scraper/board_dedupe_stream.py`.**

    **The investigation, not assumed.** Read `dedupe.py`'s `dedupe()` in full: it never reads `Listing.raw` AT ALL — its three passes (bucket by `dedupe_key()`, cross-bucket fuzzy address match, signature union-find) read only 8 scalar fields, and the fuzzy pass ALREADY uses zip/county+state BLOCKING (added 2026-08-27, "cuts O(n²) on 116K listings (6.7B comparisons) down to ~67M") — not naive full pairwise comparison. `raw` only matters inside `Listing.merge()` (which `dedupe()` calls to fold a bucket). Read `distress_score.py`'s `score_board()`/`_score_group()`/`_collect()`/`_parcel_key()` in full and grepped every `r.get(...)`/`raw.get(...)` call site: grouping needs only scalar fields (same shape as dedupe), but the actual SIGNAL computation needs ~33 named raw sub-keys per row (tax_owed, bankruptcy, probate, divorce, code_enforcement, storm_damage, title_risk, owner_mailing, calc, equity, ... — the full list is `board_dedupe_stream.SCORE_RAW_KEYS`) — far more than dedupe needs, but still an explicit, enumerable, EXCLUDING subset of everything a fully-enriched row carries (vision/comps/cama/rent_comps — already excluded by `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` — plus description, assessor_card, gis_attrs, skip_trace, strategy_fit, buyer_match, multifamily_class, data_quality, outreach, images, and more).

    **The primitive: feed the REAL function a lighter population, don't reimplement it.** Both `dedupe()` and `score_board()` are called UNCHANGED, on a population of lightweight `Listing`s built by streaming `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` once (never `load_board()`), each carrying only the fields the function actually reads. Because it's the same literal function, correctness follows from the field whitelist being exhaustive, not from re-deriving the algorithm — `tests/test_board_dedupe_stream.py` proves this empirically (same synthetic fixture scored/deduped both the old whole-board way and the new streaming way, asserted field-for-field identical) rather than resting on the grep alone, including a dedicated "noisy raw" sensitivity test that would catch `distress_score.py` ever reading a new raw key this whitelist hasn't been told about.
      - `find_dedupe_merge_groups()`: PASS 1 streams the board into lightweight stubs, each tagged `raw["_prov"] = {row_identity_hash(): True}` — a DICT, not a list, because `models._deep_merge_dict()` UNIONS dict-valued leaves on a merge conflict but REPLACES list-valued ones (verified directly, `test_prov_list_would_NOT_survive...`), so running the REAL `dedupe()` on these stubs gets free, zero-modification provenance tracking: each output stub's `_prov` ends up holding the exact set of original row hashes folded into it. PASS 2 is `dedupe()` itself (pure computation, no I/O). PASS 3 is a second targeted streaming pass over just the small candidate set, to pick the merge survivor (same completeness heuristic `scripts/run_duplicate_merge_20260930.py` already uses in production). Output: `merge_groups` ready for the ALREADY-SHIPPED `web_artifact.merge_duplicate_rows()` (commit `6719e6bd`, 2026-09-30 — this investigation discovered the apply-side half of this problem was already solved and nobody had connected it to the find-side gap).
      - `stream_score_board()`: PASS 1 streams the board into lightweight stubs carrying `SCORE_RAW_KEYS` only, while recording each row's CURRENT `distress_stack` and `dedupe_key()`. PASS 2 is `score_board()` itself, unmodified. PASS 3 diffs new vs. old stack per `dedupe_key()`-GROUP (not per row — `patch_existing_rows()` applies one value to every row sharing a key, so a key shared by rows that score DIFFERENTLY, which can genuinely happen since `dedupe_key()`'s address/case branches are looser than `_parcel_key()`'s >= 4-alnum-char parcel grouping, is dropped rather than guessed at — same defensive check `scripts/backfill_unlocatable_equity_retraction.py` already applies for this exact field). Output: a `patches` dict ready for the already-shipped `web_artifact.patch_existing_rows()`. One disclosed, deliberate gap: `score_board()` POPS `distress_stack` entirely when every listing on a parcel is sold; `patch_existing_rows()` can only SET fields, never delete one, so this publishes `{}` instead — every reader of `distress_stack` in this codebase was grepped and all of them treat `{}` and an absent key identically.
      - **Missing primitive confirmed and built: `web_artifact.delete_rows()`.** `daily_api_refresh.py`'s stale-REO prune needs to remove a row with NO survivor — `merge_duplicate_rows()` requires 2+ members per group. Built mirroring `merge_duplicate_rows()`'s exact shape (own ceiling `BOARD_DELETE_MAX_SOURCE_MB`/`BOARD_DELETE_ALLOW_LARGE`, inherits patch/merge's 2,300 MB pending a dedicated trial; keyed by `row_identity_hash()`, not `dedupe_key()`, since a destructive op should use the narrowest identity available; all-or-nothing refusal on any missing/ambiguous hash, `BoardDeleteMismatch`, nothing partial). Caught one real bug writing its own test (`test_delete_rows.py`'s sidecar-preservation assertion): an early draft popped `LAZY_DETAIL_KEYS` twice — once to compute `row_identity_hash()`, once to build `listings_detail.json` — discarding every surviving row's vision/comps/cama on the SECOND, now-empty pop. Fixed to pop once (mirroring `merge_duplicate_rows()`'s own single-pop pattern exactly) before this landed anywhere near real data. 17 tests, all passing.
      - **Migrated as proof of concept: `scripts/patch_distress_score.py`.** The task's own minimum bar, chosen because it is the ONE of the 4 scripts whose ENTIRE job is a whole-board score pass with no other full-row enrichment in between — the complete, unqualified win case. Old script: `read_board_json()` (loads via the plain `.gz`-fallback path, no size guard at all, flagged a second time by item 24 as "a real risk if anyone runs it by hand") → hand-rolled `path.write_text(json.dumps(data))` → `reseal_board(resplit=True)`. New script: `stream_score_board()` → `patch_existing_rows()` (which already regenerates the parts + manifest itself, so the separate `reseal_board()` call is gone too). Same "NOT PUBLISHING listings_slim.json.gz/detail_shards/" disclosed gap as before (both the old script and `patch_existing_rows()` independently lack it; no regression). `tests/test_patch_distress_score_integration.py`: fresh-board scoring, second-run idempotency (byte-identical file, not just "no crash"), lock-busy handling.

    **Honest scope limit, not glossed over.** `daily_api_refresh.py`/`ingest_publicindex_files.py`/`ingest_fresh_court_leads.py` were NOT migrated this session. All three call `dedupe()`/`score_board()` on a list ALREADY fully materialized via `load_board()` for OTHER reasons (equity/CAMA/GIS/title-risk/owner-mailing enrichment chains, which need full `Listing` objects with full `raw` and are out of this module's scope) — swapping just their dedupe/score calls for these streaming primitives would not, by itself, reduce THOSE scripts' peak memory, since `load_board()` already ran before either call. The dedupe FINDER is still useful there as a periodic, STANDALONE cleanup pass (the same role `scripts/run_duplicate_merge_20260930.py` already plays, now generalizable instead of a narrow one-off parcel/address heuristic) rather than as a drop-in replacement inside those 3 scripts' existing control flow — a real architectural change (streaming every enrichment step) would be needed to get THEM off `load_board()`, which is a much larger undertaking than this item's scope.

    **Tests.** `tests/test_board_dedupe_stream.py` (13 cases: bucket/fuzzy/signature-union merges + the house-number guard, each checked against `dedupe()` on fully-hydrated objects; a 11-row multi-signal fixture — foreclosure+probate stacking, tax debt, code enforcement, bankruptcy, divorce, MLS stale/price-cut, upset bid, senior-lien title risk, a fully-sold-confirmed parcel's stack removal — checked field-for-field against `score_board()` on fully-hydrated objects; the `SCORE_RAW_KEYS` sensitivity test; the `_prov` dict-union mechanism itself; the collision guard applying once when safe vs. dropping when genuinely ambiguous), `tests/test_delete_rows.py` (17 cases, same contract shape as `test_merge_duplicate_rows.py`), `tests/test_patch_distress_score_integration.py` (3 cases). Full suite: see the run noted at the bottom of this item's commit.

28. **MEASURED + PARTIALLY FIXED (2026-10-01). Real vision coverage is 9.93% (21,752/219,143), not the stale "~13%" this file quoted — and the 2026-09-30 `patch_vision_gemini.py` save failure (item 15) was stopping it from ever catching up, every day, silently.** Coverage measured two ways: a safe `board_stream.iter_board_rows()`-only sweep (no board lock needed, ~300 MB peak RSS, 21s) confirmed the board is 219,143 rows and gave a slim-visible lower bound via `raw['condition_source']` starting with `"vision-"` (set only for HIGH/MEDIUM-confidence grades) of 10,598 rows (4.84%) — `raw['vision']` itself is a `LAZY_DETAIL_KEY`, invisible to that reader by construction, so this undercounts. The real, sidecar-aware count is already printed by `patch_vision_gemini.py` itself every run: today's 09:30 log (`logs/daily-vision-20261001T093005.log`) reads "21752 already vision-scored" out of the same 219,143 rows before this run's own 27 newly-computed scores — which then also got lost to the BoardLoadTooLarge crash below — making the durably-published count still 21,752 (9.93%). The board grew 28% since the 13% figure was measured (170,588 → 219,143 rows, 2026-09-21) while the absolute scored count barely moved, so coverage has gotten WORSE in relative terms, not better, and would keep getting worse every day the save kept silently failing.
    **Root cause of the save failure, confirmed by reading `patch_existing_rows()`/`_raise_if_board_too_large_to_patch()` and `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`'s own measurement comment, not assumed:** its memory cost is proportional to the EXISTING board's total on-disk size, because it streams, re-encodes and holds every row (patched or not) before writing — NOT to how many rows the `patches` dict actually touches. Real measured data already in this file's history confirms it: a 27-row patch (today) and a 1,475-row patch (item 20, same board scale) both land in the same 3.5-4.1 GiB peak-footprint range. This means capping `VISION_MAX_LISTINGS` (already exists, defaults to 15000) tighter would not have helped the write at all — it only bounds API spend — and raising `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB` for an unattended cron job was already explicitly rejected in that constant's own comment, for the same reason this file's memory-safety notes keep repeating: real memory cost tracks board size and headroom on this machine is volatile under concurrent session load, independent of the ceiling number.
    **Fix shipped (commit `2af501c8`), scoped to stopping the bleeding, not to draining the backlog:** `patch_vision_gemini.py` now catches `BoardLoadTooLarge` around its `patch_existing_rows()` call and writes the computed-but-unpersisted patches to `logs/vision_pending_patches.json` (gitignored) instead of losing them to an uncaught traceback. A later run loads that cache, skips re-vision-scoring any candidate whose `dedupe_key()` already has a cached result (stops the wasted Gemini/Cloudflare/NVIDIA/Groq API spend), and folds the cache into its own patch attempt every time, so the backlog applies automatically the moment the board shrinks back under the ceiling or a human runs a supervised `BOARD_PATCH_ALLOW_LARGE=1` pass — the cache only clears once `patch_existing_rows()` actually succeeds. 4 new tests (`tests/test_patch_vision_gemini_patch_integration.py`), synthetic fixtures only, no board write. Full suite 6330 passed, 60 skipped, 0 failed.
    **NOT done, needs a dedicated supervised session:** the board itself is still over `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`, so every unattended daily run will keep hitting the same refusal and keep growing the cache file rather than actually publishing new vision coverage — this fix stops the API waste and data loss, it does not make coverage go up. Draining the cache needs a human-supervised `BOARD_PATCH_ALLOW_LARGE=1` run with the dual RSS/footprint watchdog this file's other large patches already establish (item 20's pattern: peak footprint watched live, killed well under ~5.5 GiB) — not attempted this session, by design (no live board write was in scope). A real structural fix (making `patch_existing_rows()`'s write cost independent of total board size, e.g. by writing incrementally instead of accumulating `listing_blobs`/`details` for all ~219K rows before the final write) would remove the need for the ceiling and the cache entirely, but is a bigger, higher-risk change to a function many other scripts depend on — flagged here as a real option, not attempted.

29. **CONFIRMED + FIXED (2026-10-01) — `raw['probate']` bare-presence false positive, the SAME bug class as the Greenville `distressed=True` stamp (`d1fe4056`/`2190aa5a`/`8081e57b`), found in the LIVE SCORER plus four other readers.** A peer session's divorce/probate/etc. field audit flagged that several scrapers write `raw['probate']` as an ALWAYS-PRESENT "we checked this notice" wrapper regardless of whether anything was actually matched: `sc_public_notices.py` writes `{"decedent": defendant or None, "es_case_number": ... or None, ...}` for every "probate" notice kind, and `column_legal_notices.py`'s SC probate path writes a dict with only `date_of_death`/`personal_representative`, never a decedent name or case number at all. `enrichment_probate_search.py` and `greenville_hard_distress.py`, by contrast, only ever write the key on a real match (guarded by `if results:`/`if probate:` upstream), so their rows always carry a real identifier.
    **Measured board-wide** (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, read-only, 219,143 rows, ~280 MB peak RSS, 15s — no board lock needed): 555 rows carry a `raw['probate']` dict; 135 of them (mostly `column_legal_notices`: 83, `sc_public_notices`: 45) have none of `decedent`/`case_number`/`es_case_number`/`nc_estate_file_no` — an empty wrapper — and **14 of those 135 were riding a bare `if raw.get('probate')` check to a WARM tier** in the live `distress_score.py` scorer on a notice that names no one (confirmed independently, not just trusted from the peer's number).
    **Every current reader of `raw['probate']` did the identical bare-presence check**, not just the scorer: `distress_score.py:732` (the HOT/WARM/COLD tier — the financially consequential one), `enrichment_strategy_fit.py:37` (`_is_probate`, feeds the LAND_WHOLESALE tag), `enrichment_property_category.py:272` (the dashboard category chip's `signals` list), `enrichment_lead_signals.py:126` (`_facet_signals`, feeds the dashboard's "🔥 N signals" chip AND `intent_score`), and `fullmer_rank.py:439` (the Fullmer buy-box ranking's `probate_heirs` +16 bonus). `divorce` (`_divorce_signal`, gates on `case_count`) and `vacancy` (`_vacant_structure`, gates on `vacant`/`boarded_up`) were already correct in `distress_score.py` — no bug there. `two_year_delinquent`/`tax_aging_surfaced`/`deed_chain` are read correctly (sub-field gated) everywhere they're actually read (`fullmer_rank.py`, `enrichment_repeat_tax_loss.py`, `scripts/build_red_flags.py`) and are not read at all by `distress_score.py`. `incarceration_check`/`bop_check` are write-side-only idempotency stamps (a miss marker) — the field the scorer actually reads, `raw['incarceration']`, is written ONLY on a real match (`enrichment_incarceration.py`/`enrichment_bop_federal.py` both gate the write), so bare presence there is correct by construction, not a bug.
    **Fix:** one shared predicate, `signal_freshness.has_real_probate()` (a leaf module, no import cycle), checking `decedent`/`case_number`/`es_case_number`/`nc_estate_file_no` for a non-blank value; all five call sites above now use it instead of bare truthiness. No new raw key, no change to what any scraper writes.
    **Tests:** `tests/test_probate_wrapper_gate.py` (16 new cases covering the predicate itself and all 5 call sites, empty-wrapper vs. real-identifier). 3 pre-existing tests broke on the fix because their fixtures used `raw={"probate": True}` — a bare boolean no real scraper has ever written — as shorthand for "give this row a LIFE_EVENT category"; updated to a realistic `{"case_number": "22E001234"}` dict (`tests/test_arv_sanity.py`, `tests/test_board_dedupe_stream.py`, `tests/test_patch_distress_score_integration.py`), matching the dict-shape convention `tests/test_scoring_audit_fixes.py` already used. Full suite: 6377 passed, 60 skipped, 1 failed (`test_raw_keep_covers_enrichers.py` on `enrichment_co_defendant_signal.py` — a concurrent session's brand-new, still-in-progress enricher not yet registered in `RAW_KEEP`; confirmed unrelated, not touched by this fix).
    **Commit note:** a concurrent session was actively committing in this same working tree while this fix was staged; its `git commit` captured both sessions' staged files in one commit, `9c4d230e` ("counties_nc.transylvania_delinquent_tax: capture outbuilding value + payment dates"), whose message describes only the other session's work. The probate-fix files inside that commit are exactly: `src/foreclosure_scraper/{distress_score,signal_freshness,enrichment_strategy_fit,enrichment_property_category,enrichment_lead_signals,fullmer_rank}.py` and `tests/{test_probate_wrapper_gate.py,test_arv_sanity.py,test_board_dedupe_stream.py,test_patch_distress_score_integration.py}`. Left as-is rather than rewriting history (the other session may already be building on this commit) — flagged here so the mismatch between message and diff doesn't cost a future reader time. No backfill of the 135 already-mis-scored board rows was attempted (out of scope for this fix; the board was at low-memory-headroom conditions for most of this session).

30. **DONE (2026-10-01) — closed 5 of the remaining concrete items in `docs/extraction_gaps.md`'s queue, prioritized by signal value and live-verified, not just code-changed.** Per the owner's mandate (every source fully extracting, not just the zero-data ones) and HERMES sec 9's "wiring a known drop beats a new source," worked the queue's remaining OPEN items in priority order:
    1. **`counties_nc.nc_ptscloud_delinquent_tax` — placeholder `PARCEL_NUM=0` collapsing distinct properties (the queue's "parcel_id 0/61 junk (13)" turned out to be a much bigger bug).** Live-pulling Hyde county's current export found the Farragut tax system exports a literal "0" for any bill whose internal parcel link was never backfilled — not a real parcel number — and 532 DISTINCT real properties (different owners, different legal descriptions) shared that one value in a single live pull. The old code used `PARCEL_NUM` as both the aggregation key AND the published `parcel_id`, so those 532 leads silently collapsed into ONE Listing per run. Fixed with `_row_identity()`: falls back to the (live-verified stable, per-property) `DESCRIPTION` text for identity when `PARCEL_NUM` is this placeholder, and promotes an embedded "PIN: <digits>" GIS parcel number to `parcel_id` whenever present (any row, not just placeholder ones) — a real county PIN is far more useful for cross-source matching than Farragut's own internal sequence number. "61", the gap note's other named value, was re-checked live and is a genuine single-owner multi-year record, not a collision — left untouched. **Live-verified**: Hyde's real export went from 1,330 Listings (old aggregation) to 1,858 (new) — 528 recovered leads — zero `parcel_id` collisions across distinct owners post-fix. Commit `d68ae7c1`.
    2. **`national.courtlistener_{bankruptcy,adversary,civil}` — a servicer/GSE caption becoming a homeowner's name.** Gap note: "servicer/GSE as owner_name (51: SERVICEMAC/FNMA/case-caption) -> owner blocklist + parse defendant from CourtListener caption." The CourtListener `/search/` feed mixes true "`<Plaintiff> v. <Defendant>`" captions in among plain "In re `<Debtor>`" ones (live-verified: "Taylor v. Honeycutt", "United States v. Bingham" on the bankruptcy feed; "Washington v. NewRez, LLC" on the federal real-property feed), and the old code dumped the whole `case_name` into `defendant` — the field nearly every name-resolution enricher (`enrichment_free_phones`, `enrichment_probate_search`, `enrichment_nc_doj`, `enrichment_resolve_name_to_property`, ...) reads as "the person to search." Added `_split_caption()` + `_is_institution()` (keyword gate plus a named-brand list — ServiceMac/NewRez/Mr. Cooper/Shellpoint/Nationstar/PennyMac/... — since several servicers carry no generic keyword of their own) to `courtlistener_bankruptcy.py`, wired into all three scrapers' `fetch()` loops: `plaintiff`/`defendant` now come from the parsed caption, and an institution is withheld from BOTH sides rather than reported as a person. **Live-verified**: bankruptcy `fetch()` against all 4 courts, 400 real listings, 0 institution leaks; civil `fetch()` against all 4 courts, 5 real listings, 0 leaks (NewRez correctly withheld, Washington correctly kept as plaintiff). Bundled into commit `9c4d230e` by a concurrent session's broad `git add` (see item 29's commit note above for the same mechanism) — the courtlistener diff inside that commit is exactly `src/foreclosure_scraper/scrapers/national/courtlistener_{bankruptcy,adversary,civil}.py` + `tests/test_courtlistener_caption_split.py`; that commit's `courtlistener_civil.py`/`courtlistener_adversary.py` hunks also carry an unrelated, independent fix (the same concurrent session's `/dockets/` -> `/search/` endpoint switch + trustee-array promotion) that predates and is compatible with this one.
    3. **`enrichment_board_quality.py` — `auction_status` "status:" label leaks + embedded newlines.** Gap note: "25 rows -> strip in a normalizer." **Live-verified against the published board** (read-only, `board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, no board write): 21 rows carry a "status:"-prefixed value, 3 carry an embedded newline (`"def.\nsal e"`, a PDF-extraction artifact) — 24 rows, matching the queued count. Step 2 of `enrich_board_quality()` now collapses internal whitespace/newlines and strips a leading "status" label before the existing empty/casing normalization. Updated one pre-existing test (`test_shared_row_detection.py::test_truthful_past_sale_status_is_left_verbatim`) whose assertion needed the old literal string, not just its intent (status not overwritten to `sale_date_passed`), which the fix doesn't change. Commit `c30b415a`.
    4. **`enrichment_board_qa.py` — equity published on an `arv_outlier`.** Gap note: "equity-on-arv_outlier (35) -> withhold." `enrichment_data_quality.py`'s `arv_outlier` flag (an ARV implausible in absolute magnitude) runs AFTER `enrich_equity`, so it could only ever caption the problem — the derived equity dollar figure had already published and nothing retracted it, unlike every OTHER ARV-distrust signal. Mirrors the established late-retraction pattern this file already uses for the "one assessor row stamped across many parcels" case: call the existing public `withhold_equity()` + `retract_equity_rank()`, no new mechanism. **Live-verified**: 422 rows on the real published board currently carry `arv_outlier` with a published equity value (grown from the queue's 35, same pattern as every other item in this file); reconstructed 50 of those real rows as Listings and ran the fixed `enrich_board_qa()` — equity correctly withheld on 50/50. The other half of this queue item, "HUD REAC portfolio-total sqft (7)," was investigated (the REAC scraper, the REAC address-backfill enricher, the live `Multifamily_Properties_Assisted` ArcGIS layer's full field list, and `enrichment_footprint_sqft.py`) but could not be reproduced against current code or the live board — left OPEN rather than guessing. Commit `64c3574f`.
    5. **`public_notices.gannett_obituaries` — per-decedent detail (age, death date, a literal home address) never fetched.** Gap note: "never fetches the per-decedent detail (age/funeral-home/survivors)." Checked two ways before fixing: a plain GET to the detail page returns only Tukios widget chrome, and even a full 37s Scrapling `StealthyFetcher` render of the live JS app never surfaces the obituary narrative in the DOM either (checked 3 real decedents) — so this needed neither a stealth browser nor a hidden API call. Graveside services will be held at ... Ashelawn Garden...". Added one extra plain GET per decedent (capped `FORECLOSURE_OBIT_DETAIL_MAX=40`/paper, gate-able with `FORECLOSURE_OBIT_DETAIL=0`), parsing age / death date / a literal home street address / service venue into `raw['obituary']`; a literal home address is set directly as the Listing's `street_address`, skipping the name->parcel resolver entirely. A detail-fetch failure is caught per-decedent and never drops the underlying list-only lead. `timeout_s` raised 120s -> 300s for the ~140 extra GETs across 8 papers. **Live-verified**: a real `fetch()` across 2 papers returned 38 leads, 10/10 detail fetches succeeded, 6 carried a real age, 1 anchored directly via a literal home address. Commit `819648a9`.
