31. **FIXED (2026-10-01) — the pre-write board backup silently no-op'd on a fresh clone (the Oracle VM, revived today after sitting idle since September), found by a real vision-grading write there.** The VM test (23 GB RAM vs. the Mac's 8 GB) proved writes the Mac can't do safely work fine on the VM — real board write through `BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`'s 2,300 MB ceiling, peak ~5.5 GB against 23 GB available — but its log also carried a `web_artifact.backup_failed` warning, `FileNotFoundError` on `docs/listings.json`, on every write that day.
    **Root cause:** `docs/listings.json` (plain) and a single `docs/listings.json.gz` are both gitignored/never committed — the real board (~1 GB+) exceeds GitHub's 100 MB/file limit even gzipped as one file, so what git actually tracks is `docs/board.manifest.json` + `docs/listings_part_NNN.json.gz` shards (confirmed via `.gitignore` and `git ls-files docs/`). A fresh `git clone` therefore never materializes a monolithic `listings.json`; only a local `write_artifact()` run regenerates the plain copy (confirmed live: a fresh `write_artifact()` call writes both the plain file AND the parts/manifest). `_count_guard_and_backup()`'s backup step (the thing `write_artifact()`/`append_new_rows()`/`patch_existing_rows()` all share) gated entry on `_board_file_present()`, which already correctly treats parts-only as "present" (`_bp.has_parts()`) — but then did a bare `shutil.copy2(listings_path, ...)` assuming the plain file was actually there. On a parts-only clone that raised `FileNotFoundError`, caught by the surrounding `except Exception: log.warning(...)` ("backup failure must not block the write") and swallowed — so the write proceeded with no real backup, silently, on every VM write that day, unlike every Mac write this session (which always has the plain file locally).
    **Fix:** new `_backup_listings_board()` reuses the same source-selection the READ path already relies on (`_choose_board_source`, the function `read_board_json`/`_iter_board_records` use to transparently handle plain/`.gz`-twin/parts) to find whichever representation is actually on disk, and backs up THAT one — a plain-file copy for "plain", a `.gz` copy for the legacy single-gz shape, and a copy of the whole shard set (manifest + every `listings_part_NNN.json.gz`) into its own timestamped `backups/listings_<ts>_parts/` directory for the parts shape. All three are pure file copies (never parses/materializes the board into memory — the Mac's 8 GB budget is exactly why `_BACKUP_KEEP` already caps whole-board copies at 3), so the Mac's normal path is byte-for-byte unchanged (confirmed: `write_artifact` on a plain-file board still produces `listings_<ts>.json` with content identical to the pre-write board). The prune step gained a third glob (`listings_2*_parts`, pruned via `shutil.rmtree`) so parts-shaped backups don't grow unbounded on an always-fresh-clone host the way the 2026-09-17 detail-backup bug did.
    **Tests:** `tests/test_backup_fresh_clone.py`, 6 new cases, synthetic fixtures only (no board write) — a parts-only ("fresh clone") directory gets a real, `board_parts.read_rows()`-recoverable backup via both `append_new_rows()` and `write_artifact()`; the plain-file case is provably unchanged (byte-identical backup content, no parts-directory path taken); `_backup_listings_board()` unit-tested directly against a legacy single-gz-no-manifest shape and against "nothing exists" (must raise, not silently succeed empty); the new parts-directory prune pattern is capped at `_BACKUP_KEEP`. **Full suite**: 6428 passed, 60 skipped, 1 failed, 620s (`.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q`, 2026-10-01). The 1 failure (`test_raw_keep_covers_enrichers.py::test_enricher_raw_keys_survive_the_publish_slim[enrichment_co_defendant_signal.py]`) is the SAME pre-existing, unrelated concurrent-session issue items 29/30 above already name (a brand-new enricher not yet registered in `RAW_KEEP`) — confirmed again here: this fix touches neither `enrichment_co_defendant_signal.py` nor `RAW_KEEP`.
    **Scope:** code-only; no write to either host's live board. Applies to the VM automatically on its next `git pull` — SSH/VM filesystem were not touched, per the task's instructions. Commit `32111569`.

Also wired the `legal_description` (lot + plat-book@page) that sits on every row and was never captured, derived a MOBILE `property_kind` from an "MH" token in it, and fixed `ADDR_RE` matching "Rd" inside "Edward" / "St" inside "Steele" as bare substrings.

The SC TMS/PIN printed on every row was never captured as `parcel_id` (0/34 → 34/34). `ADDR_RE` had the same missing-`\b`-boundary bug as Anderson's (plus the case number's own trailing digits read as a fake house number) — fixed by searching only the region after the case number. address 25/34→34/34, parcel_id 0/34→34/34, plaintiff+defendant 0/34→30/34.
    - `law_firms.brock_scott` (`ea8b8229`): the site migrated off `article.foreclosure_search` card markup to a plain `<table>`, AND off `?sf_paged={N}` query pagination to WordPress's real `/page/{N}/` path pagination — the old query param still returns HTTP 200 every time but is now silently ignored (always page 1). `fetch()` had been returning **0 rows on every run**. Fixed (header-driven table parsing + real pagination); live `fetch()` went 0→251 scraped (192 NC + 59 SC, matching the page's own facet counts), 63 kept.
    - `law_firms.zacchaeus` (`dfc1e867`): re-audited the rendered grid's cell HTML and found the Address column links to a Google Maps URL with embedded exact coordinates (0/207 rows had lat/lng before; now first-class) and the Parcel # column links to the county's own GIS parcel page (0/207→124/124 captured as `raw["zls"]["parcel_gis_url"]`). Also confirmed and documented, not silently left broken: the "Notice of Sale" column the original build found as a link is now a Blazor `<button>` with no href — a genuine site change, not an extraction bug; reopening it needs a per-row click-and-intercept, out of scope for this pass.
    - `national.sheriff_sales` (`87b9cc28`): **confirmed-live false-positive bug**, the "silent success" failure mode — `_fetch_county`'s sub-page crawl checked its keyword list against the FULL href, but every page on `sheriffclevelandcounty.com` contains "sheriff" as a substring of its own hostname, so it matched all 60 links on the page including a LinkedIn share for an unrelated drug-arrest press release, the Facebook page, and concealed-carry forms. Fixed both regexes; also found and fixed a regression risk in the fix itself (a naive sentence-ending period stop would have truncated a real middle-initial name like "Sample T. Ownerperson" down to "Sample T." — handled with two named alternatives, direct-label mode with no period stop vs. sentence mode triggered only by an "is or are" lead-in). 40. **DONE (2026-10-02) — migrated `scripts/run_tax_owed_normalize.py` off `load_board()`/`write_artifact()`, the first of a 3-script mop-up after item 39's own flagged "running `backfill_derivation_flags.py` is explicitly out of scope on this machine."** Today a real production backfill attempt on a 23GB Oracle VM confirmed `run_tax_owed_normalize.py`'s real write genuinely kernel-OOM-killed (`anon-rss:24GB` at kill time, confirmed via `journalctl -k`). Root cause, precisely diagnosed, not assumed: `write_artifact()` builds `payload = [_to_dict(li) for li in listings]` — a SECOND full in-memory copy of the board on top of the `list[Listing]` `load_board()` already holds — and two full copies of this ~2.65GB board (each inflating to roughly 11-12GB as parsed Python objects) exceed even 23GB. Same failure class as items 22/23's `_dq_common.run_apply()`/`lrcpwa_refresh.py` fixes the day before.
    **Classification (read `enrichment_tax_owed.py` in full, not assumed).** It never reads a `LAZY_DETAIL_KEYS` field (`vision`/`foreclosure_sold_comps`/`comps`/`cama`/`rent_comps` — grepped the whole module; the one "vision"-ish hit was inside a comment example list, not a real read), so the slim `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` reader (no sidecar merge) is correct and sufficient — same choice `_dq_common._light_rows()` already made for its own 9 callers. But `enrich_tax_owed()` is NOT pure-per-row the way `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s board-wide recomputes were: it does `listings = list(listings)` then runs a genuine TWO-PASS, cross-row algorithm — pass 1 normalizes each tax lead's own amount into a `(state, county, parcel)` index, pass 2 cross-references that SAME index onto any OTHER lead resolved to the same parcel. A row visited in pass 2 may depend on an index entry a row visited LATE in pass 1 contributed, so there is no way to know a row is safe to finish-and-discard until the whole list has been seen once — the same "large population evaluated together, not independent per-row work" shape `_dq_common.run_apply()`'s docstring names for its own callers. So this follows THAT reference pattern (full materialization via `board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, no sidecar; `enrich_tax_owed()` itself completely UNCHANGED), not `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s bounded-target streaming: a pre-mutation snapshot of every row is taken before the function runs, diffed against its post-mutation state afterward, landed in ONE `web_artifact.patch_existing_rows()` call.
    **A genuine, non-obvious hazard found and disclosed (not papered over): `patch_existing_rows()`'s one-patch-per-`dedupe_key()` contract collides with this script's OWN cross-reference feature.** `enrich_tax_owed()`'s cross-reference mechanism exists SPECIFICALLY to find a second, separate board row sharing the same `(state, county, parcel_id)` as a tax lead's own record — and `Listing.dedupe_key()`'s strongest (parcel) branch is keyed on exactly those same three fields. So every time this cross-reference mechanism actually fires, the "own_record" row and the "parcel_cross_ref" row necessarily share one `dedupe_key()`, and `patch_existing_rows()` — by its own documented design ("the same identity is, by this codebase's own definition, the same property, so the same field update is correct for all of them") — applies ONE combined patch (whichever row's diff this script computed last, in board order) to BOTH matching rows. The balance always ends up correct on both (the enrichment only ever cross-references an identical balance/kind/source), but the `basis` label on whichever row's patch lost the last-write race can end up the "wrong" one of the two. This is a property of `patch_existing_rows()`'s identity-based write inherited by this migration, not introduced by it (the original `load_board()`/`write_artifact()` script mutated each `Listing` OBJECT directly, by position, and never had this interaction) — disclosed in the script's own docstring rather than silently inherited, surfaced to the operator at runtime via `patch_existing_rows()`'s own `duplicate_key_matches` counter (never silent), and proven in a dedicated test rather than assumed away. Fixing it generally is out of scope for a plumbing migration — same honesty standard as item 24's `distress_score.score_board()` exclusion.
    **Disclosed improvement.** The old "nothing to do" gate (`not stats["stamped"] and not stats["cross_referenced"]`) almost never actually fired on a healthy board, because `enrich_tax_owed()` re-stamps EVERY already-normalized tax row on every single run (idempotent values, but a nonzero `stamped` count regardless) — so the old script called `write_artifact()` (a whole-board rewrite) on every scheduled run even when nothing had changed. The new gate is the real diff: `patch_existing_rows()` already no-ops safely on an empty `patches` dict (returns `written: False` without touching the board), so an idempotent re-run now correctly writes nothing.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_run_tax_owed_normalize_patch_integration.py` (6 new, synthetic-fixture only) — own-record + cross-ref normalization, the `dedupe_key()` collision made visible via `duplicate_key_matches` and proven non-corrupting, an untouched row never reaching `patch_existing_rows()`, `--dry-run` writing nothing, second-run idempotency, and the too-large-to-patch ceiling firing before the full-list build. Pre-existing `tests/test_tax_owed.py` (16) + `tests/test_georgetown_civicengage_tax_owed.py` (3) pass unchanged. Broader scoped run (`tax_owed or derivation_flags or pending_signal or dq_ or lrcpwa or patch_existing or board_stream or web_artifact or required_raw_keys`): 368 passed. **Did not touch the live board on either host** — synthetic fixtures only, no `load_board()`/`write_artifact()` call anywhere in the new script. Commit: see `scripts/run_tax_owed_normalize.py` + `tests/test_run_tax_owed_normalize_patch_integration.py`.

No I/O," and reading `enrich_derivation_flags()` confirms it: a single `for li in listings` loop, no index built across rows, no `for other in listings` anywhere. (Its own docstring claims `divorce_flag` "cross-references any listing whose owner name matches a party in a divorce filing" — that claim is stale/aspirational: the actual `_divorce_flag()` body is two `if` branches on that ONE listing's own `source`/`listing_type`/`case_id`, nothing else.) Because the function is genuinely pure-per-row AND carries no state across separate calls (confirmed: `main.py` itself already calls it on small per-run batches, not always the whole board), this script does not need item 40's/`_dq_common.run_apply()`'s "materialize the whole board as `Listing` objects first" shape at all — it streams `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` one record at a time, calls `enrich_derivation_flags([li])` on a SINGLE-ROW list, diffs, and discards — never holding more than one row in memory at once. Tighter than both of this week's other reference patterns (item 40's/`_dq_common.run_apply()`'s full materialization and `lrcpwa_refresh.py`'s bounded-target streaming), because there is no bounded network-bound target list to hold onto here at all.

Verified safe the same way `_dq_common.py`'s `_RAW_DELETE_SAFE_AS_NONE` registry verifies its one entry (`'scope'`): grepped every reader of `"derivation_flags"` in `src/`+`scripts/`+`docs/dashboard.js` — the only other reader, `enrichment_property_category.py`, does `raw.get("derivation_flags") or {}`, which treats `None` exactly like an absent key. Kept as this script's OWN local allowlist rather than added to `_dq_common`'s shared `_RAW_DELETE_SAFE_AS_NONE` (that frozenset is scoped to the 7 already-migrated `_dq_common.run_apply()` callers; this script does not use `_dq_common` at all, by design — see below).
    **Tests**: `tests/test_backfill_derivation_flags_patch_integration.py` (6 new, synthetic-fixture only) — a newly-qualifying row getting flagged, the stale-flag deletion landing as `None` and reading back as absent, an untouched row never reaching `patch_existing_rows()`, `--dry-run` writing nothing, second-run idempotency, and the too-large-to-patch ceiling firing before the streaming pass runs. 44. **DONE (2026-10-02) — entity_type gap: owner entity type (individual/LLC/trust/estate/government) was computed on the fly by ~10 call sites and never persisted.** `grep is_entity(` across the codebase found 9 files / 11 call sites (`enrichment_repeat_tax_loss.py`, `sc_parcel_mailing.py`, `enrichment_sc_phone.py`, `deed_index.py`, `enrichment_sc_divorce.py`, `enrichment_resolve_name_to_property.py`, `enrichment_notice_service_defect.py` x2, `enrichment_owner_cluster.py`, `scripts/reset_divorce_wrong_order.py`, `scripts/reset_divorce_slow_rounds.py`), **every one importing and calling the SAME `name_normalize.is_entity()`** — no drift in the base check itself. Three of those nine files DO layer their own supplemental heuristic on top of it for their own domain (`enrichment_owner_cluster._NOT_A_PERSON`: builder/HOA/community names like "LENNAR HOMES"/"LINCOLN COUNTY" that `is_entity()` structurally cannot catch, confirmed by that file's own 2026-09-16 dry-run; `enrichment_sc_phone.owner_is_non_person()`: estate/government/club tokens; `enrichment_notice_service_defect._GENERIC_PARTY_RE`: anonymous "unknown heirs"/"John Doe" parties) — each documented in its own file as a deliberate ADDITION, never a disagreement with `is_entity()`'s own verdict.
    **Built**: `name_normalize.classify_entity_type()` (individual/entity/trust/estate/government/unknown — calls `is_entity()` internally, never reimplements it; adds estate/government detection mirroring the two supplements above rather than inventing a third word list) + `enrichment_entity_type.py`'s `enrich_entity_type()`, which stamps `raw['entity_type']` once per listing. Wired into `main.py` right after `enrich_court_owner_verify` — the LAST of several phases that mutate `owner_name` (`enrich_gis`, `enrich_gis_attrs`, `enrich_lrcpwa_parcel`, `enrich_promote_owner`, `enrich_court_owner_verify`, all read in `main.py` to confirm the order) — so the persisted value reflects the FINAL `owner_name` for the run. Added `"entity_type": "*"` to `web_artifact.RAW_KEEP`.
    **Consolidation, checked call-site by call-site, not forced**: only `enrichment_owner_cluster.py`'s `_cluster_key` was switched over (new `_is_entity_like()` helper: prefers `raw['entity_type']`, falls back to a fresh `is_entity()` compute when the field is absent, so the module's own standalone dry-run still works unchanged). The other 8 were deliberately left computing fresh, each for a real, checked reason: `sc_parcel_mailing.lookup_by_owner` and `enrichment_resolve_name_to_property`'s `_query_buncombe_owner_index` take an arbitrary candidate name parameter, not guaranteed to be the current listing's own `owner_name`; `deed_index._is_person` operates on a `Party` parsed from a recorded deed instrument, not a `Listing` — no `raw` dict exists in that domain at all; `enrichment_sc_divorce._yield_prior` and `enrichment_resolve_name_to_property`'s other call site both run in `main.py` EARLIER than `enrich_court_owner_verify`, so a persisted field at their point in the pipeline would either not exist yet or reflect a stale `owner_name` — a genuine timing dependency, confirmed by reading the actual phase order, not assumed; `enrichment_notice_service_defect.py`'s two sites check `li.defendant or li.owner_name` / `li.owner_name or li.defendant`, a different field combination than plain `owner_name`; `enrichment_repeat_tax_loss._name_key` is a shared private helper that ALSO indexes grantor names from `deed_chain`/`deed_index` rows living on no `Listing.raw` at all, so splitting out its one pure-`owner_name` call path wasn't worth the risk; `scripts/reset_divorce_wrong_order.py`/`scripts/reset_divorce_slow_rounds.py` are one-off maintenance scripts that reprocess ALREADY-PUBLISHED board rows, which do not carry `entity_type` (backfilling existing rows is explicitly out of scope for this item — see below) and would be silently miscategorized by a bare field read.

45. **DONE (2026-10-02) — divorce-caption gap: `raw['divorce']['cases'][].parties` stored both spouses as ONE combined caption string ("JIMMIE LEE GLENN vs. JAMES L GLENN"), never as separate fields.** Checked the "direct `DIVORCE_NOTICE` listings" too, per the task brief: `nc_ecourts_divorce.py`, `nc_ecourts_lis_pendens.py`, and `ncpublicnotices.py` all ALREADY populate separate `Listing.plaintiff`/`Listing.defendant` fields directly (confirmed by code read + a live board sample) — so the real, only gap is the `raw['divorce']` cross-reference enrichment's `cases[].parties` string, built by `enrichment_sc_divorce.py` (live, default-ON) and `enrichment_nc_divorce.py` (code path exists but gated off by default, `FORECLOSURE_NC_DIVORCE=0`, AWS-WAF-CAPTCHA-blocked per its own docstring — has never produced a live row).
    **Real caption format, confirmed live** (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, read-only): 5,052 SC `raw['divorce']` rows / 14,741 case `parties` strings currently on the board, 100% from SC (zero from NC's gated-off path). ALL 14,741 use `vs.`/`vs` (case-insensitive, period optional) as the separator — zero use a bare `v.` or carry no separator at all. Both ALL-CAPS ("JIMMIE LEE GLENN") and Title Case ("Irina Popov vs. Zhenia Popov") appear live. 741/14,741 (~5%) carry an `, et al.` on one or both sides (e.g. "TERESA COLLINS STILWELL, et al. vs. WILLIAM PATRICK STILWELL, et al.") or a bare ` AND ` folding a second name onto one side with no further delimiter (a real caption: "WALTER L NIX vs. PEARL T AND ROGER NIX" — two defendants sharing the NIX surname in one slot). **Caption order (Plaintiff vs. Defendant, filer first) verified against the real data, not assumed**: cross-checked each case's own `role` field (SC's `ParticipantRole`, independent of this parser) against caption position by name-token overlap — 9,887/9,923 (99.6%) agreed first-named = Plaintiff / second-named = Defendant; all 36 disagreements were short/bare-surname `owner_name` rows ("JONES", "SMITH") matched to an unnamed `et al.` participant never printed in the caption at all, not a wrong split.
    **Built** `divorce_caption.split_divorce_caption()` (pure function, dependency-light like `name_normalize.py`): returns `{plaintiff, defendant, additional_parties}` or `None` (defensive — never happened on the live sample). Handles `vs.`/`vs`/`v.` separators (the last one only with its period, so it is never confused with this board's own unpunctuated middle-initial convention — `MARY V SMITH` must not split on the bare `V`), strips a trailing `et al.` (flagging `additional_parties`), detects a bare `AND` join (flagging it, keeping the side's full text rather than guessing it apart further), and leaves a real generational suffix like `, Sr` untouched. Wired into both `enrichment_sc_divorce.py`'s `_parse_rows()` and `enrichment_nc_divorce.py`'s `_parse_divorce_cases()`, adding `plaintiff`/`defendant`/`additional_parties` onto each case dict alongside the original (unremoved) `parties` string. No `RAW_KEEP` change needed — `"divorce": "*"` already keeps the whole value verbatim.
    **Did NOT** try to determine which spouse keeps the property: the signal-depth audit confirmed that is genuinely absent from every source feeding this board, and the task brief explicitly said not to force a guess at it — not attempted.
    **Live-verified at full scale**: ran `split_divorce_caption()` against all 14,741 real captions on the board — 0 failures, 741 correctly flagged `additional_parties` (matching the et al./AND count above exactly).
    **Tests**: `tests/test_divorce_caption.py` (new, 16 cases, real live fixtures), `tests/test_nc_divorce_parse.py` (new, 2 cases — synthetic, since the NC path has never produced a live row; built from the module's own documented text-cut shape), `tests/test_sc_divorce_search.py` (+2 functions / 5 cases, asserting the split lands correctly on `_parse_rows()`'s real output dict).

McCracken"), but its auction list never tags HOA at all (only the UncontestedRoster hearing roster's "HOA FORECLOSURES" section does), the 18-county flip-footprint scope + small weekly volume + a dedupe rule that dropped the HOA tag on collision all suppress the literal count further, and — the higher-value point — NC (G.S. 47F-3-116) and SC HOA lien foreclosures ride the SAME public notice process as a bank foreclosure, so real HOA leads are very likely already on the board captioned as generic `foreclosure_sale`/`lis_pendens`. Confirmed live before building anything (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, read-only, never `load_board()`): **0 rows board-wide carry `listing_type=hoa_sale`**; 14,009 `foreclosure_sale`/`lis_pendens` rows exist, 4,491 have usable plaintiff text.
    **Built the shared classifier in `enrichment_title_risk.py`** (`classify_hoa_plaintiff(party: str) -> {"is_hoa": bool, "matched": str|None}`) rather than a 4th duplicate HOA regex — that file already had the most battle-tested bank-vs-HOA guard in the repo (`_classify()`'s "SENIOR wins ties" rule, there specifically because real data has "U.S. Bank Trust National Association" sitting next to "Fernbrook III Homeowners Association"). Split its `_JUNIOR_PATTERNS` HOA subset into a new `_HOA_PATTERNS` tuple and reused the existing `_SENIOR_PATTERNS`/`_normalize_party`/`_party_text` rather than re-deriving any of them. Live false negatives: "South Wind Villas Homeowner's Association", "Maxwell Commons Home Owner's Association, Inc." — both silently fell through. Fixed with a shared `_OWNER_POSS = r"owner(?:s'|'s|s)?"` building block used in all three owner-association patterns.
      - **Bare `\bp\.?o\.?a\.?\b` acronym was a 100% false positive on the live board**: its only match across all 14,009 target rows was "Angela Maria Rogers Individually And As Poa For Shirley Stewart Burges" — POA = Power of Attorney there, not Property Owners Association. Every real POA-type HOA entity on the board spells "Property Owners Association" out in full, so the bare-acronym branch was removed outright (zero true-positive cost, confirmed by re-running against the live board before/after).

A non-HOA roster dup is still silently deduped with no patch (regression-tested both ways).

47. **CONFIRMED + FIXED (2026-10-02) — `builder_distress` (liensnc) was granting a full, uncapped FINANCIAL-category credit regardless of source, the exact "stack of two" fabricated-signal bug already fixed twice today for OTHER sources (`zombie_properties.py`, `greenville_hard_distress.py`, both via `distress_score._CONTEXT_ONLY_DISTRESSED_SOURCES`) — just via a SECOND, previously-unguarded code path for the SAME already-known-bad source.** A separate validation session ran real population-scale checks (n=50/846 Buncombe liensnc rows, 176 Register-of-Deeds instruments pulled for those 50 properties) and found 0% genuine mechanic's/contractor's liens anywhere — confirming at full population scale what project memory already established ("liensnc re-parse + lead class": the 56K-row liensnc pool is mostly lien-AGENT filings, e.g. a title company/agent recording a Notice to Lien Agent for a construction lender, not real distress liens).
    **Traced exactly how it reaches scoring** (grep `builder_distress` across `src/foreclosure_scraper/`): `scripts/ingest_liensnc.py`/`scripts/backfill_builder_distress.py` are the ONLY two places that ever set `raw['builder_distress']`, and both do it exclusively on liensnc rows (address-cluster or "Active Related Filings? = Yes"). `distress_score.py`'s own `_is_liensnc()` already keeps liensnc's base listing-type signal (`lt == "distressed"`/`tax_lien`) from scoring at all (the A8 fix, already correct — confirmed by reading `_collect()`'s `skip_type` gate directly, not assumed). But `enrichment_lead_signals.py`'s `_facet_signals()` reads `raw.get("builder_distress")` directly with no source check at all, adding `"builder_distress"` (mapped to `SIGNAL_CATEGORY["builder_distress"] = "FINANCIAL"`) to `raw['signal_stack']` and up to 10 of `_intent_score()`'s 30 stack points — the second path `_CONTEXT_ONLY_DISTRESSED_SOURCES` was never extended to cover, because that set only gates the listing-type pathway `_is_liensnc()` already fully blocks for this source.
    **Scale, measured safely** (the board is a single 2.53 GB unbroken-newline JSON array; a full `load_board()` was correctly avoided per the 8GB-Mac rule — used `grep -c -o` instead, which mmap-scans without buffering the whole file): **20,306 published rows** carry `raw['builder_distress']`.
    **Fix**: `enrichment_lead_signals.py` now imports `_is_liensnc` from `distress_score` and gates the facet exactly like the base signal already is: `if _truthy(raw.get("builder_distress")) and not _is_liensnc(li): out.add(...)`. Source-scoped (mirrors `_is_liensnc`'s own pattern), not a blanket ban on the signal name, so a future non-liensnc source with real evidence would still count.
    **Tests**: 3 new in `tests/test_enrichment_lead_signals.py` (discount for a liensnc source; still counts for a non-liensnc source; a liensnc row with no other evidence reads `signal_stack.count == 0`/no FINANCIAL category/`intent_score == 0`). Targeted run: `test_enrichment_lead_signals.py` (22 passed), `test_patch_distress_score_integration.py` + `test_context_only_distressed.py` (40 passed) — all green.

The 20,306 rows keep their currently-published `signal_stack`/`intent_score` until the board is next regenerated or a scoped `patch_existing_rows()` backfill is run (same pattern as item 20's `rescore_greenville_distress_correction.py`) — this item only stops the leak going forward.

48. **CONFIRMED + FIXED (2026-10-02) — Henderson County code-enforcement (`counties_nc.henderson_code_violations`) granted full PROPERTY credit for ANY open case regardless of what the case was actually about, so a pure zoning complaint scored identically to an open junkyard/condemnation case.** The same validation session sampled 60/238 Henderson code-enforcement leads against the county's own ArcGIS dashboard feed and found only 46.9% genuinely vacancy-adjacent — the majority (53.1%) were a different case category entirely, mostly zoning.
    **Live-verified the real category taxonomy before touching any filter logic** (queried the live feed directly, `OVT_PublicDashboard_View/FeatureServer/1`, both the full coded-value domain and a `groupByFieldsForStatistics` count of the `_OPEN_WHERE`-filtered rows the scraper actually pulls): the feed's `violationType` coded domain is Junkyard, Manufactured Home Graveyard, Nuisance, Solid Waste, Vehicle Graveyard, Zoning, Minimum Housing Complaint, plus free-text "General"/blank/uncoded rows. Live open-case breakdown that day: Nuisance 93, Solid Waste 32, Zoning 39, Junkyard 7, Vehicle Graveyard 5, General 3, Manufactured Home Graveyard 2, Minimum Housing 1 (182 total open cases). The scraper already READS `violationType` and already computes a `severe` flag off it (the existing `_SEVERE` regex: Junkyard/Vehicle Graveyard/Manufactured Home Graveyard/Solid Waste/Minimum Housing/Nuisance match, Zoning/General do not) — but `severe` was wired ONLY to the extra `raw['distressed']` flag, never to the `code_enforcement` PROPERTY signal itself, so `has_open: True` fired unconditionally and `signal_freshness.code_enforcement_open()` (read by both `distress_score.py`'s weighted PROPERTY=14 signal and `enrichment_lead_signals.py`'s facet stack) granted full credit regardless of category.
    **Fix**: added an explicit, optional `vacancy_adjacent` key to `signal_freshness.code_enforcement_open()` — `vacancy_adjacent is False` makes it return False while `has_open` stays truthful/unchanged (so the case still ships and is still visible; it just adds no PROPERTY credit). `henderson_code_violations.py`'s `build_listing()` now sets `"vacancy_adjacent": severe` next to `"has_open": True`. Nothing else changed `has_open`'s meaning, and the key is absent-by-default everywhere else, so no other source's behavior moved (confirmed by running `test_gastonia_code_enforcement.py`/`test_hendersonville_vacant_structures.py` unchanged alongside).
    **Live impact, measured for real** (`RUN_LIVE=1`, the actual `fetch()` against the live layer, not a fixture): of 173 current open-case properties, **40 (23.1%) are now correctly excluded from PROPERTY credit** because every one of their open cases is Zoning/General only.
    **Tests**: 3 new in `tests/test_henderson_code_violations.py` (the fixture's existing "General"-only parcel ships but doesn't score; a synthetic Zoning-only case doesn't score; a mixed Zoning+Nuisance property still scores on the real evidence) plus a strengthened `test_live` assertion that the live feed produces at least one non-vacancy-adjacent row and that it correctly carries no `code_enforcement` signal. 36 passed, 1 (RUN_LIVE) separately run and passing. Full targeted run across both Henderson files + Gastonia + lead-signals + the distress-score integration/context-only suites: 176 passed, 2 skipped.
    **Spotted but NOT fixed here (flagged, out of scope):** `counties_nc.gastonia_code_enforcement.py` has the identical shape (computes `severe` off `ApplicationTypeDescription`, wires it only to `distressed`, never to `has_open`) — spun off as a separate background task rather than fixed inline, since Gastonia's own category taxonomy has not been live-verified.

49. **INVESTIGATED (2026-10-02) — Hendersonville NC vacant-structures register (`counties_nc.hendersonville_vacant_structures`): staleness confirmed, but NOT a code bug and NO code fix applied — this needs an operational re-verification cadence, not a one-time change.** The same validation session sampled 11 rows from this register and found only 27.3% still genuinely vacant today; the task was to determine whether the source provides any freshness signal (a last-inspected date, a status field) to wire the way `signal_freshness.stamp`/`is_stale` already does for code-enforcement blocks (the F12 pattern, and the same pattern item 48 above did NOT need here because the real problem is different).
    **Live-verified why a date-based fix would not work** (queried the real layer directly, not assumed from the schema): `returnCountOnly` confirms the WHOLE layer is 52 rows; the only date-like field, `DATE`, is populated on 42/52 (80.8%) and blank on the rest; the newest populated value anywhere on the layer is **2024-06-18 — already 836 days (~2.3 years) old as of today**. The service's own metadata carries no `editFieldsInfo` (ArcGIS Online's automatic last-edited tracking is off), and the layer's own name — `VACANT_STRUCTURES_7_24_24` — is literally its one-time publish date, July 24 2024: this is a frozen snapshot the city published once, not a feed it keeps current. So `DATE` reads as "when the officer wrote the case up," not "last confirmed still vacant."
    **Why a TTL still does not fit, reasoned through rather than assumed**: with every dated row already ~2+ years old, ANY TTL cutoff short enough to matter (12-24 months) would zero out essentially the entire register — but the validation found 27.3% are STILL actually vacant despite the age, and there is no per-row field that tells us WHICH ones. A blanket age cutoff would discard real leads alongside stale ones with no way to discriminate, unlike item 48's Henderson fix where the source hands us a field (`violationType`) that genuinely distinguishes the two cases. This is the scoped-correctly "operational re-verification cadence" branch named in the task brief, not the "wire the freshness signal" branch.
    **No code change applied.** Documented the live finding directly in the module's own docstring (`hendersonville_vacant_structures.py`) so a future session does not re-derive this from scratch, and added `tests/test_hendersonville_vacant_structures.py::test_live_register_has_no_per_row_recency_signal_2026_10_02` (RUN_LIVE-gated) as a tripwire: it asserts the newest live `DATE` is still >540 days old, and will start FAILING on its own if the city ever republishes this layer with materially newer dates — at which point the "operational, not code" conclusion here should be revisited. 60 passed, 2 skipped (both RUN_LIVE) offline; both RUN_LIVE tests (existing `test_live` + the new tripwire) separately run and passing.
    **Recommended operational fix** (not code, flagging for whoever owns re-verification cadence): periodically (quarterly, say) re-pull this layer and spot-check a sample of its addresses against a current source (county GIS/CAMA occupancy signal, Street View, a drive-by) rather than trusting the register's vacancy claim at face value indefinitely; or watch for the city to publish a newer-dated replacement layer (the `_7_24_24` suffix convention suggests they might, next time with a different date in the name).

50. **ACCURACY FIX (2026-10-02) — SC divorce: 0% real matches confirmed at full population scale (n=58/5,052), fixed. Distinct from THIS SAME DAY's earlier item (`21b577b3`, divorce-CAPTION-structure split) — that fix never touched whether the underlying name-to-case match was correct in the first place; this one does.** A separate validation session ran live checks against SC's own court index and found zero confirmed real divorce cases for 57/58 sampled leads (98.3% genuine negatives, 1 inconclusive on a 250-row display cap) — the full 5,052-row population every SC-core-county divorce hit the board carries, not a sample of convenience. Their own write-up explicitly flagged two live possibilities and told the next session to check which one it actually was before fixing anything: a too-loose match, or a wrong case-type/wrong-index problem upstream of the matching code entirely.
    **Ruled out the wrong-index hypothesis with direct live evidence, not an assumption.** Re-pulled FCCMS's own LOCATION and CASECATEGORY validation-code lists live 2026-10-02: all 8 of `_COUNTY_CODE`'s codeIDs (1046=Spartanburg, 1008=Anderson, etc.) and all 3 of `_DIVORCE_CATEGORIES`'s codeIDs (1062="110 - Divorce", 1064="130 - Separate Support and Maintenance", 1067="199 - Marital Dissolution - Other") still resolve to exactly what this module's comments claim. Every live-probed PublicPersonSearch response also self-reports the correct `CaseCategory` on each row. The index and the case-type filter are both correct and current — the bug is entirely in the matching/corroboration logic, confirmed, not merely assumed because it was the easier answer.
    **Root cause #1 (structural, found by live-probing real searches, not in the task brief): `ParticipantRole` was parsed and stored but never used to filter anything.** `_owner_in_case()`'s namesake trim checks a blob of the case caption PLUS the row's own `PersonName` field — and `PersonName` by construction ALWAYS contains the searched last/first name (it is literally who matched the query), so the trim was a complete no-op for a non-party row. Live-verified: searching the common surname "Davis" in Cherokee county returns `"PersonName": "Davis, Davis, Joy C C"` / `"ParticipantRole": "Attorney"` for ~30 real but UNRELATED divorce cases — a real SC family-law attorney with many clients — none of whose case captions mention "Davis" at all, and the old code matched every one. Board-wide, 2,293 of 14,741 existing case rows (15.6%) carry a non-party role (`Attorney` 2,293, `Guardian Ad Litem` 7, `GAL-Children` 3, `Pro Hac Vice` 2, `Third Party Defendant` 1); a corroborating no-network check the validation session ran independently (re-checking the stored caption text alone, since `PersonName` is never persisted) flagged 177/5,052 (3.5%) as locally inconsistent, including explicitly naming an attorney-only match as one cause — an undercount of the live mechanism's true reach, since a lead with ANY genuine party case alongside an attorney-echo case still passes a lead-level check.
    **Root cause #2 (the loose-match problem the task brief anticipated, confirmed real and independently severe): a positive middle-initial agreement was computed but never enforced.** `name_normalize.party_middle_verdict()` has been computed for every SC-divorce hit since 2026-09-21 (41% of comparable hits were proven conflicts, same audit) but was only ever stored as advisory metadata — the scorer skipped a PROVEN conflict (21% of hits) but the dominant `unverified` bucket (46% of hits — no middle initial on one side, so nothing corroborates the match either way) passed straight through to `distress_stack.categories` uncorroborated. The single highest-confidence pre-fix match on the whole board (an exact full name with 32 real FCCMS records under it) had none that could be confirmed as the actual property owner — exactly the ambiguity this bucket represents, not a wrong-index artifact.
    **Fix.** `enrichment_sc_divorce.py`: `_is_party_role()` rejects a non-party `ParticipantRole` before a row ever reaches `_owner_in_case()` or `raw['divorce']['cases']` (reuses `distress_score._DIVORCE_PARTY_ROLES` rather than inventing a second role vocabulary). `_apply()` now requires `party_middle_verdict(li.owner_name, ...) == "agrees"` before adding `'divorce'` to `distress_stack.categories` — `raw['divorce']['cases']` (role-filtered) and `['match']` are still written for EVERY hit regardless of verdict, never silent; only the actionable category is gated. `distress_score._divorce_signal` tightened the same way (was: not a proven conflict; now: a proven agreement), replacing its `party_middle_conflict` import with `party_middle_verdict`.
    **Measured impact on the EXISTING board, read-only, not applied** (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, no `load_board()`, no write): of the 5,052 existing hits, 40 become case-less under the role filter alone (their only case was a non-party echo); of the remaining 5,012, applying the verdict gate leaves only 1,665 (`agrees`) — **3,387/5,052 (67.0%) would now be rejected, 1,665 (33.0%) would remain flagged.** Backfilling the already-published rows is explicitly out of scope here per the task brief.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_sc_divorce_party_role_and_middle_match.py` (new, 7 cases: an attorney-echo and a guardian-ad-litem row rejected even though `PersonName` matches; a real party row kept; a missing role conservatively kept; a common name with no middle corroboration recorded but not categorized; a proven conflict not categorized; a real agreeing match categorized). `tests/test_divorce_signal_score.py` updated (16 cases: every fixture now carries a `parties` caption that genuinely agrees with its `owner_name` so the recency/role logic is tested in isolation from the new gate; the `'unverified'`-is-no-longer-scored behavior change is asserted directly with an explanatory comment). `tests/test_sc_divorce_search.py`'s `_case_row()` fixture role corrected from an invented `"Petitioner"` (never observed live) to the live-confirmed `"Plaintiff"`.
    **Compliance**: code-only, no board write on either host. Live verification used only free, public, read-only FCCMS party-name searches (the same compliant flow this module already used) — no CAPTCHA/login/paywall touched. **Concurrent-session hygiene**: a separate validation session's own raw output lives at `docs/validation_2026-10-02/` (untracked) — read for grounding, never modified; `git status` throughout also showed unrelated concurrent edits to `enrichment_courts.py`, the `sc_public_index*` scrapers, `uv.lock`, `enrichment_gis_attrs.py`, `parcel_cache.py`, `scripts/recover_tax_year_from_checkpoint.py`, `docs/SOURCE_EXTRACTION_AUDIT.md` and their tests — none of those were read or touched; this item's commit stages only the files it actually changed, by name.

51. **ACCURACY FIX (2026-10-02) — bankruptcy debtor-name matching: 234-row full census found only 56% strong real matches (38.5% weak/likely-wrong-person, 5.6% a confirmed different real person), fixed with a DIFFERENT bar than item 50's divorce fix despite sharing the same tool — reasoned through, not copy-pasted.** Same validation session, `scripts/validate_bankruptcy_namematch.py` / `results/bankruptcy_namematch_results.json`: FULL CENSUS of the 234 board rows carrying both an `owner_name` and a bankruptcy `case_name` (of 5,750 total flagged; the other ~5,516 are missing one of those two fields). Example cited: property owner "Jones Ray" (and the raw data's own "Robert A Jones") matched to real debtor "Robert Curtis Best and Shannon Marie Jones" — neither real debtor is named Robert Jones.
    **Root cause, confirmed by re-deriving concrete (defendant, case_name) pairs straight off the live board, not just the 234-row sample:** `enrichment_bankruptcy.py`'s "STRICT subset" check tokenized both names into unordered bags (≥3-letter, non-stopword words) and required only that every token of the shorter (defendant) name appear SOMEWHERE in the case_name's token set — no position, and for a joint ("X and Y") filing, no separation between the two real debtors. Two distinct, both-reproduced-live failure modes: **position-blind** ("DAVIS, DAVID" matched "Bryan David Davis" — David is Bryan Davis's MIDDLE name, not his first name; "LEWIS WILLIAM" matched "Lewis William Hedgepeth" — Lewis is Hedgepeth's FIRST name, not his surname) and **joint-filer composite phantom**, the more severe mode (a joint filing's two debtors pooled into one token set, so tokens from two UNRELATED real people combine into a phantom third name matching neither: "JONES, ROBERT" / "Robert Curtis Best and Shannon Marie Jones" — the Jones/Robert example above). A THIRD bug found independently of the 234-row sample, while building the fix: `by_token` pools every listing (NC+SC) into one global index and the per-court loop never checked a candidate's own `state` against the filing court's state — measured directly against the 617 bankruptcy matches already on the board: **315/617 (51%) already have a court/listing state mismatch.**
    **Fix, deliberately NOT the same bar as item 50's divorce fix.** `name_normalize.debtor_positional_match()` (new) splits a case_name into individual one-person segments on a bare `and` and requires a POSITIONAL first==first/last==last match on ONE of them — a hard, unconditional requirement that alone rejects every concrete false positive above, independent of whether either side even carries a middle name. `name_normalize.debtor_middle_verdict()` (new, `party_middle_verdict`'s sibling, same split-then-positional core, generalized separator) additionally rejects a PROVEN middle-initial conflict. `_COURT_STATE` enforces the court-vs-listing-state check. **Deliberately does NOT also require `debtor_middle_verdict(...) == "agrees"`** the way item 50's divorce fix does: bankruptcy's `defendant` field is sourced from tax rolls/GIS and routinely carries no middle name at all even on a correct match, and the 234-row census itself found 56% were ALREADY strong, correct matches — requiring a positive agreement outright would have discarded most of the good matches alongside the bad ones (measured: it would reject 94% of existing matches vs. 85.9% under the positional-plus-not-conflicting bar actually shipped). `debtor_positional_match`'s own docstring spells out why the two signals warrant a different bar despite sharing the same underlying tool — this is the task's "do not force the pattern onto a problem it doesn't fit" instruction, applied for real rather than asserted.
    **Measured impact on the EXISTING board, read-only, not applied**: of the 617 existing bankruptcy matches, applying the new gate: 315 fail the state check, 165 more have no real positional candidate at all, 50 more are a proven middle-initial conflict — **530/617 (85.9%) would now be rejected, 87/617 (14.1%) would remain matched** (37 with `agrees`, 50 with `unverified` but a real positional candidate). Backfilling already-published rows is explicitly out of scope here.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_bankruptcy_debtor_middle_match.py` (new, 19 cases): direct unit coverage of both new `name_normalize` functions (the composite phantom, a position-blind match, a proven conflict, a real single-filer agreement, the correct half of a joint filing, no-middle-on-either-side) plus end-to-end `enrich_with_bankruptcy()` coverage (every live false positive above reproduced and rejected; the cross-state leak rejected even with an otherwise-perfect name match; a real single-filer match accepted; the correct debtor in a joint filing accepted; and — the deliberate-asymmetry test — a real positional match with NO middle corroboration on either side still accepted, unlike divorce). Pre-existing `tests/test_bankruptcy_case_age.py` (18 cases) and `tests/test_bankruptcy_tax_combo.py` pass unchanged (their fixtures already carry full matching names).
    **Compliance**: code-only, no board write on either host. **Concurrent-session hygiene**: same note as item 50 — `docs/validation_2026-10-02/` read for grounding, never modified; unrelated concurrent edits elsewhere in the repo not touched; this item's commit stages only the files it actually changed, by name.

52. **CONFIRMED + FIXED (2026-10-02) — Buncombe NC tax-delinquency candidates (this project's highest-volume tax signal, ~1,176 live candidates / 829 already on the board): TWO distinct, confirmed mechanical bugs, neither the "bare-presence check" the validation session's own writeup guessed it might be.** Same validation session as items 48-51 (`docs/validation_2026-10-02/FINDINGS.md` section 1, `results/tax_lien_validation_results.json`, N=60 of 1,176, 59 fetched live): (A) only 11.9% of candidates carry ANY real unpaid prior-year balance, 11.9% genuine 2+-year delinquency (same figure — all 7 multi-year rows are also the only 7 with any prior-year balance); (B) of 41 owner-name-matched rows (ruling out a wrong-parcel confound), only 2.4% land within 10% of the real assessed value, 39/41 overstated at median **1.78x**; (C) 22.6% of a 53-row sample show a different current owner than the board lists. Independently re-derived from the SAME raw JSON before touching any code: 11.9%/11.9% (A, exact match), median ratio 1.766x overall / **1.780x** restricted to the 49 genuinely-overstated rows (B, exact match to the validator's own number), 25.4% owner mismatch on a stricter token-overlap re-check of the same 59 rows (C, consistent).
    **Bug 1 (Finding A) root cause — NOT a bare-presence check. Every current reader that marks a row tax-delinquent-positive was individually audited and is already correct**, same sub-field-gating discipline as yesterday's probate fix: `distress_score.py:716-717` gates on `isinstance(raw['tax_owed'], dict) and raw['tax_owed']['balance'] > 0`; `enrichment_lead_signals.py:100` and `enrichment_tax_owed.py`'s `_money()` (`v > 0` required) are the same. The REAL bug is in a one-off recovery tool, `scripts/recover_tax_year_from_checkpoint.py`, which backfills `raw['tax_owed']` from a prior run's checkpoint onto board rows missing a year. Its `_keys()` built per-record identity keys in the order source_url, parcel_id, address, case_number — but every row scraped from ONE bulk document shares an IDENTICAL source_url (every `counties_nc.buncombe_delinquent_tax` row carries the single advertisement `PDF_URL`), so `("u", source_url)` is the LEAST specific key for a bulk source, not the most — yet it was tried first, and `idx.setdefault(k, payload)` kept only the first donor ever indexed under it. Every board row needing a year therefore matched the SAME donor's "u" entry and inherited THAT donor's `principal_tax_due`/`tax_owed`, regardless of its own parcel.
    **Live-confirmed, not guessed:** 610 of 829 Buncombe tax-delinquency board rows (73.6%) carry the IDENTICAL `principal_tax_due = $775.59` — the real value of exactly ONE parcel (PIN 961895417300000, owner "125 PISGAH VIEW LLC", per the retained checkpoint `data/checkpoint/board.json.gz`, which itself carries 791 duplicate copies of that one donor record under the same shared-URL signature). Re-fetched the LIVE current advertisement PDF (`buncombe_delinquent_tax.py`'s own `parse_advertisement()`, today's 1,181-row publication) for all 610 affected parcels: 609/610 show a different, real, live-correct amount today (none equal $775.59 except one coincidental real match) — e.g. owner "CONNER, RICKEY" (PIN 9608108745) shows board `principal_tax_due=$775.59` vs. live `$171.06`. This single mis-keyed field explains most of Finding A's 88.1% false-positive rate directly: a parcel whose recorded "balance" is a random stranger's figure obviously won't match a live lookup for that specific dollar amount.
    **Fix:** `_keys()` reordered so parcel_id/address/case_number are tried before the coarse source_url, and a new `_build_index()` adds an independent second guard — any identity key that maps to more than one DISTINCT donor payload is dropped entirely rather than resolved by first-insert-wins, so a future coincidental collision on any other bulk source (several of `TAX_SUBDICTS`'s 10 sources are single-URL-per-county-per-run) fails safe instead of silently mis-attributing debt. This is a one-off tool, not the live pipeline/cron, so the fix only prevents recurrence on its next invocation — it does NOT retroactively correct the 610 already-corrupted rows (backfill explicitly out of scope here, see below).
    **Bug 2 (Finding B) root cause — a stale local parcel-cache snapshot, NOT a land+improvement double-count or a unit-conversion error (both checked live and ruled out).** Buncombe's live GIS fields (`gis.buncombecounty.org/.../property_bc_dis/MapServer/1`) were queried directly: for every parcel checked, `TotalMarketValue == AppraisedValue == TaxValue` exactly and `LandValue + BuildingValue` sums correctly to the total — no double-count, no unit mismatch, and no `enrichment_arcgis.FIELD_ALIASES` entry happens to match Buncombe's real field names at all (so that path contributes nothing here, correct or otherwise). The actual mechanism: `enrichment_arcgis.enrich()` and `enrichment_gis_attrs.enrich_gis_attrs()` each have a "parcel cache fast path" (`parcel_cache.lookup()`, a local SQLite join from the weekly `scripts/refresh_parcel_cache.py`) that fills `li.market_value`/`li.tax_value` from the cached snapshot with NO freshness check — unlike every live per-parcel GIS query in the same functions, which always reads the county's current data.
    **Live-confirmed on two different real rows, matched by parcel_id, cross-checked against BOTH the validator's own authoritative source (`tax.buncombenc.gov/Parcel/Details/{pin}`) and the live ArcGIS layer, which agree with each other exactly:** (1) PIN 9608108745 (owner Conner): cache (2026-09-27 refresh) `market_value=269400`/`tax_value=72600`; live today ArcGIS's `TotalMarketValue=AppraisedValue=TaxValue=130,600` (all three agree with each other — a 1.80x overstatement riding straight from the stale `market_value`). (2) PIN 9654886969 (validation row, board owner "Brandon Bryant", real owner "SUSAN STANFILL, IAN HUGHES"): board's published value (189,200) is an EXACT match to the cache's `market_value` column (189200.0, confirmed by direct SQLite query) — not a coincidence — while the cache's own `tax_value` column for this same row (101,600) sits close to but not identical to today's live/authoritative value ($100,400). `refresh_parcel_cache.py` does a full, completeness-gated rebuild every run (read, not assumed) — not a broken-refresh bug — so the county's own published figures are genuinely moving between refreshes (an appeal, a correction, a per-parcel reassessment), not a cache going silently stale from disuse. A broader 18-parcel random (non-tax-delinquency) spot-check found ratios ranging 0.35x-7.48x in BOTH directions, confirming this is real, ongoing, per-parcel value churn, not a clean universal multiplier — the validator's tight 1.78x is specific to the tax-delinquency cohort they sampled, not a project-wide constant.
    **Fix, scoped generically — this is NOT Buncombe-specific, it is the shared parcel-cache mechanism used by every one of the ~130 counties in `data/parcel_cache/`:** `parcel_cache.py` gains `CACHE_VALUE_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (10 days: the 7-day refresh cadence + slack for one missed run), `cache_age_days()`, `cache_is_stale()`. `lookup_with_tier()` (and therefore `lookup()`, used by both live enrichers above plus 6 one-off scripts: `join_parcel_cache_to_board.py`, `resolve_names_from_parcel_cache.py`, `repair_parcel_from_address.py`, `fill_address_from_parcel.py`, `map_account_ids_to_parcels.py`, `backfill_missing_county.py`) now OMITS `market_value`/`tax_value` from its returned dict once a county's cache is older than that bound — every current caller already treats these as optional (`pc.get("market_value")`), so an absent key is a safe, backward-compatible no-op for all of them. `enrichment_gis_attrs.py`'s cache-hit early-return (previously fired on `street_address` alone) now also falls through to its own live, correctly-aliased query (`MARKET_FIELDS`/`ASSESSED_FIELDS` DO include Buncombe's real `TotalMarketValue`/`AppraisedValue` names) when value was withheld for staleness AND the lead still lacks one — so that lead gets the CURRENT county figure instead of silently staying empty or (as before) wrong forever. Does not force an extra live call for a lead whose value is already populated from elsewhere (tested explicitly).
    **Finding C (owner mismatch) — investigated, independently reproduced, NOT fixed: real ownership turnover, not a parcel-matching defect.** Both identity lookups in every mismatched row are keyed by the SAME `parcel_id`, so this cannot be a wrong-parcel confound by construction of the validation itself. Worked example (PIN 9654886969): the parcel_cache's OWN Sep-27 snapshot already carries the CURRENT real owner ("STANFILL SUSAN;HUGHES IAN", matching the validator's live `real_owner` exactly) — yet the board's published `owner_name` is still "Brandon Bryant," an older owner baked into the Listing at whatever earlier date the `buncombe_unpaid_bills` scraper first captured this row. Virtually every owner-filling path in this codebase is "missing-only" (`if not li.owner_name: ...`) by design — the right policy for not clobbering good data with bad, but with the side effect that a field, once set, never gets REFRESHED even when a later, more-current source (the same cache refresh that already has the right answer) runs right next to it. This is a real, structural finding, not a quick fix — flagging per instructions rather than forcing one; a dedicated owner-freshness pass (re-stamp `owner_name` when a newer parcel_cache/live-GIS owner disagrees, with its own staleness/confidence rules) is future work.
    **Tests:** `tests/test_parcel_cache_staleness.py` (9 cases — `cache_age_days`/`cache_is_stale` missing/fresh/old/boundary; `lookup_with_tier` keeping value fresh and withholding it stale, including through the `zero_pad` tier; the plain `lookup()` wrapper; two `enrich_gis_attrs()` integration cases proving the live-query fallthrough fires when value was withheld and does NOT fire when a value is already present). `tests/test_recover_tax_year_from_checkpoint.py` (5 cases — `_keys()` ordering, `_build_index()` refusing an ambiguous shared-url key while still resolving each parcel's own payload by its specific key, a genuine-duplicate-donor key still resolving, an end-to-end board-row match reproducing the exact Buncombe collision with real numbers). Full related suite (`test_parcel_cache*`, `test_dq_parcel_cache_id_tolerance`, `test_gis_cache`, `test_parcel_variants`, `test_arcgis_distress*`, both new files): 196 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, 194s.
    **Scope recap and what is NOT done:** Bug 1's fix is in a one-off recovery script outside the live pipeline — no board-write-path change, no backfill risk introduced by the fix itself. Bug 2's fix touches `parcel_cache.py` + `enrichment_gis_attrs.py`, both IN the live `main.py` pipeline, scoped to withholding/re-fetching VALUE only — owner/address/sqft/acreage fills are untouched. **Neither fix retroactively corrects the board that is already published**: the 610 Buncombe rows still carry the wrong `tax_owed`/`principal_tax_due` from bug 1, and an unknown — likely sizeable, since the bug is generic to every cached county, not just Buncombe's 829 tax-delinquency rows — number of rows board-wide still carry a stale cache-sourced `market_value`/`tax_value` from bug 2. A board-wide backfill/re-enrichment pass to correct already-published values is separate future work, explicitly out of scope for this session (code-only; no live board write on Mac or the Oracle VM).
    **Compliance / concurrent-session hygiene:** code-only, no board write on either host. `docs/validation_2026-10-02/` read for grounding, never modified. Items 50-51 landed in this same file concurrently while this item was being written; re-read the file immediately before each edit and only ever appended this item's own numbered entry. This item's commit stages only the files it actually changed, by name: `scripts/recover_tax_year_from_checkpoint.py`, `src/foreclosure_scraper/parcel_cache.py`, `src/foreclosure_scraper/enrichment_gis_attrs.py`, `tests/test_parcel_cache_staleness.py`, `tests/test_recover_tax_year_from_checkpoint.py`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`.

53. **FIXED (2026-10-02/03) — owner_name "missing-only" fill policy: item 52's Finding C, flagged there as future work, built now.** `owner_name` (and nothing else — see scope below) could be FILLED but never REFRESHED: `enrichment_arcgis.py` and `enrichment_gis_attrs.py`'s parcel-cache fast paths and `enrichment_gis_attrs.py`'s live-GIS-attrs path all gated on bare `if not li.owner_name: ...`, so a board row's owner stayed frozen at whatever a scraper first captured, even sitting right next to an already-fresher parcel_cache snapshot on the SAME run.
    **Re-verified live, fresh, same mechanism (not re-derived from yesterday's JSON alone):** refetched `tax.buncombenc.gov/Parcel/Details/{pin}` just now (crossing into 2026-10-03) for 5 of the earlier session's mismatched rows — 4/5 reproduce exactly: PIN 965488696900000 board "Brandon Bryant" vs. live "SUSAN STANFILL, IAN HUGHES"; PIN 966553276600000 board "Tim Magee" vs. live "JAMES DAVENPORT, LISA DAVENPORT"; PIN 964695441000000 board "SARAH CARPENTER LIM IRREVOCABLE TRUST" vs. live "FONDA HAIGHT"; PIN 970030317600000 board "COPHER JEREMY, COPHER ELDARA" vs. live "LYUBOV BORTNIK, BOGDAN BORTNIK". The 5th (PIN 979549470400000) came back genuinely ambiguous to script — the page repeats several "Owner Name(s)" blocks (subject parcel plus what looks like a nearby-sales-comps panel) and the first one read neither the board's name nor the validator's recorded real_owner, needing a human read of the page to disambiguate rather than a blind regex; left uncounted rather than forced either way. Separately, independently re-ran the token-overlap check against the existing `tax_lien_validation_results.json` (not re-scraped, same raw evidence item 52 used): 15/59 (25.4%) — matches item 52's own number exactly, confirming it still holds today, not a one-time artifact.
    **No existing staleness signal — a related, separate finding.** Looked for a last-updated/last-verified field for owner_name before building a threshold against one: there isn't one. `Listing.first_seen`/`last_seen` look like candidates but are not usable — `Listing.merge()` (`models.py`) bumps `last_seen = max(...)` on EVERY merge unconditionally, even a merge that touches nothing else about the row (the same method's generic field loop is what keeps owner_name frozen to begin with). A row can show today's `last_seen` while its `owner_name` has sat untouched for a year. There was, before this fix, no way to tell how stale any given row's owner_name actually was.
    **Fix**, new module `owner_freshness.py`, deliberately mirroring item 52's own `CACHE_VALUE_MAX_AGE_DAYS`/`cache_is_stale()` pattern one grain finer (per-LISTING-field instead of per-cache-FILE): every refresh-capable write now stamps `raw['owner_name_as_of']` (ISO date) via `stamp_owner_name()`; `should_refresh_owner_name(li, candidate)` replaces the bare `if not li.owner_name` guard at both parcel-cache fast paths (`enrichment_arcgis.enrich()`, `enrichment_gis_attrs.enrich_gis_attrs()`) and the live-GIS path (`enrichment_gis_attrs.apply_gis_attrs()`) — true for a plain blank-fill (unchanged behavior), or for a genuinely different candidate name once the existing stamp is missing (legacy data — same "missing == stale" convention `cache_is_stale()` uses for an absent cache file) or at least `OWNER_REFRESH_MIN_AGE_DAYS` (1 day — just enough to stop two current-state sources flapping within one run; real turnover is months-scale) old.
    **Cost-bounded, explicitly.** `enrichment_gis_attrs.enrich_gis_attrs()`'s two `skipped_done` short-circuits (the ones that make a full re-enrich tractable at all — see item 52's own comments on the 6.8h uncapped run) both gained an `owner_refresh_due` escape hatch, but ONLY when `li.parcel_id` is set — i.e. only when the free, local, already-on-every-pass parcel_cache lookup can answer the recheck. A lead with no parcel_id gets zero behavior change. When `owner_refresh_due` is the ONLY reason execution reached the parcel-cache block and that lookup MISSES, both files bail immediately rather than falling through to the live network query below — a local miss is not grounds to pay for a live re-query just to re-verify a name. `enrichment_arcgis.py`'s own cache-block entry condition got the same `owner_refresh_due` OR-term, same miss-bail.
    **Scope — what this deliberately does NOT touch, and why.** `enrichment_court_owner_verify.py` (reverts owner_name to the docket defendant on a GIS-vs-defendant surname mismatch) is a DIFFERENT, pre-existing mechanism — a wrong-PARCEL/bad-geo-snap guard, not a staleness policy — untouched. Every scraper that sets `owner_name` once at ingest straight from a notice/caption (`brunswick_legal_notices.py`, `nc_coastal_tax_foreclosure.py`, `wake_tax_foreclosure.py`, `column_legal_notices.py`) and every court-sourced defendant/debtor/divorce-party name are HISTORICAL records of who was party to a specific filing at a specific time — refreshing those to "whoever owns it today" would make them WRONG, exactly the trap the task that created this fix warned about; left as pure fill-only. `enrichment_ncpts_lrc.py`'s live NCPTS query and `enrichment_owner_mailing.py`'s `is_target()` (`not has_mailing(li)`) are BOTH still missing-only and would show the identical staleness bug if re-run against an already-complete lead — not fixed here because both are live-network-only (no free local cache to bound the recheck cost against, unlike the parcel_cache paths this item fixes), flagged as their own future work rather than forced. `enrichment_owner_cluster.py` was checked and is NOT part of this bug — it is a read-only GROUP BY recomputed fresh every run straight off current `owner_name`, not a persisted missing-only field; `owner_mailing_address` (`raw['gis']['mailing']`, filled at `enrichment_gis_attrs.py`'s same parcel-cache block) was investigated but left missing-only this pass — lower outreach cost of staleness than the owner's actual name, same reasoning item 52 used to leave sqft/acreage ungated on the value axis.
    **`web_artifact.RAW_KEEP`** gained `"owner_name_as_of": "*"` — without it `_slim_raw()` silently drops the stamp at publish and every row would look permanently unstamped (always "unknown age") the very next run after this lands, defeating the staleness gate entirely (the same failure mode this file's own `landed_by`/`entity_type` RAW_KEEP comments already warn about).
    **Tests:** `tests/test_owner_freshness.py` (21 cases) — `owner_name_as_of`/`owner_name_age_days`/`is_owner_refreshable` missing/fresh/old/boundary/malformed-stamp; `should_refresh_owner_name` for a blank owner, a same-name-different-punctuation non-change, a different name blocked while fresh-stamped and allowed once stale or never stamped, an empty candidate; `stamp_owner_name` setting both fields; `enrichment_arcgis.enrich()` integration (refreshes a stale unstamped owner with NO network call, does not re-flap an owner stamped today, a no-parcel_id lead takes the byte-for-byte unmodified fallthrough path, a parcel-cache MISS on an owner-only-stale recheck bails without a live query); the same three shapes against `enrichment_gis_attrs.enrich_gis_attrs()`; `apply_gis_attrs()` direct unit coverage (refreshes stale/unstamped, does not touch a fresh stamp, still fills a blank exactly as before). Targeted suite (`test_owner_freshness`, `test_parcel_cache_staleness`, `test_gis_cache`, `test_owner_mailing_scdot_supplement`, `test_sc_gis`, `test_gis_exempt_tax_relief_bridge`, `test_scdot_breaker`, `test_task6_parcel_mailing`, `test_nc_gis_addr_fields`, `test_sc_mail_spines`, `test_sc_coastal_rosters`, `test_skip_trace_free`, `test_clean_parcel_no_length_gate`): 142 passed, 19 skipped, 0 failed.
    **Compliance:** code-only, no board write on either host (this Mac is over `BOARD_LOAD_MAX_SOURCE_MB`/`BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB` right now regardless). This fix takes effect on the NEXT real pipeline run, same as item 52's — it does NOT retroactively correct any already-published row; the live-confirmed ~25% mismatch rate on the existing board stands until that next run.

54. **3-COLUMN VERDICT (2026-10-03) — `bop_federal` (3/148), `liensnc_posthumous_filing` (7/148), `two_year_delinquent` (7/148). One real bug found and FIXED; the other two are genuinely narrow, not bugs.**
    **`bop_federal` — real cause is one-time candidate starvation, not a federal-custody population ceiling, PLUS a real accuracy bug found and fixed.** Live-queried bop.gov directly today: the endpoint still works exactly as documented (fresh 200, real `InmateLocator` JSON, `Captcha:false`), and a scripted breadth probe got transiently Cloudflare-blocked after 2 queries — consistent with the code's own existing circuit breaker, not a dead endpoint. Read-only board measurement (`board_stream`, no load_board): the candidate pool is 193,709 NC/SC rows with an owner name, but **every single `bop_check`/`bop_federal` row on the board (all 150 of them, 133 miss + 17 match) carries the identical `checked_at` minute, 2026-09-29T18:18** — the day the feature was built (commit `9c16d8ee`). It has been queried exactly ONCE, ever, for 150 of 193,709 candidates (0.08%); nothing re-runs it (no cron/launchd entry calls `run_pending_signal_enrichers.py`, and `main.py`'s full pipeline hasn't completed since July). 3/148 counties is simply "whichever 3 counties happened to own the first 150 board-order rows that day," not a real signal about federal vs. county custody rates.
    **Real bug found while live-checking the 17 matches against the current board, not guessed:** 2 of 17 (11.8%) are stale. A Buncombe parcel's owner is now "COVENANT PRESBYTERIAN CHURCH" (an entity — `_name_parts` would reject it outright) yet still carries `raw['bop_federal']['matched_name'] == "CECIL BENNETT"`, someone with zero relation to the church. A second Buncombe parcel's owner is now "MAXWELL, RONALD" while its stamp still names "BETTY BAKER" (released 2012). Root cause: this is the SAME "missing-only, never revisited" shape item 53 just fixed for `owner_name` itself, one layer downstream — `enrich_bop_federal`'s own `if ... .get("bop_federal"): continue` guard treats any existing match as permanent, so a later ownership change (sale, donation, correction) never gets reconciled against a name-keyed match stamped against whoever used to own the parcel.
    **Fix**, `enrichment_bop_federal.py`: new `_owner_still_supports_match()` + `_clear_stale_matches()` pre-pass, run before candidate selection on every `enrich_bop_federal()` call — drops `raw['bop_federal']` (and, only when its own `source` is specifically BOP, the shared `raw['incarceration']`) when the CURRENT owner no longer parses to the name that was actually queried, so the row falls back to being an ordinary candidate (re-queried immediately if the new owner is a different real person, left correctly empty if the new owner is now an entity) instead of quietly keeping someone else's federal record forever.
    **Downstream wiring required, found and fixed in the same pass**: `scripts/run_pending_signal_enrichers.py` — the script most likely to actually run this enricher going forward — has an explicit `_RAW_DELETE_SAFE_AS_NONE` allowlist gating which popped raw keys its before/after diff is even ALLOWED to represent as a `None` write (`patch_existing_rows()` has no delete primitive). Without adding `"bop_federal"`/`"incarceration"` to that set, the new clear would look correct in memory but silently never reach the published board through that script. Updated the allowlist + its docstring (re-verified every reader of both keys uses `.get()`/truthy checks, never a bare presence test — safe to represent as `None`).
    **Tests:** `tests/test_bop_federal.py` +9 cases (`_owner_still_supports_match` unchanged/entity/different-person/no-matched-name-to-check; `_clear_stale_matches` drops the tag and a BOP-sourced `incarceration` but never a NC-DAC/SC-DOC one; end-to-end through `enrich_bop_federal` for both the entity-owner and different-person-owner cases); 2 existing tests updated for the new `stale_cleared` stat key. `tests/test_run_pending_signal_enrichers_patch_integration.py` +1 case proving the deletion actually lands as `None` through the real patch path (the regression that would have caught the allowlist gap). Targeted: 47 passed, 0 failed. Full suite re-run clean after these changes: **7,250 passed, 61 skipped, 0 failed** (10 more than item 53's 7,240 baseline, matching the 10 new cases added here).
    **`liensnc_posthumous_filing` — real, distinct, and trustworthy; does NOT inherit the broader liensnc/builder_distress "majority not real distress" problem.** That problem (see the 2026-10-02 accuracy-validation work, commit `19d3888e`) is about treating a routine "Appointment of Lien Agent" filing itself as a FINANCIAL-distress signal when it's actually ordinary construction-permitting activity. This module makes a narrower, independently-checkable claim instead: the deed owner-of-record AT FILING TIME already reads as a decedent's estate/heirs (`HEIRS`/`DECEASED`/`ESTATE OF` token, via the same `enrichment_owner_name_signal.classify()` the codebase already trusts elsewhere), and it is explicitly stamped `confidence: "low"` and **never read by `distress_score.py` or `enrichment_lead_signals.py` at all** — RAW_KEEP-only, zero scoring-inflation risk today, unlike `builder_distress`.
    **Live-verified 6 of the 7 current counties' real examples against Gaston County's own live ArcGIS parcel layer today** (`gis.gastoncountync.gov/publicgis/.../Parcels/FeatureServer/11`, not the board's cached copy): `PIN 3565-08-7426` → `CURR_NAME1 = "ARMSTRONG DONALD R HEIRS"`, `PIN 3516-25-6330` → `"GILL MARIE H HEIRS OF"`, `PIN 3504-61-3009` → `"CRANK ELLABELLE K HEIRS"` — exact match to the board's extracted owner string, confirming the signal reflects the county's real, CURRENT record, not a stale/garbled scrape. A genuinely useful nuance surfaced in the same check, not a correctness bug: 3 other examples (Gaston `KISER JASON LEE HEIRS`, `MASSEY JAMES WAYNE HEIRS`, `BELL KAY F...HEIRS`) now show `CURR_NAME1` as a builder/general-contracting LLC with the heirs' name preserved only in `PRVYRNAME1` — the parcel has ALREADY been sold to a flipper/investor since the lien-agent filing, meaning by the time the tag surfaces, the heir-owned window to talk to the actual seller may have already closed. Both mechanisms this module ships behave exactly as its own docstring claims: `date_mismatch` (the cross-record decedent join) is architecturally correct but scores 0/19 today for the documented structural reason (NC liensnc rows carry no county to join on, and no NC source attaches a real `date_of_death` yet — only SC sources do); 100% of the 19 real tagged rows (7 counties: Gaston, Catawba, Lincoln, McDowell, Pitt, Cleveland, Polk) use the fallback `owner_name_token` mechanism. No code change — investigated, live-verified, correctly left alone.
    **`two_year_delinquent` — genuinely distinct from `multi_year_delinquent_tax`/`years_delinquent`, and the CSV's 7/148 is correct, not a presence-check bug — but re-running the actual coverage-generator script against today's board surfaced a real, separate landmine worth flagging.** Re-ran `scripts/run_pending_signal_enrichers.py`-adjacent measurement directly: `is_two_year_plus == True` (the real signal) appears on exactly 488 rows across exactly the CSV's 7 counties (Buncombe, Lincoln, Henderson, Onslow, Graham, Gates, Hyde) — sourced from `nc_its_public_tax.py` (Onslow/Graham only), `albemarle_observer_tax_lists.py` (Gates only, deliberately — "only Gates prints a year per row"), and the real `nc_ptscloud_delinquent_tax` branch of a one-off August gap-filler. `multi_year_delinquent_tax` (8/148: Buncombe, Gaston, Guilford, Catawba, New Hanover, Burke, Pickens, Oconee) is a completely different vendor/engine with almost no county overlap — genuinely two distinct tiers, not fragmentation, confirmed by measurement not inference.
    **Landmine found, NOT fixed (dead code, no live benefit to patching it)**: a bare `raw.get(key_name)` truthy/presence check on this exact field — the obvious first thing anyone would write to measure it — overcounts by **5x** (35/148 counties, ~29,800 rows) instead of 7/148, because `scripts/fill_all_gaps.py` (one-off, August 2026, never on any cron, last touched 2026-08-26, commit `1fbf3777` literally titled "100% ALL FIELDS...=100%") unconditionally stamps `{"is_two_year_plus": False, "tax_year": None, "source": "default"}` under the SAME canonical key on every lead it couldn't determine a real value for — 28,851 rows board-wide. Both of this repo's own real consumers (`fullmer_rank.years_delinquent()` and the actual coverage-CSV generator) already correctly check `is_two_year_plus` truth rather than bare presence, so this does NOT corrupt anything live today — but it is exactly the kind of "stamp a default to look complete" anti-pattern the current zero-gap mandate exists to move away from, and the next person who writes a naive presence check (this session's own first attempt included) will get fooled by it. Left `fill_all_gaps.py` untouched: it is not in the live pipeline and nothing will ever re-run it, so patching it cannot un-pollute the 28,851 rows already on the board and has no forward benefit. Flagged here instead of silently fixed, per the zero-gap mandate.
    **Also flagged, not fixed**: `sc_catalis_delinquent_roll.py` (covers Pickens/Chester/Hampton/Fairfield/Aiken SC, also writes a real `two_year_delinquent`) produces **zero rows anywhere on the live board** — Pickens' real tax-delinquency data comes entirely from a different source (`pickens_delinquent_parcels`/`multi_year_delinquent_tax`). Spun off as its own follow-up (`task_102c3c34`) rather than guessed at — needs a live check of whether the scraper is dead, broken, or just superseded for Pickens and still live-needed for the other 4 counties.
    **Compliance**: all 3 investigations were read-only against the live board (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, no `load_board()`) except the `bop_federal` fix itself, which is code-only (no board write on this Mac — still over `BOARD_LOAD_MAX_SOURCE_MB`/`BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`). One live BOP.gov breadth probe (2 queries) and one live Gaston County ArcGIS query (3 PIN lookups) were made against real external public endpoints; no scraping, no writes, both well within normal single-query use of free public APIs this codebase already calls routinely.

55. **CONFIRMED + FIXED (2026-10-03) — `gastonia_code_enforcement.py` had the identical has_open/severe shape as Henderson's 2026-10-02 bug (item 48, commit `19d3888e`), spun off that day specifically because Gastonia's own category taxonomy had not yet been live-verified. It has now been, and the bug is real.** Full live crawl of the real `devsvcs.gastonianc.gov` CityView feed today (same recursive quadrant query the scraper itself uses): 19,429 unique cases, 3,304 open, 1,493 properties with at least one open case. Gastonia's category field (`ApplicationTypeDescription`) is a DIFFERENT vocabulary from Henderson's ArcGIS `violationType` coded domain (confirmed live — free text on a CityView locator API, not an ArcGIS layer), so it had to be independently classified rather than reusing Henderson's category names. Pulled each sampled case's real `Description` text off its `StatusReference` detail page (not just the bare category label) to disambiguate paperwork from physical condition wherever the label alone was ambiguous.
