    **Real breakdown of the 3,304 open cases, live 2026-10-03**: Vegetation/Weeds 1,533 (already correctly excluded pre-fix — too common/low-specificity, same reasoning as Henderson's Zoning), Public Nuisance 857, Zoning/Land Use 299, Housing 262, Street/Sidewalk Obstruction 79, Building Code 63, Fence/Wall 43, Unauthorized Encampment 41, Signage 39, Tree Removal 23, Junk Vehicles 21, Abandoned Vehicle 11, Livestock 8, Historic District 8, Graffiti 7, Commercial Maintenance Code 7, Drought Violation 3. Sampled real Descriptions confirmed the paperwork/permit categories are genuinely not property-condition signals (a Building Code sample was a lapsed fire-alarm monitoring contract; a Historic District sample was a resident actively installing a pool and running new underground utility lines — the opposite of vacant; Fence/Wall samples were height/placement disputes) and surfaced one real gap in the PRE-EXISTING `_SEVERE_RE`: Abandoned Vehicle (11 open) was never on it despite all 3 sampled cases describing genuine junk/debris ("Bunch of junk", "tow truck... never move") — the same concept as Junk Vehicles under a different label. Added it. Graffiti (7 open) was deliberately left off despite one sample mentioning a "vacant residence" — 2 of 3 samples were graffiti on a stop sign and a utility line, unrelated to the subject parcel, too noisy a category on this small a sample to trust.
    **Impact**: before this fix, ALL 1,493 open-case properties scored full PROPERTY `code_enforcement` credit regardless of category (`vacancy_adjacent` was never wired, same as pre-fix Henderson). With the live-verified category set, 719 of 1,493 (48.2%) carry ONLY non-vacancy-adjacent open cases (Vegetation/Weeds, Zoning/Land Use, Street/Sidewalk Obstruction, Building Code, Fence/Wall, Signage, Tree Removal, Livestock, Historic District, Graffiti, Drought Violation) — essentially the same scale of over-crediting Henderson had (53.1%).
    **Fix**, mirroring Henderson's exact convention: added `"vacancy_adjacent": severe` to the `code_enforcement` raw block (`build_listing()`), which `signal_freshness.code_enforcement_open()` already knows how to read (it was written generically when Henderson was fixed, and Lincoln/Charlotte/arcgis_distress_layers already use the same key) — `has_open` stays truthful, PROPERTY credit is just withheld for non-vacancy-adjacent-only properties. Also extended `_SEVERE_RE` to add `abandoned vehicle`.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_gastonia_code_enforcement.py` — 2 existing tests updated to assert `vacancy_adjacent` alongside `severe`; +6 new (Abandoned Vehicle is severe+vacancy_adjacent; a constructed-but-realistic Zoning/Land Use case is open but not vacancy-adjacent and does not reach `distress_score._signals_for`; the real Vegetation/Weeds fixture withholds PROPERTY credit through the new gate specifically, not just the old label; a severe case alongside a non-severe one at the same parcel still scores; the base code_enforcement signal scores when vacancy-adjacent; a live smoke test gated on `RUN_LIVE=1`, ~80s full-city crawl). Targeted (`test_gastonia_code_enforcement.py` + `test_henderson_code_violations.py` together): 59 passed, 2 skipped (both RUN_LIVE opt-ins) — then RUN_LIVE=1 on Gastonia's live test alone: 1 passed, reproducing the exact live numbers above (`properties=1493 not_vacancy_adjacent=719`). Full suite (excluding this file + RUN_LIVE elsewhere, to isolate the live crawl time): 7,234 passed, 61 skipped, 0 failed; `test_gastonia_code_enforcement.py` alone (offline): 23 passed, 1 skipped.
    **Compliance**: code fix only. No board write attempted or possible on this Mac (8GB, over the board-size write gates regardless) — this takes effect on the next real pipeline run, same as items 48/52/53. Live network calls were read-only GETs/POSTs against Gastonia's own free, public, anonymous CityView endpoint (the same one this scraper already calls in production) and its own `StatusReference` detail pages — no scraping of anything the production scraper doesn't already fetch, no writes, no auth bypass.

56. **SYSTEMATIC SWEEP (2026-10-03) for the "stored classification derived from owner_name, never revalidated when owner_name changes" bug shape that hit 3 separate times today (items 53/54 above: `owner_name` itself, `bop_federal`, `owner_name_signal`). Re-read all 3 fixes first, then grepped every module for the same skip-once-forever guard against a name-keyed match. Found and fixed 2 more genuine instances, both tied to the SAME shared `raw['incarceration']` key `bop_federal` already shares; checked and ruled out every other plausible candidate.**
    **`enrichment_incarceration.py` (NC DAC / SC DOC state-prison match) — real bug, same shape as `bop_federal`, literally the sibling module `bop_federal`'s own module docstring names as covering "state prison" while it covers federal.** Its `if (li.raw or {}).get("incarceration"): continue` guard treats any existing NC-DAC/SC-DOC match as permanent, so a later ownership change never gets reconciled against a name-keyed match stamped against whoever used to own the parcel. Live-checked against the real board (`board_stream`, read-only, no `load_board()`): of the rows carrying `raw['incarceration']` or `raw['jail_booking']` (395 + 322 respectively, overlapping), **11 rows name someone with zero relation to the CURRENT owner, now an unrelated entity** — e.g. a Henderson County parcel matched "MICHAEL PEAK" but now reads "FIRST CITIZENS BANK & TRUST CO"; a Gaston County parcel matched "DEANA WILSON" but now reads "GASTONIA CITY OF"; an Anderson County SC parcel matched "USUF ABUZAHRI" but now reads "ANDERSON CITY OF" — plus several more rows (~26 in a quick re-derivation, some inflated by suffix-parsing noise like "JR" that the codebase's own shared `_name_parts` already has, not an artifact of this check) where the current owner parses to a different real person. This is the project's own "Motivated-seller engine" core incarceration lead source (`project_motivated_seller_engine` memory) and a `distress_score` LEGAL weight-8 signal — a materially bigger match population than `bop_federal`'s one-time 150-row/17-match sample, so a materially bigger exposure to the same bug.
    **`enrichment_jail_bookings.py` (county-jail roster match, the much larger pre-trial/local-hold pool) — same shape, one lane over.** Both `match_rosters` (bulk-roster lane) and the per-name `SEARCH_ROSTERS` lane skip a listing the moment `raw['jail_booking']` is truthy, forever, with no reconciliation against a later owner-name refresh. Same live board check as above surfaced the Gaston/Anderson examples cited there.
    **Fix**, both modules, mirroring `enrichment_bop_federal.py`'s own `_owner_still_supports_match()` / `_clear_stale_matches()` convention exactly (commit `9e3fd2c9`): a pre-pass run first on every `enrich_incarceration()` / `enrich_jail_bookings()` call drops the module's OWN match when the CURRENT owner no longer parses to the name actually queried (now unparseable as a person, or parses to a different name) — `enrichment_incarceration.py` only ever clears a `raw['incarceration']` entry whose `source` is `DAC_SOURCE`/`SCDC_SOURCE`; `enrichment_jail_bookings.py` only ever clears `raw['jail_booking']` and, conditionally, a `raw['incarceration']` entry whose `source` is its own `"{county} County jail roster"` string — neither ever touches an entry the OTHER module (or `bop_federal`) wrote to the same shared key. Both return a new `stale_cleared` stat.
    **Downstream wiring**: `scripts/run_pending_signal_enrichers.py` runs `jail_bookings` as one of its 8 steps through the merge-only `patch_existing_rows()` path (`enrichment_incarceration.py`'s `enrich_incarceration` is NOT one of the 8 — it's called from `main.py`'s full-rewrite pipeline instead, so no allowlist risk there). Added `"jail_booking"` to `_RAW_DELETE_SAFE_AS_NONE` (alongside the `"incarceration"` entry item 54 already added) — without it this fix would look correct in memory but silently never reach the published board through that script, the same hazard item 54's own fix flagged and fixed for `bop_federal`/`incarceration`. Re-verified every reader of `jail_booking` (`distress_score.py`, `enrichment_lead_signals.py` via `custody_ended`, `jail_roster_history.py`, `scripts/backfill_jail_rosters.py`) uses `.get()`, never a bare presence test.
    **Other candidates checked and ruled out** (all confirmed by reading the actual code, not assumed): `enrichment_entity_type.py` — explicitly "idempotent and unconditional: it always (re)computes and overwrites" per its own docstring, already immune by design. `enrichment_divorce_no_subsequent_deed.py` — unconditionally re-derives from `raw['resolved_from_name']` every call, no skip-once guard. `enrichment_co_defendant_signal.py` / `enrichment_hoa_plaintiff_signal.py` — both "pure computation, drops nothing," unconditional overwrite every call, and both key off HISTORICAL court-filing parties (plaintiff/defendant/co-defendant at time of filing), not a "current state" field. `enrichment_rod_lookup.py` / `enrichment_rod_name_index.py` — both re-query from `li.owner_name` fresh on every call (only cached within a single run's process lifetime), no persisted skip-once guard. `fullmer_rank.py` does not read `incarceration`/`jail_booking` at all (checked directly — unlike `owner_name_signal`, there is no "reverse direction" consumer-reads-stale-field risk here); `distress_score.py`/`enrichment_lead_signals.py` both already gate `incarceration` through `signal_freshness.custody_ended` (release-status staleness, a DIFFERENT and already-handled dimension) but had no owner-identity staleness gate before this fix — now closed at the source instead of needing a third gate added at every read site.
    **Scope — one known gap left deliberately open**: `scripts/backfill_jail_rosters.py` (a standalone, occasional manual tool, not part of `main.py`'s pipeline or `run_pending_signal_enrichers.py`'s 8 steps) calls `enrichment_jail_bookings.match_rosters()` directly in its `--apply` path and has its OWN independent re-implementation of the same `if ... .get("jail_booking"): continue` skip check in its dry-run `scan_board()` — neither goes through the new `_clear_stale_matches()` pre-pass, so a stale match would still block a rematch if this script is ever run. Left untouched: its `--apply` path needs the whole board in memory and is already blocked on this Mac by the standing board-write freeze, and wiring the fix in requires auditing whether `_owner_still_supports_match`/`_owner_of` behave correctly against its `SimpleNamespace` scan stand-in (not a real `Listing`) — a small but separate piece of work, not forced into this change.
    **Flagged, not fixed, as precise scoped follow-ups (per the zero-gap mandate — found genuine candidates, ran out of budget to live-verify + fix both in one pass)**: (1) `enrichment_voter_phone.py`'s `raw['owner_phone']` has the identical `if raw.get("owner_phone"): continue` skip-once-forever shape, gating a NAME+ADDRESS match that would plausibly go stale the same way after a sale — but unlike `bop_federal`/incarceration, `_set_phone()` does not stamp the matched name into the block, so detecting staleness needs a different technique (re-derive the match live against the current owner and diff), and the same shared key is also written by `enrichment_free_phones.py`/`enrichment_sc_phone.py`/`enrichment_county_phone.py`/`enrichment_skip_trace.py`, each needing its own check for the same guard. Spun off as `task_3d96a9c7`. (2) `enrichment_sos_agent.py`'s `raw['sos_agent']` (NC SOS registered-agent/officer contact for entity-owned leads) has the identical `if isinstance(raw.get("sos_agent"), dict): continue` shape — genuine instance of the bug, but it is a skip-trace/outreach CONTACT field, never read by `distress_score.py`/`fullmer_rank.py` (confirmed by grep), so lower real-world consequence than a scoring signal; also needs a new provenance stamp added first (the block does not currently record which entity name it was resolved for). `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py` has the identical parallel guard and would need the same fix. Spun off as `task_6b450bcb`.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_incarceration_stale_match.py` (new, 12 cases) + `tests/test_jail_bookings_stale_match.py` (new, 13 cases) — `_owner_still_supports_match`/`_clear_stale_matches` unit coverage for both modules (unchanged/entity/different-person/no-matched-name/wrong-source-left-alone), plus end-to-end through `enrich_incarceration()`/`enrich_jail_bookings()` proving the real-world entity-owner and different-person cases clear + (when re-matchable) requery within the same run, mirroring `test_bop_federal.py`'s own stale-match section exactly. `tests/test_incarceration_rotation.py` — 1 existing assertion updated for the new `stale_cleared` key (same mechanical update item 54 made to its own bop_federal tests). `tests/test_run_pending_signal_enrichers_patch_integration.py` +1 case (`test_jail_stale_match_deletion_lands_as_none_for_both_keys`) proving the `jail_booking` deletion actually lands as `None` through the real patch path — the regression that would have caught the allowlist gap, mirroring item 54's own `bop_stale_match` integration test. Targeted (both new files + every incarceration/jail_bookings/bop_federal/patch-integration test): 182 passed, 0 failed. Full suite re-run clean after these changes: **7,283 passed, 62 skipped, 0 failed** (672s).
    **Compliance**: code-only, no board write on this Mac (still over `BOARD_LOAD_MAX_SOURCE_MB`/`BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB`). The board-state numbers above (11 stale entity-owner rows, 26 stale different-person rows) came from one read-only `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` pass over the real published board — no `load_board()`, no scraping, no live network call to any state/county roster. Takes effect on the next real pipeline run for `enrichment_incarceration.py` (via `main.py`) and the next `run_pending_signal_enrichers.py` run for `enrichment_jail_bookings.py` — does not retroactively correct any already-published row.

57. **CLOSED (2026-10-03) — the two CONTACT-field follow-ups item 56 deliberately spun off instead of guessing at (`task_3d96a9c7` owner_phone, `task_6b450bcb` sos_agent). Both verified live-population-scale first; different real-world risk, so different fix shape — not a blind mirror of item 56's pattern.**
    **`owner_phone` (ncsbe_voter, enrichment_voter_phone.py's own SAME-STATE NC match) — real bug, FIXED.** Live-checked against the board (`board_stream`, read-only): of 14,630 rows carrying a phone from this lane, re-deriving the match against a TARGETED, memory-bounded rescan of the SAME cached `data/ncvoter/` snapshot (not a different/refreshed one — ruling that out as the cause) using the CURRENT owner_name/street_address/county finds **2,121 (14.5%) no longer reproduce the stored phone** (1,743 of those find no match at all today). Manually traced one to its source voter record: Buncombe parcel `9648-62-3059-C0401`'s `owner_phone` still carries voter "BAKER, BETTY"'s number (verified against `data/ncvoter` directly) while `owner_name` has moved on to "MAXWELL, RONALD" — the identical parcel/person item 56's own `bop_federal` writeup names as its "released 2012" stale-match example, now confirmed to ALSO be carrying a stale phone under the same root cause. The cross-state xref lane (`ncsbe_voter_xref`/`sc_voter_xref`, 1,487 rows) is NOT part of this bug — `enrichment_sc_phone.py`'s existing identity gate already re-verifies it every run; live-checked 264/1,487 (17.8%) mismatches and all 264 were already correctly flagged `do_not_dial`/`contradicted`. The direct ncsbe_voter lane was simply never wired into that same gate.
    **Why this is harder than item 56's mirror, and the actual design**: a stale phone is a CONTACT, not a scoring signal — it might still reach that person for a different property, so clearing it would destroy real information, not just stop a false one. Checked both candidate fixes the task posed: a bare `owner_name_as_of`-style date stamp (owner_freshness.py's own convention) is NOT enough here, because unlike `owner_name` the comparison target (WHICH voter the phone was matched to) was never stored at all for this lane — a date alone can't tell a consumer whether `owner_name` has since diverged from an unknown quantity. Checked every real consumer (`campaign_export.py`, `api_server.py`, `analytics_dashboard.py`, `enrichment_dnc.py`, `enrichment_line_type.py`, `docs/dashboard.js`'s `ownerPhoneBlock()`): all of them ALREADY funnel exclusively through `enrichment_sc_phone.owner_phone_block_reason()`/`is_owner_phone_usable()` — none reads `owner_name` next to `owner_phone` to self-judge staleness, so a stamp nothing reads would not genuinely help. The fix instead extends that SAME existing, already-shipped, already-trusted gate (`enrichment_sc_phone.py`'s `DIRECT_SOURCES`) to also cover this lane: `_set_phone()` now stamps `matched_name` ("LAST,FIRST") on every new match, and `enrich_voter_phone()` runs both newly-written and any EARLIER run's own matches through `flag_unverified_xref_phones()` every call (the identical "re-checked on every run" policy `enrichment_sc_voter_xref.py` already uses) — a mismatch sets `do_not_dial`+`identity_check="contradicted"` and KEEPS the phone value; a still-good match is `identity_check="corroborated"` (immediately, without needing the cross-state lane's address/middle-initial corroboration, since a same-state match already required address-or-county agreement at write time — added as an explicit early-exit branch in `_check()` so the ~85% of this lane that is still fine is not wrongly downgraded to "unverified"). Zero consumer-side changes needed: `do_not_dial`/`identity_check` are already in `_SLIM_RAW["owner_phone"]` and `docs/dashboard.js`'s `_LEAN_RAW`/`ownerPhoneBlock()`, and every consumer already reads them generically. A legacy block with no `matched_name` (every one of the 14,630 existing rows, until the pipeline runs again) is left byte-for-byte untouched — same "code-only, takes effect next run, no retroactive correction" convention as every fix in this family.
    **`sos_agent` (NC SOS registered-agent/officer contact) — real, high-proportion but low-volume bug (344 rows total; `SOS_AGENT` is gated OFF by default). Verdict: (c), a real outreach-policy tradeoff, NOT guessed at — plus the one safe, additive piece that needed no policy call.** A word-boundary-safe re-check (the shipped `enrichment_sos_dissolution._is_business()` has its OWN unrelated substring bug — `"inc"` matches inside "Vinc-ent"/"Linc-oln" with no word boundary, flagged separately below, not fixed here since it is a different bug than the one assigned) against the CURRENT `owner_name`/`defendant` finds **at least 174/344 (50.6%)** no longer read as the business this profile was resolved for: 152 where the current party is not even business-shaped anymore (a person, a city/county, a trade name), 22 where it reads as a DIFFERENT business. Concrete confirmed examples: Buncombe parcel `9629-91-6321-00000` — owner/defendant now "TRIVETTE, BRUCE" (a person), `sos_agent` still names "Lentz Investments, LLC" (status: **Dissolved**), contact "Ethel P. Lentz"; 7 Lincoln County parcels whose owner/defendant is now "LINCOLN COUNTY" (reverted after a tax sale — plus one, `4603666720`, now owned by "NEWDOMINION BANK") all still carry "BECM Properties LLC"'s agent, contact "Michael James Hester". Some of the 22 "different business" cases are near-name-collision noise, not real staleness (e.g. "J AND M FAMILY HOMES LLC" vs. a stored "J & M Family Homes, Inc." — almost certainly the same real entity, an SOS search-ambiguity artifact, not an ownership change), so 50.6% is a floor, not a precise rate.
    **Why this did NOT get the same gate as owner_phone**: owner_phone's fix leans on a hard, pre-existing, business-approved compliance line (TCPA/do-not-dial) and an ALREADY-SHIPPED gate built for an almost-identical risk on a sibling source — extending it was applying an existing policy, not inventing one. `sos_agent` feeds a MAILING contact with no such line and no sibling gate to extend; whether a stale registered-agent contact should be suppressed outright, kept with a visible warning, or trigger an active re-resolve (a real network/stealth-browser cost this module is explicitly gated off by default to avoid spending casually) is a genuine outreach-policy call that depends on how aggressively the business wants to re-spend SOS lookups and how wrong a mailing contact is tolerable to show — not this task's call to guess at. **What WAS added** (the safe, additive piece, no policy decision required): every freshly-resolved profile now stamps `resolved_for_entity` (the entity name `_entity_of(li)` actually resolved) and `resolved_at`, propagated correctly to co-owned leads; added to `_SLIM_RAW["sos_agent"]`/`docs/dashboard.js`'s `_LEAN_RAW` (previously the slim/dashboard view did not even surface which entity a contact was resolved for, so a human reviewer had no way to spot a mismatch without reading the full board). This auto-clears or suppresses nothing. A profile resolved before this fix has neither field and is left exactly as it was.
    **Incidental, NOT fixed here, flagged for a separate task**: `enrichment_sos_dissolution._is_business()`'s marker list (`"inc"`, `"co."`, ...) is checked with a bare substring test, no word boundary — matches inside ordinary words/surnames ("Vincent", "Lincoln"), shared by `enrichment_sos_agent.py` AND `enrichment_nc_sos.py` (both import it). Confirmed live: `_is_business("LINCOLN COUNTY")` and `_is_business("VINCENT, ANGELA")` both wrongly return `True`. This inflated the raw sos_agent mismatch count before the word-boundary-safe recheck above corrected for it; it is a pre-existing, independent bug, not the one this task was scoped to.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_voter_phone_identity_gate.py` (new, 7 cases) — a fresh match on each stamping path, an earlier match contradicted once owner_name moves on (the live Buncombe case verbatim), an earlier match still corroborated when unchanged, a legacy block with no `matched_name` left exactly alone, a foreign-source phone never gated, and a mixed batch proving only the eligible rows move. `tests/test_sos_agent.py` +3 cases — a fresh resolution stamps both fields, a propagated profile keeps its OWN `resolved_for_entity` (not the donor's), a legacy profile is left alone. Existing `tests/test_sc_phone_gate.py` (62 cases) re-run clean — the cross-state lane's own behavior is provably unchanged. `tests/test_board_slim.py`'s client/server slim-allowlist equality check re-run clean after the `_SLIM_RAW`/`docs/dashboard.js` edit. Targeted (every touched/related file): 247+95 passed, 0 failed.
    **Compliance**: code-only, no board write on this Mac. Every number above came from read-only `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` passes over the real published board, plus a targeted (want-last-name-filtered, tens-of-MB, not the multi-GB full index) single rescan of the already-cached `data/ncvoter/` files already on disk — never a live network call, never `load_board()`. Takes effect on the next real pipeline run; does not retroactively correct any already-published row.

58. **INVESTIGATED + FIXED (2026-10-03) — a prior VM run mentioned a Gaston County timeout and a Rutherford login-wall in passing, both unexplained. Both trace to `rod/aumentum.py`/`rod/cott.py` (the ROD vendor adapters `counties_generic/nod_discovery.py`, `counties_nc/nc_rod_substitute_trustee.py`, and `national/cash_buyer_deeds.py` all share), not `sc_coastal_rosters.py` (SC, unrelated) — Gaston/Rutherford are NC. Different verdicts for each, both confirmed with fresh live evidence today, not assumed from the existing docstrings.**
    **Rutherford — genuine, confirmed, PERMANENT login wall. No code change; already handled correctly.** Live-probed today 2026-10-03 (not just re-read 2026-10-02's docstring claim): both `cott.discover_recent_nods("NC","Rutherford")` trials hit `/User/Login.aspx?ReturnUrl=...` and returned `[]` via the existing `_is_login_wall()` short-circuit in 0.3-0.6s (no hang, no silent-empty-success); a raw `curl` with a persistent cookie jar across the full redirect chain lands on a real "Account Sign In" page with a `type="password"` field — ruling out the one plausible alternative, the SAME cookieless-redirect-loop artifact `docs/completeness_document.md` (2026-08-02) flagged for Buncombe ("do not classify as login-walled based on the redirect" without a cookie jar). The identical cookie-jar treatment on Polk (same vendor app) instead lands cleanly on the real "Guest User" search menu — so Polk is open and Rutherford is specifically, substantively walled, not a parsing artifact. Checked for an alternate free path and found none: Rutherford's own county page (`rutherfordcountync.gov/departments/register_of_deeds/index.php`) links to this exact same walled URL as its only "Register of Deeds Index Search" link; Cott's newer RecordRoom product (`recordroom.cottsystems.com`, free guest index, already used for Union SC via `rod/cott_recordroom.py`) has no Rutherford tenant at all — every guessed slug (`rutherfordnc`, `ncrutherford`, etc.) redirects to the generic `Subscription/Expired` page, the identical pattern a deliberately-fake slug produces, proving no real tenant exists there, not just a walled one. Per CLAUDE.md: not defeated, no credentials held, manual-lane candidate only.
    **Gaston — NOT a wall, NOT transient, NOT a timeout-value problem. A real, fixable bug: the adapter targets a dead host that stopped being Gaston's ROD in 2026-05-28. FIXED.** `rod/aumentum.py`'s `AUMENTUM_COUNTIES[("NC","Gaston")]` pointed at `deeds.gastongov.com`. Live-tested today 2026-10-03, repeatedly: plain `curl`, `curl -k`, `curl --http1.1`, and this module's own `curl_cffi` `impersonate="chrome"` (x3 trials) all TCP-connect on :443 then hang through the TLS handshake with zero bytes back, across every timeout tried (20s/25s/30s/45s) — an indefinite hang, not a slow-but-completing response, so a longer `timeout_s` would not have helped (ruling out "timeout value too short"). Ruled out a local/sandbox network problem in the same session: `google.com` (0.2s), `gastongov.com`'s own apex domain (1.4s), and sibling Aumentum tenants Buncombe (0.4s) and Mecklenburg (0.4s, expected 403 without impersonation) all answered normally. Confirmed the actual production code path degrades gracefully today (catches the exception, logs `aumentum.date_sweep_failed`, returns `[]` in ~30s) rather than crashing — but still a 30s tax every run for zero chance of a result. Gaston's OWN county page (`gastongov.com/730/Register-of-Deeds`) links a "Records Search" to `gastonnc.courthousecomputersystems.com` (CCHS) today, confirming independently-documented history (`docs/completeness_document.md`/`docs/completeness_deeds.md`, both 2026-08-02; `rod/doc_images.py`'s own `("NC","Gaston"): ("unreachable", ...)` entry; `docs/deed_index_scoping_2026-09-20.md`): Gaston migrated its ROD off Aumentum to CCHS on 2026-05-28 and the Aumentum mapping was simply never removed. **Fix**: removed the dead `("NC","Gaston")` entry from `AUMENTUM_COUNTIES` — a single-source-of-truth dict every consumer (`nod_discovery.ROD_TARGETS`, `nc_rod_substitute_trustee.SOURCES`, `cash_buyer_deeds.VENDOR_DISPATCH`, `rod/enrich.py`'s `VENDORS`, `enrichment_marriage_license.py`'s `_vendor_covered_counties`) already derives its Gaston coverage from dynamically, by this codebase's own existing design — so one dict edit makes all five consumers fail fast (`[]`, no network call) instead of hanging 30s. Also removed the now-redundant explicit Gaston tuples from `nod_discovery.py`'s `ROD_TARGETS`, `nc_rod_substitute_trustee.py`'s `SOURCES`, and `cash_buyer_deeds.py`'s `VENDOR_DISPATCH` (belt-and-suspenders — the dict change alone was sufficient, but the task explicitly listed each as "wired through aumentum" and leaving dead tuples around invites someone re-adding the dead URL beside them), updated both files' docstring county lists/counts, and updated `cash_buyer_deeds`'s scraper `name` string ("11 counties" -> "10"). **Not fixed, deliberately scoped out**: a real Gaston NOD-via-date-sweep replacement against the actual live host (`gastonnc.courthousecomputersystems.com`, a DevExpress "LRSearch" MVC app — a different protocol from both this module and the classic-ASP `rod/cchs.py` app Burke/Lincoln/Cleveland use) is new work, not a re-point; `enrichment_gaston_rod.py` already covers that host but only for a per-owner-name lien-existence lookup, not a date-range sweep, so it cannot be dropped in as-is. Flagged in all four touched files' docstrings/comments rather than left as a silent gap.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_nc_rod.py` — updated `test_sources_cover_in_scope_nc_counties` (Gaston removed from the expected set) + new `test_gaston_deliberately_excluded_dead_aumentum_host` (regression guard: fails loudly if Gaston is re-added to `SOURCES` without a live-reverified `AUMENTUM_COUNTIES` entry). `tests/test_aumentum_rod.py` — new `test_gaston_not_in_aumentum_counties` + `test_gaston_calls_return_empty_without_any_network_io` (monkeypatches `AsyncSession` to raise if ANY of `search_by_name`/`discover_recent_nods`/`discover_recent_sold_recordings`/`_date_swept_docs` touch the network for Gaston — proves the fix is "stop trying," not "catch the exception after waiting"). 59. **RECONNAISSANCE DONE, SCRAPER BUILT BUT SHIPS GATED OFF (2026-10-03) — commit `6e00d55b`'s "Asheville AccelaServicesView" was scoped as a real, live, buildable code-enforcement lead source (2,738 rows, ~1,000 estimated after category filtering) but deliberately left unbuilt pending its own category reconnaissance. Did that reconnaissance. The premise was wrong: the entire table is a dead 2016-2018 snapshot.**
    **Live-verified 2026-10-03** via direct `outStatistics` MIN/MAX queries against the real service (`gis.ashevillenc.gov/.../AccelaServicesView/MapServer/0`): every date field on every row — `date_opened`, `date_statused`, `date_closed`, `record_status_date`, `date_assigned` — maxes out at 2018-12-14, checked both overall (2,738 rows) and per `record_type_category` (all 9 categories, no exceptions). Sampled real `description` text across every category confirms the same thing case-by-case (every pulled sample dated 2016, status already `Closed`). Statuses that read as currently-open (`Open` 68, `NOV Mailed` 41, `NOV Served` 50, `Citation Pending` 32, `Unsafe Structure` 5, `Deteriorated Structure` 13, `Monitor` 13, etc. — 274 rows total) all have their OWN `record_status_date` frozen at the same ~2018 ceiling, so there is no way to tell whether any of them are still open today. The Accela-to-ArcGIS sync behind this view appears to have simply stopped running after 2018-12-14 and nothing replaced it — consistent with `city_websites/asheville_min_housing.py`'s independent 2026-09-15 finding that Asheville's current minimum-housing process publishes no public case registry at all.
    **Category taxonomy, live-verified** (NOT Henderson's or Gastonia's vocabulary — Accela's own `record_type_category` field, cross-checked against real per-case `description` text): **severe/vacancy-adjacent** — Housing Code Referral (69: "house doe not have power or water service", occupancy-without-utilities complaints), Junked Vehicles (127: textbook junk-vehicle blight), Damage - Incident (72: tree-through-roof, apartment fires, "all utilities disconnected" — structures made uninhabitable by a casualty event). **Not severe** — Short Term Rental (723, zoning/unpermitted-homestay complaints), Other Referral (248, mostly landscaping/zoning trivia — one real exception sampled, "Empty residence unsecured, occupied by vagrants," but one hit in a 95%+-noise category, same shape as Henderson's excluded "General"/Gastonia's excluded "Vegetation/Weeds"), Sign Violation (144), Stop Work Order (86: active *unpermitted construction*, the opposite signal from vacancy), Land Use (79, zoning/home-occupation paperwork), FMO Referral (32: despite the name, every sampled case is routine fire-marshal permit coordination, not a violation). The 268-row severe total (69+127+72) matches `docs/enumeration_r2/r2_municipal_NC_West.md`'s independent 2026 count of "268 addressed distress cases" almost exactly — the category counts are real and stable; only the dates underneath them are dead.

Identifying data is `address` (single free-text string), `parcel_number`/`apn` (Buncombe PIN, identical values, present on 92.3% of the 91 open severe-category rows live-sampled today). `record_comments` (a 50,000-char internal case-log field) deliberately NOT requested, matching this repo's existing PII discipline for similarly-wide case-tracker fields.

Today it reports `max_date_opened=2018-12-14 age_days=2850` and returns `[]` without ever issuing the feature query — a real, live-tested, self-healing gate, not a hardcoded disable someone has to remember to revisit. Registered via the normal `_registry.discover()` auto-discovery (confirmed: 239 scrapers discovered, including this one) and added to `main.DATELESS_OK_SOURCES` (a code case has no sale date) so a future revival is not silently dropped by `_active_only()` the first time it actually emits a row.

Removed the entry; `enrich_with_code_enforcement()` now only matches against Charlotte, which is still live.

These numbers describe what the dead table WOULD produce if trusted, not a current lead count; they are reported for completeness, not as live board rows.

Checked 35 more counties live (beyond the 11 already checked that day: Mecklenburg, Spartanburg, Greenville, Richland, Guilford, Durham, York, Henderson, Burke, Transylvania, New Hanover). 32 genuine negatives, 2 already-wired-elsewhere, 1 real net-new find: Greenwood SC.**

Counties checked: NC Rutherford, Cleveland, Polk (both of its two differently-named but identical-schema services), Gaston, McDowell, Lincoln, Madison, Mitchell, Carteret, Onslow, Brunswick, Pender; SC Laurens, Pickens, Colleton, Beaufort, Georgetown, Anderson, Oconee, Union, Horry (plus all its other 38 non-parcel MapServer layers, in case a building-level layer existed — `Building Footprints` turned out to be geometry-source metadata only), Aiken, Barnwell, Berkeley, Calhoun, Chester, Darlington, Florence, Greenwood, Hampton, Lancaster, Lexington, Saluda, Sumter (35 counties; Charleston's `GIS_VIEWER/New_Parcel_Search` service returned HTTP 500 "not started" on every attempt today — genuinely inconclusive, not a confirmed negative). Also directly confirmed, live, that the NC OneMap statewide fallback behind ~90 NC counties with no dedicated layer (`NC1Map_Parcels/FeatureServer/1`, 70 fields) carries no condition field at all — its only structure-adjacent column, `struct`, is a bare Y/N "has a structure" flag (verified on a 500-row Wake sample, 500/500 `'Y'`).

Carteret's `Condition`/`GradeAndCDU` (Average/Good/Fair/Poor/Very Poor/Unsound/Excellent/Very Good — real, 854 of 64,295 parcels distressed, live-verified) looked like a fresh find but was already wired, just through an older sibling module than today's `condemned` bridge: `enrichment_cama_condition.py`'s `CAMA_SOURCES`, built 2026-07-02 — the established home for "assessor CONDITION rating" as a signal, already covering Carteret + Buncombe + Onslow + York + Spartanburg + Gaston (year-only). That module's own docstring claim "No SC county in our footprint exposes a bulk ArcGIS Condition column" was already stale before this session (Spartanburg contradicted it since 2026-08-19) — fixed the comment to name both SC exceptions.
    **The one real net-new find: Greenwood SC's CAMA layer** (`greenwoodsc.gov/.../Operational_Layers/CAMA/MapServer/9` — the SAME endpoint `parcel_cache.PARCEL_LAYERS["Greenwood"]` already queries for owner/mailing) carries `Condition`/`ConditionText` + `Quality`/`QualityText`. Distinct `ConditionText` values live-verified: Excellent, Very Good/Excellent, Good/Very Good, Average/Good, Average, Badly Worn/Average, Badly Worn, Worn Out/Badly Worn, Worn Out (22,441 non-null of 39,547 total parcels). Sampled real rows, not just the field name, to rule out a Greenville-style degenerate field: "117 MADDOX RD" (LAUNCH PAD MOBILE LLC, tax value $5,000, Badly Worn), "16 EDGEWOOD DR" (BUTLER E BRYAN, built 1948, $17,300, Badly Worn) — genuine low-value LLC/individual-owned structures, the motivated-seller profile this engine targets. **719 of 39,547 parcels (1.8%) carry a pure-distressed rating** (Badly Worn 681 + Worn Out 28 + Worn Out/Badly Worn 10), confirmed by a direct `returnCountOnly` query, not a sample extrapolation. The blended label `Badly Worn/Average` (600 more) was deliberately excluded — a genuine middle value on this scale, not a severe one, and including it would repeat the exact "field LOOKED right but was degenerate/ambiguous" mistake this recon was told to avoid.
    **Wired as a lead source, not an enrichment join — deliberately, after checking why**: a PIN-join enrichment (Carteret's pattern) would have had zero effect, because a live board read (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, read-only) found only 6 Greenwood SC leads on the whole board today and none carry a `parcel_id` to join against. Instead added `greenwood_cama_condemned` to `counties_generic/arcgis_distress_layers.py`'s `LAYERS` — the existing config-driven framework built exactly for "a layer whose rows ARE the distress signal" — `where="ConditionText IN ('Worn Out','Badly Worn','Worn Out/Badly Worn')"`, each row becoming its own `Listing` with real owner/situs/mailing/tax value, independent of any pre-existing board row. Generalized `Layer.condemned` (new field, default `False`) to stamp `raw["condemned"]=True` — Spartanburg's own bare-boolean shape, not a fabricated `code_enforcement` case record, since this is an appraiser rating, not a tracked case — replacing the 2026-10-02 hardcoded `lay.slug == "rockhill_code_demolition"` check with an explicit per-layer opt-in (Rock Hill's layer gets `condemned=True` too; `new_hanover_demolition_permits`, which shares Rock Hill's `process="demolition_permit"` string but is a homeowner's own voluntary teardown application, correctly still gets nothing). **Live-verified end to end**: ran the actual `_fetch_layer()` production code path against the real endpoint — 719 Listings returned, every one carrying `raw["condemned"] is True` and zero carrying a fabricated `code_enforcement` block, counts matching the direct query exactly (681/28/10).

61. **BUILT (2026-10-04) — the human-assisted, per-lead NC eCourts / NC SOS verification lane `docs/validation_2026-10-02/VERIFICATION_PIPELINE_SPEC.md` section 4 proposed as "option 2" for the one real gap that spec's whole `raw.verification` architecture admits no automated verifier closes: NC eCourts Smart Search (AWS-WAF image-grid CAPTCHA) and NC Secretary of State business search (a separate bot-check). Owner explicitly approved building this, with one condition: document clearly where it lives — this entry, plus the module's own docstring and a one-line pointer added to the spec itself, are that.**
    **Lives in**: `src/foreclosure_scraper/verification_human_lane.py` (core logic: `find_lead`, `build_check_specs`, `open_for_human`, `make_verification_record`, `pending_wall_record`, `parse_saved_ecourts_page`, `parse_saved_sos_page`, `check_new_names_liveness`, `verify_ecourts_from_saved_page`, `build_patch_preview`) + `scripts/verify_lead_human_assisted.py` (the operator CLI). Scoped to the two highest-priority types in the spec's own priority table: `probate`/`heir_estate` (the "Boone St" 9th-heir pattern) and `lis_pendens`/`divorce` (the underlying NC eCourts case lookup) — refuses (with an explanatory reason, not a crash) for any other `listing_type` or any row whose `state` is not `NC`.
    **How it runs, in practice — two invocations, a human in between**: `uv run python scripts/verify_lead_human_assisted.py --parcel-id <id> --county <county>` (or `--source-url`/`--case-number`/`--street-address`) finds the one board row via `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` (read-only, never `load_board()` on this Mac), works out exactly which NC eCourts Smart Search category + search name/case-number to use (and, only when the primary party reads as a business via `name_normalize.is_entity()`, an NC SOS business-search lookup too), prints those criteria to the terminal, and opens the real portal URL in the operator's own default browser (`webbrowser.open` — a bare navigation, nothing typed/clicked/submitted). It then writes a `verdict: "wall"` placeholder record and stops. **The human runs the search, solves the CAPTCHA/bot-check themselves, and saves the result page** (Ctrl+S / "Webpage, HTML only") — the identical compliant pattern `docs/MASTER_GAPS_WALLS_AND_MANUAL_LANES.md` section 5 already documents for every other walled source in this codebase, and the same one `scripts/ingest_saved.sh` already automates ingestion for. Re-running the SAME command with `--saved-ecourts-page <file>` (and `--saved-sos-page <file>`, if an SOS check was also queued) parses that saved page and emits the real `confirmed`/`refuted`/`unconfirmed` verdict.
    **Reused, not rebuilt**, per the spec's own instruction: NC eCourts page parsing is `scripts/parse_nc_ecourts_export.parse_nc_ecourts_html` verbatim (the same parser `scripts/ingest_saved.sh` already runs against every other saved eCourts page — lazy-imported via the identical `sys.path.insert(0, str(_REPO / "scripts"))` pattern `crewai_tools.py` already uses for the same module). NC SOS profile parsing is `enrichment_sos_agent._parse_profile` verbatim, pointed at a saved page's visible text instead of a live Scrapling-stealth render. Decedent/heir liveness on any NAME the saved eCourts page reveals that the board's slim `owner_name`/`defendant`/`plaintiff` fields did not already carry — the actual "is this 9th heir still alive" check — reuses `enrichment_nc_voter_lookup.nc_voter_lookup`, the exact free, no-CAPTCHA call `docs/validation_2026-10-03/scripts/validate_probate_heir_buncombe.py`'s `heir_voter_check()` already proved live on 2026-10-03. That voter lookup is real network I/O and runs automatically once a human-cleared eCourts page hands it a name — it is not the walled resource, so automating it is fine and is exactly what closes the loop on the Boone St pattern end to end.
    **`raw.verification` schema followed exactly**: `{"signal", "checked_at", "verdict" in confirmed|refuted|stale|unconfirmed|wall, "evidence", "source", "verifier_version": "v1"}`. Verdict is deliberately conservative where the underlying data does not support more: `parse_nc_ecourts_html`'s own output schema carries no case-STATUS field, so a found case is `"confirmed"` (a real, human-cleared record was found and parsed) rather than guessing "dismissed"/"satisfied" from data that parser does not expose — the exact kind of overclaim CLAUDE.md's own "silent success" warning calls out. `"refuted"` is only emitted for NC SOS, where `_parse_profile`'s `status` field is real, well-defined data (`Dissolved`/`Administratively Dissolved`/`Revoked`/`Withdrawn`). `"stale"` is deliberately never produced here — that is a later freshness pass over `checked_at`, not a point-in-time human check's job.
    **Deliberately NOT wired to the board**: `build_patch_preview(row, record)` shows exactly what a follow-up write step would hand `web_artifact.patch_existing_rows()` (a `dedupe_key()`-able identity plus the merged `raw.verification` list), but neither the module nor the CLI calls it. Left out of this pass on purpose, per the task's own conservative instruction: this is an 8 GB Air already pinned against `BOARD_LOAD_MAX_SOURCE_MB`/`BOARD_PATCH_MAX_SOURCE_MB` by other work, and a real write path deserves its own review (dedupe_key() collision risk against a row that moved since `find_lead` read it; merge-vs-replace semantics on an existing multi-signal `raw.verification` list) rather than riding in on a first pass that was told to stay conservative. Wiring that write path — almost certainly a thin new script that re-reads the named row, recomputes its `dedupe_key()`, and calls `patch_existing_rows()` with `build_patch_preview`'s own output — is the clearly-flagged follow-up.
    **The hard rule, confirmed by reading the actual code, not just the docstring**: nothing in either file ever fills in, submits, or reads a CAPTCHA/bot-check challenge. `open_for_human` is a one-line `webbrowser.open(url)`. The only two network calls either file makes on its own initiative are the already-compliant, non-walled ones this codebase already runs elsewhere: `board_stream.iter_board_rows()` (reading the published board) and `enrichment_nc_voter_lookup.nc_voter_lookup` (the free NC voter-file check). Confirmed live end-to-end against the real board (2026-10-04, `--no-open`): queuing a real Buncombe estate lead produces a correct `verdict: "wall"` record; feeding it a synthetic saved-page fixture produces a `verdict: "confirmed"` record with a real `newly_discovered_names` entry and a real (live) NC-voter-lookup liveness result for it.
    **Tests**: `tests/test_verification_human_lane.py` (new, 39 cases) — `classify_signal` over every in-scope/out-of-scope `listing_type`; the `_match_row` predicate (parcel-id punctuation normalization, case-number case-insensitivity, multi-identifier AND, street+zip); `find_lead` with `board_stream.iter_board_rows` monkeypatched (stops at the first match, returns `None` on no match, raises `ValueError` with zero identifiers given) — deliberately NOT re-testing the board-file-format mechanics `board_stream`/`board_parts` already cover elsewhere; `build_check_specs` (probate with/without a case number, lis_pendens preferring `defendant`, divorce's category, an entity defendant also building an SOS spec, and all three scope-refusal paths: non-NC state, out-of-scope `listing_type`, missing county); `make_verification_record`'s schema and bad-verdict guard; `pending_wall_record` for both spec types; `parse_saved_ecourts_page` against a synthetic Smart-Search-grid fixture built to `parse_nc_ecourts_export.py`'s own documented `tr.k-master-row`/`.party-case-*` class contract (finds the case, detects a newly-discovered name, narrows to the right case when two are on the page, and returns `"unconfirmed"` — not `"refuted"` — on a genuine no-results page); `parse_saved_sos_page` (active/dissolved/empty, reusing `enrichment_sos_agent._parse_profile`'s real line format); `check_new_names_liveness`/`verify_ecourts_from_saved_page` with `nc_voter_lookup` monkeypatched out (never a live call in the test suite) proving the liveness check runs for probate/heir_estate and is skipped for lis_pendens/divorce; `build_patch_preview`'s merge-by-signal behavior. All 39 pass. Targeted sweep (`-k "nc_ecourts or sos_agent or nc_sos or voter_lookup or verification_human_lane or name_normalize"`, 166 tests across every module this one touches or imports from): 166 passed, 0 failed. Full-suite run: 8,217 passed, 65 skipped, 6 failed, 651s — all 6 failures are in files this change never touches (`test_hud_homestore.py`, `test_pipeline_fixes.py`, `test_scraper_revival_2026_09_21.py` x3, `test_source_docs_current.py`) and this change modifies zero existing source files (confirmed: `git status` shows only new files plus this doc edit), so they are pre-existing, not a regression introduced here.
    **Compliance**: code-only, no board write on this Mac. Every check above was either read-only (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`) or the already-compliant NC voter lookup; the one real NC eCourts/SOS exercise was a synthetic local fixture, not a live portal hit — this pass never actually drove a human through a real CAPTCHA, by design (that is the part only a real operator, on a real lead, can do).

62. **PIPELINE CHANGE (2026-10-05): NC SOS registered-agent lookups now run as a Mac -> VM hand-off. The Mac no longer writes the board.**
    **Why.** The daily SOS lookup (launchd `com.highway.foreclosure.sosagent`, 14:00 ET, `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.sh` -> `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py`) worked: 17 to 27 entities resolved with a contact every day. Then the save failed every day with `BoardLoadTooLarge` (`patch_existing_rows` refuses the 2,652 MB board over its 2,300 MB ceiling). Nothing cached the answers, so about 20 real LLC contacts a day were thrown away from late September on, and the board stayed at 344 rows with `sos_agent`. The Oracle VM is the only board writer now, so the lookups were split the same way as the stealth scrapers (`national.stealth_handoff`).
    **Mac side (lookups, no board write).** `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py` streams the board read-only once (`board_stream.iter_board_rows()`, about 22 s and 300 MB), picks targets with the same selection as before (NC rows with an entity name and no `sos_agent`, HOT/WARM first via `enrichment_sos_agent._prio()`), skips anything the ledger already answers, looks the rest up through the unchanged `enrichment_sos_agent._batch_lookup()` stealth session, and merges every answer into the cumulative ledger **`docs/handoff/sos_agent_results.json`**. It commits and pushes ONLY that file (`run_stealth_sources.py`'s pattern: `HANDOFF_PUSH=0` skips git, `pull --rebase --autostash` before the push). The board lock is no longer held for the lookups; the script takes it briefly around the git commit and the rebase only (they rewrite the working tree). A run lock (`logs/.sos_agent_refresh.lock`) stops two SOS runs (two stealth browsers) from overlapping. The ledger is written before any git step, so a failed push loses nothing; the next run pushes it.
    **The ledger.** One JSON object, `entities` keyed by `enrichment_sos_agent.entity_key()` (case, punctuation and spacing do not matter), one entity per line, sorted. Each entry: `entity`, `status` (best answer known: resolved > miss > error), `last_attempt`, `checked_at`, `first_checked_at`, `checks`, `source` (`lookup` or `board_seed`) and, for resolved entries, `profile`, which is exactly what goes into `raw['sos_agent']` (the `_parse_profile()` fields + `profile_url` + `match_count` + the 2026-10-03 `resolved_for_entity`/`resolved_at` stamps, the same shape `enrich_with_sos_agent()` writes). Entries are never dropped and a resolved entry is never downgraded. Recheck: resolved never; an answered miss after `SOS_AGENT_RECHECK_DAYS` (30); a lookup that got no answer after `SOS_AGENT_ERROR_RECHECK_DAYS` (3). The 344 board profiles seeded 154 entries, filed under `resolved_for_entity` or the registry's own `legal_name`, and under the row's current owner only when it is the same entity: item 57 found at least 174 of those 344 profiles no longer belong to the row's current owner, and filing them under the new owner would have spread one LLC's agent across another owner's rows. Format and rules: `src/foreclosure_scraper/sos_agent_handoff.py`'s docstring.
    **VM side (apply, no network).** `main.py` calls `sos_agent_handoff.apply_sos_agent_handoff(enriched)` right before the (still gated-off) live `with_sos_agent` phase, for every `FORECLOSURE_ROLE` (`vm`, `all`). It attaches the ledger's profile to every NC row owned by a resolved entity that has no `raw['sos_agent']` yet, through `enrichment_sos_agent.propagate_profiles()`, the module's own matching and propagation (`enrich_with_sos_agent()` now uses the same helper). A missing, unreadable or broken file is logged (`sos_agent_handoff.absent` / `.unreadable` / `.failed`) and the run carries on. A file older than `SOS_AGENT_HANDOFF_STALE_HOURS` (72) logs `sos_agent_handoff.stale` and is still applied, because registered agents do not go stale in days. `SOS_AGENT_HANDOFF_APPLY=0` turns the step off. Live lookups stay off on the VM (`SOS_AGENT` unset). `sos_agent` is `"*"` in `web_artifact.RAW_KEEP` (`tests/test_raw_keep_covers_enrichers.py` passes).
    **Volume, pacing and back-off (Mac).** Default daily cap 150 (`SOS_AGENT_MAX_CHECK`), with a random 4 to 6 s pause between lookups (`SOS_AGENT_PAUSE_MIN_S` / `_MAX_S`), so it is about 7 to 10 s a name and 150 names fit in about 25 minutes. Budgets: `SOS_AGENT_MAX_SECONDS` 2700 (the module's `_MAX_SECONDS`, was 900); the wrapper's hard kill `SOS_TIMEOUT` 4200 (`SOS_MAX_RUNTIME` is still accepted as the old name; was 2400/3600). The 6-in-a-row breaker (`SOS_AGENT_BREAKER_FAILS`) is kept, but it now counts lookups that got NO answer (timeout, exception, Cloudflare block page, a profile with no SOSID). An answered "no such entity" resets it. Before, six genuine misses in a row stopped the batch: on 10-01 and 10-02 both runs stopped after the same 7 names (1 hit + 6 misses) in under a minute, and with about 55% of names being misses, a 150-name run would usually have stopped early. Adaptive cap, state in `data/sos_agent_backoff.json` (local, never committed): a breaker trip, a failed session, or more than half no-answers halves the next run's cap (floor `SOS_AGENT_MIN_CHECK`, 40); a clean run (no breaker, 10% or fewer no-answers, not cut off by the time budget) adds `SOS_AGENT_CAP_STEP` (25) back toward 150; anything else holds. Every run logs `cap=N (why)` and `next cap=N (verdict: why)`. `SOS_AGENT_CAP=N` (wrapper) or `--cap N` (script) is a one-off small run: it never raises the adaptive cap, but a bad one still lowers it.
    **How to check it is working.** Mac: `tail -30 ~/foreclosure-scraper/logs/sos_agent_refresh.log` (look for `sos_agent: targets=... resolved=...`, `next cap=...`, `git: pushed <sha>`), `grep sosagent logs/job_events.jsonl | tail -3`, `cat data/sos_agent_backoff.json`, and `uv run python scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py --dry-run` (read-only: ledger counts, entities awaiting the VM, the next targets). Repo: `git log --oneline -5 -- docs/handoff/sos_agent_results.json` (one `sos_agent hand-off: +N resolved...` commit a day) and the file's `counts`/`last_run`. VM: `grep sos_agent_handoff logs/vm-run-*.log | tail` (`sos_agent_handoff.applied ... attached=N status=ok`; `stale`/`absent` means the Mac job is not pushing), then the board's count of rows with `raw.sos_agent` should move up from 344 after the VM run that follows a push.
    **Live, 2026-10-05.** Run 1 (`HANDOFF_PUSH=0`, cap 10, through the real wrapper): 10 attempted in 69 s, 3 resolved with contacts (Metpro, PK Ventures I LP, Covenant Presbyterian Church), 7 answered misses, 0 no-answers, breaker not tripped; ledger 164 entities (157 resolved: 154 seeded + 3). VM-apply proof on the Mac with no board write: 48 real rows streamed into `Listing` objects, `apply_sos_agent_handoff()` against the real file attached the exact ledger profile to 33/33 expected rows across 12 entities (spelling variants included), and left already-profiled, SC and ledger-miss rows untouched. Run 2 (`SOS_AGENT_CAP=10`, pushed, through the real wrapper on committed code `ee44ec25`): skipped run 1's 7 misses (`not due=7`), 10 attempted in 72 s, 4 resolved with contacts, 6 misses, 0 no-answers; ledger 174 entities (161 resolved, 13 miss); hand-off commit `4dec7f01` (only `docs/handoff/sos_agent_results.json`) pushed to origin; job event `ok`. Re-running the VM-apply proof against that pushed file: 41/41 expected rows filled across 16 entities, controls untouched. The 14:00 scheduled run that day ran the OLD code before this change and failed as before (17 resolved, lost); the first scheduled run on this code is 2026-10-06 14:00 at cap 150 (no back-off state yet, so it starts at the max).
    **Result selection (fixed later on 2026-10-05; it used to take the FIRST search hit).** sosnc.gov's business search is "Starting With", so a generic name returns every entity whose name begins with it, and `_one()` opened `links[0]`: "COVENANT PRESBYTERIAN CHURCH" (Buncombe) got "Covenant Presbyterian Church of the Associate Reformed Presbyterian Church" (Jacksonville), the first of 7. Live captures the same day show it was not the only case: "DB Homes" lists "DB Homes, Inc." (Dissolved) first and the board's "DB HOMES LLC" (Current - Active, SOSID 1618927) second; "Mill Creek Properties" lists the Inc. before the L.L.C.; "Asheville West, LLC" is registered twice and the Dissolved twin (0647041) is listed first, ahead of the active one (1684466). **What the result list holds** (real markup, `tests/fixtures/sosnc_search_*.html`): a "Records Found: N" line, then one accordion per entity: the heading ("Name • SOSID", "Status • Organization Type") and a hidden box with "Legal name", optional "Prev Legal name", "Sosid", "Date formed", "Status", "Citizenship", "Business type" and the "More information" link to `Business_Registration_profile/<id>`. No address, city or county anywhere in the list. **The matching rule** (`enrichment_sos_agent.select_result()`, reading the page `_one()` already has; the fetch, pacing, breaker and back-off are unchanged): (1) the result's Legal name must equal the searched name after `sos_core_key()`: `name_normalize.normalize_name()` (case, punctuation, apostrophes and backticks), "&" = AND, dotted or spaced acronyms joined (L.L.C., L L C), a leading/trailing THE and trailing designators dropped (LLC, PLLC, LP, LLP, INC, INCORPORATED, CORP, CORPORATION, CO, COMPANY, LTD, LIMITED, PA, PC, "LIMITED LIABILITY COMPANY", "LIMITED PARTNERSHIP", ...), spaces removed. A longer or shorter name never matches, and "Prev Legal name" is not used. (2) When both names carry a designator that fixes the entity type (LLC type, corporation type, partnership type) the types must agree, so "DB HOMES LLC" never takes "DB Homes, Inc."; CO/COMPANY/LTD/LIMITED fix no type, and a name with no designator (most church/association owner fields) matches any type. (3) Several matches: prefer the same designator type as the searched name, then an active status ("Current - Active", "Active/Failure to Pay Fee"). The list has no location field, so there is no county/city tie-break. (4) No match, or a tie left after (3), is **ambiguous**: nothing is opened or attached; the batch outcome lists it under `ambiguous` with the candidate count, the same-name count and the first five candidates, and the ledger records `status: "ambiguous"` (never applied, rechecked after 30 days like a miss). It is an answer, so it resets the breaker streak. The opened profile's own Legal name is checked again before it is accepted (`profile_name_differs` is ambiguous too). A result list with profile links that cannot be parsed is a no-answer (error). Every profile chosen this way carries `match_rule: "exact_name_v1"` and `exact_matches`.
    **Contact parsing (same change).** "Not Listed" and similar placeholders are no contact: dropped from `registered_agent`, officers and addresses, never `best_contact_name`. A commercial registered-agent service stays as `registered_agent` with `agent_is_service: true` (now also caught on normalized words: "U S Corporation Co", "Business Filings Incorporated", "The Corporation Trust Company"); it is never `best_contact_name`, and neither its registered office nor a mailing address that names it becomes `best_contact_address`. `clean_contact()` applies the same rules to stored profiles, conservatively (only a placeholder or service contact is replaced). `raw['sos_agent']` only gained fields (`match_rule`, `exact_matches`); nothing was renamed.
    **Ledger re-check and VM clear.** Every Mac run first re-checks each `resolved` ledger entry (`sos_agent_handoff.recheck_resolved()`, idempotent, again after the merge with the file on disk). `profile_confirmed()` rejects a profile whose Legal name is not the entry's entity under the rule above (`legal_name_differs`), and an older first-hit profile from a search with several candidates that is not active (`inactive_first_hit`: the Asheville West case, where an active same-name entity may have been passed over). A rejected entry becomes `status: "mismatch"`: the profile moves to `rejected_profile`, its SOSID into `rejected_sosids`, `mismatch` records the reason and date; it is never applied, and the next run re-queries it ahead of every other target (rows still carrying the rejected profile count as candidates). Board profiles that fail the same check are never seeded into the ledger again. The VM apply (`apply_sos_agent_handoff`, same signature) now, before attaching, removes `raw['sos_agent']` from rows carrying a profile the ledger rejected for the entity it was attached for (its `resolved_for_entity`, or the row's current entity for a legacy profile; a row whose owner later changed to someone else is left alone, item 57), runs `clean_contact()` on the profiles left in place, and attaches only `profile_confirmed()` profiles, so even a ledger the Mac has not re-checked never applies a rejected one. Counts: `cleared`, `cleaned`, `attached` in `sos_agent_handoff.applied`. The initial clean-up of the ledger as pushed in `4dec7f01` (161 resolved): 22 resolved entries became `mismatch`: 5 `legal_name_differs` (COVENANT PRESBYTERIAN CHURCH -> the Jacksonville church; DB HOMES LLC, MILL CREEK PROPERTIES LLC, MOUNTAIN VIEW ENTERPRISES LLC and SEDGEFIELD PROPERTIES LLC -> the same-name Inc.), 17 `inactive_first_hit` (e.g. Asheville West, LLC; 3B BAKEDGOODS, LLC; Diedrich Services, Inc.; Lentz Investments, LLC; Yorktown Funding, Inc.); 21 of the 22 were board seeds, 1 a lookup. 4 confirmed profiles had their contact cleaned (Crayton Fields HOA and Investment Properties of Asheville: "Not Listed" contact dropped; PK Ventures I LP: "U S Corporation Co" flagged as a service, no longer the contact or its address; Camp Porter Cove: "Business Filings Incorporated" flagged). Ledger 161 resolved -> 139 resolved + 22 mismatch (commit `31a68593`). **Board rows too** (`register_board_profile_mismatch()`, in the Mac's board scan): a legacy board profile was seeded only under the registry's own spelling, so a rejection never reached rows spelled differently. When a row's entity has the profile's core name (or the profile's `resolved_for_entity`) and the profile fails the rule for it, the SOSID is recorded as rejected under the row's entity (`source: "board_check"`): 2 entities, 6 rows ("J AND M FAMILY HOMES LLC" x4 carrying "J & M Family Homes, Inc."; "ROBERSON LAND DEVELOPMENT CO LLC" x2 carrying a dissolved first hit). The 167 board rows whose profile names a different entity than the row's current owner are still left alone (item 57). Tests: `tests/test_sos_agent_matching.py` (55 cases, real captured markup).
    **Live, 2026-10-05 evening (code `31ceee89` + `ed456b3b`).** Run 3 (`SOS_AGENT_CAP=10`, real wrapper): the 10 targets were exactly the 10 mismatches that have board rows, re-queried first. COVENANT PRESBYTERIAN CHURCH -> **ambiguous** (7 candidates, 0 with the same name; nothing opened, never applied). 6 resolved to a different, correct entity than the old first hit: DB HOMES LLC 0665525 (DB Homes, Inc., Dissolved) -> 1618927 DB HOMES LLC (Current-Active); Asheville West, LLC 0647041 (Dissolved twin) -> 1684466 (Current-Active); MILL CREEK PROPERTIES LLC 0095863 (Inc.) -> 1446137 Mill Creek Properties, L.L.C.; MOUNTAIN VIEW ENTERPRISES LLC 0099615 (Inc.) -> 1980657 Mountain View Enterprises LLC; SEDGEFIELD PROPERTIES LLC 0131076 (Inc.) -> 1683706 Sedgefield Properties LLC; DANA HILL CORP 0252024 (Dana-hill Corporation, Admin. Dissolved) -> 0579886 Dana Hill Corporation (Current-Active). 3 re-confirmed the same entity (Bradley Street Cottages, WNC Building, Yorktown Funding). 10 attempted in 73 s, 0 no-answers, breaker not tripped; pushed `5cc12eed`. Run 4 (`SOS_AGENT_CAP=2`, after `ed456b3b`): the 2 board-row mismatches. J AND M FAMILY HOMES LLC -> 2608419 J & M Family Homes, LLC (a different company from the Inc. the rows carried); ROBERSON LAND DEVELOPMENT CO LLC re-confirmed 0512625; pushed `0b4b6363`. Ledger now 179 entities: 153 resolved, 1 ambiguous, 12 mismatch (registry-spelling seeds with no board row to re-query for), 13 miss. **VM-apply proof on the Mac, no board write**: 35,825 NC entity rows streamed into `Listing` objects, `apply_sos_agent_handoff()` against the pushed file: `cleared` 13 (every one immediately re-filled with the right entity's profile), `cleaned` 4, `attached` 53 (40 rows that had none); the Buncombe COVENANT PRESBYTERIAN CHURCH row stays empty; rows with `sos_agent` would go 344 -> 384. The VM applies this on its next run with this code.
    **Files.** `src/foreclosure_scraper/sos_agent_handoff.py` (new: ledger, recheck, seeding, back-off, VM apply), `src/foreclosure_scraper/enrichment_sos_agent.py` (`entity_key`, `_prio`, `stamp_profile`, `propagate_profiles`, pause, breaker bookkeeping, `_MAX_SECONDS`), `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.py` (rewritten), `scripts/sos_agent_refresh.sh`, `src/foreclosure_scraper/main.py` (apply step), `docs/OPERATIONS.md`. **Tests:** `tests/test_sos_agent_handoff.py` (new, 33 cases: ledger merge never loses entries, recheck, seeding, back-off down/up/hold, the VM apply shape + propagation, missing/stale/unreadable/broken hand-off non-fatal, breaker classification against a fake page, the script end to end on a scratch board it must not modify); `tests/test_ops_shell.py` and `tests/test_publish_plumbing.py` updated (the SOS wrapper is no longer a board writer); `tests/test_sos_agent_refresh_patch_integration.py` removed (it tested the board-patch path that no longer exists).
    **Compliance.** The fetch is unchanged (the same Scrapling stealth session that passes sosnc.gov's JS challenge; no CAPTCHA solving anywhere). Only where results go, the volume and the pacing changed. No board write on this Mac; nothing on the Oracle VM was touched.

63. **FIXED (2026-10-05): the 10/5 run's ~380 new duplicate groups were "placeholder twins", not a streaming-merge regression. The merge is fixed for future runs. A reviewed, opt-in clean-up is ready for this run's publish.**
    **Root cause (real pairs; scrapers run live on the Mac and compared with the published board).** Spartanburg's vacant registry and condemned layer and Rutherford's tax roll publish an unnumbered lot as `0 <ROAD>`. Buncombe's lien PDF uses `99999 <ROAD>`. `_to_dict` nulls that sentinel on publish, and later `scripts/fill_address_from_parcel.py` wrote the parcel cache's numbered situs onto the published rows (`raw.situs_address_source = parcel_cache:exact`). On 10/5 the same source scraped the same row again as `0 PATCH DR SPARTANBURG`, with the same parcel and the same `dedupe_key()`. The house-number guard read 0 vs 499 as two houses, so the prior copy `499 PATCH DR SPARTANBURG` aged on as a separate row. Examples: 714252203123, 712249389566 (spartanburg_vacant), 7037-51-0954.56 (condemned), 879362599800000 `99999 PINEY KNOB RD` vs `560 PINEY KNOB RD` (buncombe), 1647690 `0 COBB RD` vs `212 COBB RD` (rutherford). Classification of every prior row of those 4 sources against the live scrape: 332 + 38 + 30 + 32 rows blocked by the guard on a sentinel, and only 4 by two real numbers. **Not the 10/4 rewrite:** replaying the same rows through the pre-rewrite `load_board()+dedupe(fresh+prior)` gives 362 dup groups vs the streaming merge's 361. dedupe()'s pass-1 and pass-3 guards fire the same way. The new input was the backfill.
    **Merge fix (default on, `FULLRUN_PERSIST_PLACEHOLDER_TWINS=0` turns it off).** `src/foreclosure_scraper/placeholder_twins.py` holds the one rule. In `board_persist.merge_prior_board()`, a prior row the guard refused is deferred when it is a placeholder twin of exactly one fresh row (via the parcel branch of `dedupe_key`). It folds after the stream only if the fresh row and all its twins agree on at most one real house number and there are fewer than 4 twins. Otherwise it ages as before. Guards: valid parcel (normalized, at least 7 chars, not one repeated digit, not validation's book/page patterns; digitless is already rejected), a shared source (primary or `also_seen_in`), no unit designator, no resolver-derived parcel (`parcel_from_geo`/`parcel_from_address`), and the fresh scrape listing that parcel key once. The fold is `Listing.merge()` fresh-first, and it keeps the prior's numbered situs over the sentinel. Replay on the real 4-source fixture: dup groups 361 -> 31 (the prior board alone had 35); 345 rows folded; 18 ambiguous rows aged (for example 711295579416 `120 KALMIA ST` / `921 LOGAN ST`).
    **Clean-up of THIS run (option A chosen: in memory at publish).** `scripts/resume_from_checkpoint.py --collapse-dry-run` streams `data/checkpoint/board.json.gz` twice (read-only, no lock, no Listing objects). It prints groups, rows to drop, counts by source, skip reasons, a sample and a plan digest. The apply is OFF unless `RESUME_COLLAPSE_PLACEHOLDER_TWINS=<digest>` (or `1`) is set for `--publish-only`/`--run`. It runs after the pre_publish checkpoint is loaded and before `write_artifact`. Per group, the live row absorbs its aging copies (fold + clear `pulled_sale`/`presumed_withdrawn`) and the copies are dropped. A digest mismatch or a changed row skips the collapse with an error, and the board publishes uncollapsed. It is an env var, not a vm_resume.sh argument, because vm_resume.sh pulls new code mid-script while bash keeps executing the old copy. **Measured:** the planner over a 226,366-row stand-in (whole published board, 4 sources replaced by the as-shipped merge output) finds 328 groups / 338 rows (spartanburg_vacant 248, condemned 36, rutherford 25, buncombe 19). Added peak is about 15 MiB (32.6 traced, minus 17.4 for stream parsing), 0.4 MiB retained. Apply peak is 2.3 MiB for 329 groups, and the in-memory plan takes 1.4 s on about 254K Listings. That is against the publish path's 14.9 GB peak. Option B (`merge_duplicate_rows()` after publish) was rejected: a second full write plus about 3 GB backup plus temp on a VM with about 8-9 GB free; its 2,300 MB ceiling is unmeasured at this size (`BOARD_MERGE_ALLOW_LARGE=1`); slim and detail_shards stay stale; and its fold would carry the aging copy's `presumed_withdrawn` onto the live row.
    **VM procedure (after the enrich-only run finishes, NOT before).** `cd ~/foreclosure-scraper && git pull --rebase --autostash origin main && uv run python scripts/resume_from_checkpoint.py --collapse-dry-run --phase pre_publish --plan-out logs/placeholder_twins_plan.json`. Review the output, then: `RESUME_COLLAPSE_PLACEHOLDER_TWINS=<digest> setsid nohup bash deploy/oracle/vm_resume.sh --publish-only >/dev/null 2>&1 < /dev/null &`. Without the variable the publish is exactly as before.
    **Tests.** `tests/test_placeholder_twins.py` (54 cases, real pairs as fixtures: fold + kept address, the kill switch reproducing the 10/5 duplicate, ambiguous numbers, real different numbers, other source, `also_seen_in`, units, parcel listed twice in fresh, placeholder/short parcels, resolver parcels, too many copies, terminal ambiguous twin; plan vs file-stream digest equality, apply in place, plan mismatch refusal, board_selfcheck's duplicate invariant before/after; resume dry run changes nothing, default off, reviewed digest, stale digest, CLI flag). Scoped suites: 458 passed.
    **Not fixed here (spun off).** `main.run()`'s post-enrich `dedupe()` (dedupe2; it collapsed 35,250 rows on 10/5) merges live rows with their aging prior copies, so the live row inherits `pulled_sale` / `presumed_withdrawn` (25 on the 4-source fixture). Since the rewrite, `merge_prior_board` also returns prior-only rows first, so in dedupe2 the prior copy is the merge base. Fixed in item 64, which also adds an opt-in repair to this procedure.
64. **FIXED (2026-10-05): dedupe2 handed live rows the "presumed withdrawn" tag. Fixed for future runs; an opt-in repair for this publish is part of item 63's dry run and digest.**
    **Mechanism (code, then real rows).** `Listing.merge()` keeps the BASE row's non-empty top-level fields and backfills the empty ones; in `raw` the OTHER row wins leaf collisions and adds every key only it has. Every `dedupe()` pass uses the earlier row as the base (pass 1 bucket, pass 2 `a.merge(b)` with i < j, pass 3 the lowest index). `merge_prior_board()` appends aged prior-only rows to its output while it streams and the fresh rows after, so in dedupe2 the aged row is the base: the result shows the aged row's parcel, address, owner, source, source_url, listing_type and status; `last_seen` is the live row's (max); and `raw['pulled_sale']`, the aged row's `stale_case` (stamped by the previous run's board_quality) and the 'presumed_withdrawn' status (backfilled when the live row had none) come along. The tag survives in either order; the order only decides whose fields win. board_quality then moves a HOT stack to WARM on the tag, and lead_signals caps intent at 69 on the inherited stale_case.
    **Real rows.** Replay of the four item-63 sources (live scrape on the Mac vs the FULL rows of the 223,832-row board the 10/5 VM run merged, through `merge_prior_board()` then `dedupe()`): 23 live+aged merges in dedupe2, all 23 tagged, 0 showing the live row's parcel and address. Examples: BLANTON 'LOOKOUT RD' (072025785900000, live) + HINSON-BAROODY '328 LOOKOUT RD' (072005966600000, aged) became one row showing HINSON-BAROODY, presumed withdrawn; BOZA 'S TURKEY CREEK RD' + BRALEY '84 OLD TURKEY CREEK RD'; '128 GEORGE ST' + '128 GEORGIA ST' (spartanburg_vacant). Live-first order: the same 23 tags.
    **Not "its own old copy".** All 23 joined two DIFFERENT parcels. The first `dedupe()` (fresh rows only) had already fused each shown property's fresh record into a neighbour (pass 2 scores a numberless street, 'LOOKOUT RD', or a sentinel, '0 HUGH ST', against a numbered one at >= 92, and the house-number guard needs two numbers), so merge_prior_board() found no fresh row for the prior copy and aged it, and dedupe2 met the two again. Every shown parcel was in that scrape (23/23). The 9/25 full run's link_validation checkpoint (post-merge, pre-dedupe2, 197,890 rows) through the real `dedupe()` on light rows gives only 3 live+aged merges (all different properties); the 35,250 rows dedupe2 collapsed on 10/5 come from keys that enrichment filled after the merge, which only the VM checkpoint holds.
    **Fix (future runs).** `dedupe.merge_rows()` (src/foreclosure_scraper/dedupe.py:134), used by all three passes (:230, :322, :372): across a live row and an aged one (`is_aged()` = `raw['pulled_sale']`), the live row is the base (`live.merge(aged)`, i.e. merge_prior_board()'s fresh.merge(prior)), then `drop_withdrawn_tags()` (:120) removes pulled_sale, the 'presumed_withdrawn' status and a stale_case the live row did not carry itself. Live+live and aged+aged merges are unchanged, which rows match is unchanged, and nothing new is held in memory. Replay: tagged 23 -> 0, showing the live row 0 -> 23, same row count. Caveat (Listing.merge(), unchanged): inside `raw` the aged copy still wins leaf collisions, as in merge_prior_board.
    **Clean-up of THIS publish (extends item 63: same dry run, digest and env gate).** `--seen-since <run start>` (dry run) and `RESUME_SEEN_SINCE=<run start>` (publish) add a reseen part: a row carrying pulled_sale whose last_seen is at or after the run start absorbed a row created this run (aged rows keep the prior board's last_seen; the newest on the board the 10/5 run merged is 2026-10-02T21:47; the run started 2026-10-05T01:27:29Z). The digest then covers the run start and every repaired row; without the option, plan and digest are item 63's, unchanged. Apply (`placeholder_twins.repair_reseen()`): pop pulled_sale, status 'presumed_withdrawn' -> None, pop stale_case, put a HOT stack back where the down-rank reason is the tag, recompute intent (`enrich_lead_signals`), then re-run board_quality's stale check (`downrank_if_stale`, extracted from step 3) so a past sale date still down-ranks. A repaired placeholder row counts as live for the twin rule. The twin collapse now also keeps the live row's own distress_stack and intent and drops the copy's stale_case (the copy's demoted stack used to win the raw merge). Replay on the as-shipped dedupe2 output written as a checkpoint: the planner picks exactly the 23 rows and nothing else, file stream and Listings give the same digest, apply leaves 0 tagged. Also fixed: item 63's `digest()` raised TypeError when a twin group had a copy with no street address (None against a string in `sorted()`; found by running the planner over the whole published board, so the VM dry run could have crashed). Members now sort by their JSON text, so a digest printed by the old code does not match the new one; none was taken on the VM.
    **What it cannot repair.** The live row's own top-level values (parcel, address, owner, source, listing_type, any field the aged row had filled): the merge kept only the aged row's, the checkpoint holds no other copy, and every enricher after dedupe2 ran on the merged row. In the replay that is all 23 rows; each shows a neighbour that was in the scrape, and the lead the live row was is missing from this board until the next run. Also not judged: a tagged row whose live member was a carryover or source-dated row (last_seen before the run start; counted by the dry run, 1 in the replay), and a row whose shown property really left the source but was fused into a live neighbour would wrongly lose its tag (0 of 23 in the replay). The run summary's tier counts predate the repair (the note says so).
    **Memory.** Planner over the whole published board (223,832 rows, streamed the way the VM dry run streams the checkpoint, tracemalloc): the stream alone peaks at 17.4 MiB; the twins-only plan at 33.6 MiB; with the reseen part at the 10/5 run start also 33.6 MiB (that board has no row seen after it); with a stress cutoff (2026-09-22) that makes 880 tagged rows reseen, 34.9 MiB peak and 1.3 MiB retained, about 1.5 KB per reseen row, so even all 35,250 rows dedupe2 collapsed on 10/5 would add about 52 MiB.
